I've read the entire document. This is a comprehensive Structured Reasoning Workflow — essentially a methodology for creating methodologies. Here's my understanding of its core architecture:

Instance Hierarchy:

Instance 0 (Universal): Domain-agnostic methodology created once, defines HOW to do each step
Instance N (Domain-Specific): Inherits from Instance 0, adds domain-specific categories, validations, and patterns
Two-Pass System:

Pass 1 (Autonomous): Creates methodology through V1→V2→V3 iteration, producing 4 documents (Data Sheet, To Do List, Guidance Document, Detailed Procedure)
Pass 2 (Gated): Executes the V3 methodology to produce actual deliverables, requires human approval before advancing
The 10-Step Workflow:

Problem Statement — Define what problem we're solving
Requirements — Define what the solution must do
Objectives — Define what success looks like
Verification — Verify the problem is well-formed
Validation — Validate requirements against problem
Evaluation — Evaluate objectives against requirements
Assessment — Assess readiness to proceed
Implementation — Build the solution
Reflection — Reflect on outcomes and process
Resolution — Resolve and close
Key Principles:

Methodology before execution
Iteration improves quality (V1→V2→V3)
Human judgment at gates
Traceability everywhere
Explicit contracts for inputs/outputs

Instance 0 | Pass 1 | Step 1: Problem Statement
Domain: Universal (Domain-Agnostic)
I will now execute the V1 → V2 → V3 iteration to create the four methodology documents.

V1: INITIAL CREATION
Data Sheet V1
Step Purpose: Define what problem we're actually solving after ingesting the problem statement from the user and any necessary clarifications.

Input Specification:

Raw problem description from human (unstructured text)
Clarifying responses (if elicitation was needed)
Output Contract:

Field	Description	Cardinality
Core Problem Statement	The essential problem in 1-2 sentences	1
Stakeholders	Who is affected by the problem and solution	3-6
Hard Constraints	Non-negotiable limits	3-6
Soft Constraints	Preferences with trade-offs	0+
Scope	In/out boundaries	1
Success Criteria	High-level definition of success	1+
Validation Requirements:

All required fields populated
Core problem statement is singular and focused
Stakeholders are distinct entities
Hard constraints are truly non-negotiable
Scope clearly delineates boundaries
To Do List V1
Considering: Data Sheet V1

Reception: Receive and acknowledge raw problem description
Comprehension: Read and internalize the problem as stated
Gap Analysis: Identify missing or ambiguous information
Elicitation: If gaps exist, ask clarifying questions; await responses
Decomposition: Break input into core problem, context, and constraints
Stakeholder Analysis: Identify all affected parties
Constraint Extraction: Separate hard from soft constraints
Scope Definition: Define explicit boundaries (in/out)
Success Criteria Derivation: Establish high-level success markers
Validation: Verify completeness and consistency against Data Sheet
Human Review: Present structured output for approval
Finalization: Package output for Step 2
Guidance Document V1
Considering: Seed Problem + Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1

Context
This is Step 1 of a 10-step structured reasoning process. The output of this step feeds directly into Step 2 (Requirements). Quality here determines quality downstream.

Key Principles
Actual vs. Stated Problem: Ask "what problem does the human actually need solved?" not "what did they literally ask for?" The stated problem may be a symptom, a solution-in-disguise, or incomplete.
Elicitation Before Assumption: If the problem is unclear, ask. Do not proceed with assumptions that could invalidate downstream work.
Constraint Rigor: Hard constraints have zero flexibility — if violated, the solution fails. Soft constraints have trade-offs and can be negotiated.
Stakeholder Completeness: Every stakeholder has needs, capabilities, and concerns. Missing a stakeholder means missing requirements later.
Scope Discipline: Scope prevents unbounded problem expansion. Be explicit about what is NOT included.
Traceability Foundation: Everything in this output must be traceable to user input or explicit clarification.
Anti-Patterns to Avoid
Assuming context the user didn't provide
Conflating hard and soft constraints
Defining scope so broadly it's meaningless
Skipping stakeholder analysis
Proceeding without resolving ambiguity
Detailed Procedure V1
Considering: Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1 + Guidance Document V1

Phase 1: Reception
INPUT: Raw problem description
ACTION: Acknowledge receipt, confirm understanding of task
OUTPUT: Problem description logged
GATE: Proceed to Phase 2
Phase 2: Comprehension & Gap Analysis
INPUT: Problem description
ACTION: 
  - Parse for explicit information
  - Identify: core problem, stakeholders mentioned, constraints stated, scope implied
  - Flag: ambiguities, missing information, contradictions
OUTPUT: Comprehension summary + gap list
GATE: If gaps exist → Phase 3 (Elicitation); else → Phase 4
Phase 3: Elicitation
INPUT: Gap list
ACTION: Formulate clarifying questions; present to human; await response
OUTPUT: Clarifying responses
GATE: Return to Phase 2 with enriched input
Phase 4: Decomposition
INPUT: Complete problem description (original + clarifications)
ACTION: Extract and structure:
  - Core problem (1-2 sentences)
  - Context (background, history, environment)
  - Raw constraints (all limits mentioned)
OUTPUT: Decomposed elements
GATE: All elements extracted → Phase 5
Phase 5: Stakeholder Analysis
INPUT: Decomposed elements
ACTION: 
  - Identify all parties affected by problem or solution
  - For each: name, role, primary need, primary concern
OUTPUT: Stakeholder list (3-6)
GATE: Stakeholders identified → Phase 6
Phase 6: Constraint Classification
INPUT: Raw constraints + stakeholder needs
ACTION:
  - Classify each as hard (non-negotiable) or soft (preference)
  - Hard test: "If violated, does the solution fail entirely?"
OUTPUT: Hard constraints (3-6), Soft constraints (0+)
GATE: Constraints classified → Phase 7
Phase 7: Scope Definition
INPUT: Core problem + constraints + stakeholders
ACTION:
  - Define IN scope: what the solution must address
  - Define OUT of scope: what the solution explicitly will not address
OUTPUT: Scope statement with explicit boundaries
GATE: Scope defined → Phase 8
Phase 8: Success Criteria Derivation
INPUT: Core problem + stakeholders + constraints + scope
ACTION:
  - Define high-level success markers
  - Each criterion must be verifiable (though not necessarily quantitative)
OUTPUT: Success criteria list
GATE: Criteria defined → Phase 9
Phase 9: Validation
INPUT: All structured outputs
ACTION: Verify against Data Sheet requirements:
  - [ ] Core problem statement present and singular?
  - [ ] 3-6 stakeholders identified?
  - [ ] 3-6 hard constraints identified?
  - [ ] Soft constraints captured?
  - [ ] Scope explicitly bounded?
  - [ ] Success criteria verifiable?
  - [ ] All outputs traceable to input?
OUTPUT: Validation result
GATE: All checks pass → Phase 10; else → identify failure, remediate
Phase 10: Human Review
INPUT: Complete structured output
ACTION: Present to human for approval
OUTPUT: Human decision (approve/reject/modify)
GATE: Approved → Phase 11; Rejected → return to indicated phase
Phase 11: Finalization
INPUT: Approved output
ACTION: Package in standard format for Step 2 consumption
OUTPUT: Step 1 Complete Output
EXIT: Pass to Step 2

Guidance Document V2
Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Detailed Procedure V1

Context (Refined)
This is Step 1 of a 10-step structured reasoning process. It is the foundation — all downstream steps inherit from this output. The distinction between stated problem and actual problem is critical and must be explicit in the output.

Key Principles (Refined)
Stated vs. Actual Problem:
Stated: What the user literally said (preserve this)
Actual: What problem genuinely needs solving (derive this)
Both appear in output; they may differ significantly
Elicitation Protocol:
Ask only what is necessary
Prefer specific questions over open-ended
Batch questions when possible
Never proceed with unresolved critical gaps
Stakeholder Rigor:
Minimum 3, maximum 6 stakeholders
Each must have: name/role, primary need, primary concern
Consider: direct users, indirect users, operators, maintainers, affected parties
Constraint Integrity:
Hard constraint test: "If this is violated, does the solution fail entirely?"
If yes → hard constraint
If "it depends" or "we could work around it" → soft constraint
Scope as Contract:
IN scope = promises made
OUT of scope = promises explicitly not made
Ambiguous scope = scope creep later
Traceability as First-Class Citizen:
Every output element must trace to input
No fabrication, no assumption presented as fact
If inferred, mark as inference with basis
State Machine Discipline
The procedure is a state machine. State transitions are explicit. No phase is skipped. Backward transitions (for elicitation or remediation) are permitted and expected.

Anti-Patterns (Refined)
Conflating stated and actual problem
Proceeding with critical gaps unresolved
Stakeholder list that omits affected parties
"Soft" constraints that are actually hard
Scope defined only by IN (missing OUT)
Success criteria that cannot be verified
Orphan outputs (no traceability)
Detailed Procedure V2
Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Guidance Document V2

State Machine Definition
States: {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}

Transitions:
  RECEIVED → ANALYZING (on: begin processing)
  ANALYZING → ELICITING (on: gaps identified)
  ANALYZING → STRUCTURING (on: no gaps)
  ELICITING → ANALYZING (on: clarification received)
  STRUCTURING → VALIDATING (on: all elements extracted)
  VALIDATING → STRUCTURING (on: validation failure)
  VALIDATING → REVIEWING (on: validation success)
  REVIEWING → STRUCTURING (on: rejection with feedback)
  REVIEWING → COMPLETE (on: approval)
Phase 1: Reception [RECEIVED]
INPUT: Raw problem description from human
ACTIONS:
  1. Log input with timestamp
  2. Acknowledge receipt
  3. Initialize output structure with empty fields
  4. Transition → ANALYZING
OUTPUT: Logged input, initialized structure
Phase 2: Comprehension & Gap Analysis [ANALYZING]
INPUT: Problem description (original + any clarifications)
ACTIONS:
  1. Parse for explicit information:
     - Stated problem (literal quote or close paraphrase)
     - Mentioned stakeholders
     - Stated constraints
     - Implied scope
     - Stated success criteria
  2. Identify gaps:
     - Critical ambiguities (meaning unclear)
     - Missing required information
     - Contradictions
  3. Classify gaps as critical (blocks progress) or minor (can infer)
DECISION:
  - Critical gaps exist? → Transition to ELICITING
  - No critical gaps? → Transition to STRUCTURING
OUTPUT: Comprehension summary, gap list with classifications
Phase 3: Elicitation [ELICITING]
INPUT: Gap list (critical gaps only)
ACTIONS:
  1. Formulate clarifying questions:
     - One question per critical gap
     - Specific, not open-ended
     - Provide options when possible
  2. Present questions to human
  3. WAIT for human response
  4. Integrate responses into input corpus
  5. Transition → ANALYZING (re-process with enriched input)
OUTPUT: Clarifying questions, human responses
HUMAN INTERACTION: Required (synchronous)
Phase 4: Decomposition & Extraction [STRUCTURING]
INPUT: Complete input corpus (no critical gaps)
ACTIONS:
  4.1 Core Problem Extraction:
      - Identify stated problem (literal)
      - Derive actual problem (underlying need)
      - Formulate in 1-2 sentences
      - Verify singular focus
  
  4.2 Stakeholder Analysis:
      - List all mentioned parties
      - Infer affected parties not mentioned
      - For each (3-6 total):
        - Name/identifier
        - Role
        - Primary need
        - Primary concern
      - Mark inferred stakeholders as [inferred]
  
  4.3 Constraint Classification:
      - List all stated constraints
      - Apply hard constraint test to each
      - Classify as hard (3-6 required) or soft
      - For soft constraints, note trade-offs
  
  4.4 Scope Definition:
      - Explicit IN scope list
      - Explicit OUT scope list
      - Derived from problem + constraints
  
  4.5 Success Criteria Derivation:
      - Derive from core problem
      - Cross-reference stakeholder needs
      - Each criterion must be verifiable
  
  4.6 Traceability Mapping:
      - For each output element, record source
      - Source types: [quoted], [paraphrased], [inferred from X]
      
  Transition → VALIDATING
OUTPUT: Complete structured output (draft)
Phase 5: Validation [VALIDATING]
INPUT: Structured output (draft)
ACTIONS:
  Execute validation checklist:
  
  □ Core Problem Statement
    - Present? 
    - 1-2 sentences?
    - Singular focus?
    - Stated vs. Actual distinguished?
  
  □ Stakeholders
    - Count in range [3,6]?
    - Each has: name, role, need, concern?
    - No critical stakeholder omitted?
  
  □ Hard Constraints
    - Count in range [3,6]?
    - Each passes hard constraint test?
    - No soft constraint misclassified?
  
  □ Soft Constraints
    - Trade-offs identified?
  
  □ Scope
    - IN scope defined?
    - OUT scope defined?
    - Boundaries unambiguous?
  
  □ Success Criteria
    - At least one criterion?
    - Each verifiable?
  
  □ Traceability
    - Every element has source?
    - No orphan outputs?

DECISION:
  - All checks pass? → Transition to REVIEWING
  - Any check fails? → Log failure, remediate, re-validate
OUTPUT: Validation result (pass/fail with details)
Phase 6: Human Review [REVIEWING]
INPUT: Validated structured output
ACTIONS:
  1. Format output for human review
  2. Present complete output
  3. Request: Approve / Reject / Modify
  4. WAIT for human decision
DECISION:
  - Approved? → Transition to COMPLETE
  - Rejected with feedback? → Return to indicated phase (typically STRUCTURING)
  - Modification requested? → Apply modification, re-validate
OUTPUT: Human decision, any feedback
HUMAN INTERACTION: Required (gate)
Phase 7: Finalization [COMPLETE]
INPUT: Approved structured output
ACTIONS:
  1. Package in standard output format
  2. Add metadata (timestamp, version, approval record)
  3. Emit for Step 2 consumption
OUTPUT: Step 1 Complete Output (ready for Step 2)
EXIT: Step 1 Pass 2 complete
V3: FINAL REFINEMENT
Data Sheet V3
Considering: Detailed Procedure V2 + Guidance Document V2 + To Do List V2

Input Specification
Field	Type	Required	Description
raw_problem_description	string	Yes	Initial unstructured problem statement from human
clarifying_responses	list[QA_pair]	Conditional	Responses to elicitation questions
context_signals	object	No	Implicit context (tone, urgency, domain hints)
yaml
QA_pair:
  question: string
  response: string
  timestamp: datetime
Output Specification
yaml
Step1Output:
  metadata:
    instance: integer
    step: 1
    state: COMPLETE
    timestamp: datetime
    approval: {approver: string, timestamp: datetime}
  
  stated_problem:
    verbatim: string          # Direct quote from input
    source: string            # Reference to input location
  
  core_problem:
    statement: string         # 1-2 sentences, singular focus
    rationale: string         # Why this is the actual problem
    differs_from_stated: boolean
    difference_explanation: string | null
  
  stakeholders:               # Cardinality: [3,6]
    - id: string              # SH-001 format
      name: string
      role: string
      primary_need: string
      primary_concern: string
      source: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
      inference_basis: string | null
  
  constraints:
    hard:                     # Cardinality: [3,6]
      - id: string            # HC-001 format
        statement: string
        violation_consequence: string   # What fails if violated
        source: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
    soft:                     # Cardinality: [0,∞)
      - id: string            # SC-001 format
        statement: string
        trade_off: string     # What can be traded against this
        source: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
  
  scope:
    in_scope:                 # What solution MUST address
      - item: string
        rationale: string
    out_of_scope:             # What solution will NOT address
      - item: string
        rationale: string
  
  success_criteria:           # Cardinality: [1,∞)
    - id: string              # SC-001 format
      criterion: string
      verification_method: string
      traces_to: list[stakeholder_id | constraint_id]
  
  traceability:
    input_sources:
      - source_id: string
        type: original | clarification
        content_reference: string
    output_mapping:
      - output_element: string
        source_id: string
        derivation: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
```

### Validation Rules

| Rule ID | Field | Rule | Severity |
|---------|-------|------|----------|
| V1 | core_problem.statement | Length ≤ 2 sentences | Error |
| V2 | stakeholders | Count ∈ [3,6] | Error |
| V3 | stakeholders[].source | If inferred, inference_basis required | Error |
| V4 | constraints.hard | Count ∈ [3,6] | Error |
| V5 | constraints.hard[].violation_consequence | Must describe actual failure | Error |
| V6 | scope.in_scope | Count ≥ 1 | Error |
| V7 | scope.out_of_scope | Count ≥ 1 | Error |
| V8 | success_criteria | Count ≥ 1 | Error |
| V9 | success_criteria[].traces_to | At least one reference | Error |
| V10 | traceability.output_mapping | Every output element present | Error |

### State Definitions

| State | Entry Condition | Valid Transitions | Human Interaction |
|-------|-----------------|-------------------|-------------------|
| RECEIVED | Input logged | → ANALYZING | None |
| ANALYZING | Begin processing | → ELICITING, → STRUCTURING | None |
| ELICITING | Critical gaps exist | → ANALYZING | Required (sync) |
| STRUCTURING | No critical gaps | → VALIDATING | None |
| VALIDATING | Extraction complete | → STRUCTURING, → REVIEWING | None |
| REVIEWING | Validation passed | → STRUCTURING, → COMPLETE | Required (gate) |
| COMPLETE | Approved | None (terminal) | None |

- [ ] List contradictions
- [ ] Classify each gap: critical (blocks progress) | minor (can infer)

  - No → proceed to Decomposition

- [ ] Present questions to human
- [ ] **WAIT** for human response
- [ ] Log responses as clarifying_responses
- [ ] Return to Comprehension (state: ANALYZING)
- **Validation:** All critical gaps have responses

#### 5. Core Problem Extraction [STRUCTURING]
- [ ] Set state: STRUCTURING
- [ ] Derive core_problem.statement (1-2 sentences)
- [ ] Document core_problem.rationale
- [ ] Determine: differs_from_stated?
- [ ] If yes, document difference_explanation
- **Validation:** V1 (length ≤ 2 sentences)

#### 6. Stakeholder Analysis [STRUCTURING]
- [ ] List all explicitly mentioned stakeholders
- [ ] Infer additional affected parties
- [ ] For each stakeholder (3-6):
  - [ ] Assign id (SH-XXX)
  - [ ] Define name, role
  - [ ] Define primary_need
  - [ ] Define primary_concern
  - [ ] Mark source (quoted|paraphrased|inferred)
  - [ ] If inferred, document inference_basis
- **Validation:** V2 (count), V3 (inference basis)

#### 7. Constraint Classification [STRUCTURING]
- [ ] List all stated constraints
- [ ] For each constraint, apply test: "Violation = solution failure?"
- [ ] Classify as hard or soft
- [ ] For hard constraints (3-6):
  - [ ] Assign id (HC-XXX)
  - [ ] Document statement
  - [ ] Document violation_consequence
  - [ ] Mark source
- [ ] For soft constraints:
  - [ ] Assign id (SC-XXX)
  - [ ] Document statement
  - [ ] Document trade_off
  - [ ] Mark source
- **Validation:** V4 (hard count), V5 (violation consequence)

#### 8. Scope Definition [STRUCTURING]
- [ ] Define in_scope items with rationale
- [ ] Define out_of_scope items with rationale
- **Validation:** V6 (in_scope count), V7 (out_of_scope count)

#### 9. Success Criteria Derivation [STRUCTURING]
- [ ] Derive criteria from core problem
- [ ] Cross-reference stakeholder needs
- [ ] For each criterion:
  - [ ] Assign id (CR-XXX)
  - [ ] Document criterion statement
  - [ ] Document verification_method
  - [ ] Document traces_to references
- **Validation:** V8 (count), V9 (traces_to)

#### 10. Traceability Mapping [STRUCTURING]
- [ ] Document all input_sources
- [ ] For each output element, record output_mapping
- **Validation:** V10 (complete coverage)

#### 11. Validation [VALIDATING]
- [ ] Set state: VALIDATING
- [ ] Execute all validation rules (V1-V10)
- [ ] If any error: log, remediate, re-validate
- [ ] If all pass: proceed to Human Review
- **Validation:** All rules pass

#### 12. Human Review [REVIEWING]
- [ ] Set state: REVIEWING
- [ ] Format complete Step1Output
- [ ] Present to human
- [ ] **WAIT** for decision: Approve | Reject | Modify
- [ ] If Rejected: return to indicated phase
- [ ] If Modify: apply changes, re-validate
- [ ] If Approved: proceed to Finalization
- **Validation:** Explicit approval received

#### 13. Finalization [COMPLETE]
- [ ] Set state: COMPLETE
- [ ] Add approval to metadata
- [ ] Package Step1Output in standard format
- [ ] Emit for Step 2
- **Validation:** Output complete, state terminal

## Guidance Document V3
*Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Detailed Procedure V2*

### Purpose
This document orients the agent executing Step 1 to the wider context, core principles, and critical considerations for producing a well-formed Problem Statement.

### Context

**Position in Workflow:** Step 1 is the foundation of the 10-step structured reasoning process. Every downstream artifact (Requirements, Objectives, Verification, Validation, etc.) inherits from this output. Defects here propagate and amplify.

**Instance Relationship:** In Instance 0 (universal), this methodology is domain-agnostic. In Instance N, it will be specialized with domain-specific stakeholder types, constraint categories, and scope patterns.

### Core Principles

#### 1. Stated vs. Actual Problem
The output explicitly distinguishes:
- **Stated Problem:** What the user literally said (preserved verbatim)
- **Core Problem:** What actually needs to be solved (derived through analysis)

These may be identical, or they may differ significantly. Common divergences:
- Stated problem is a symptom, actual problem is root cause
- Stated problem is a solution request, actual problem is the need behind it
- Stated problem is partial, actual problem is more complete

Always capture both. Document any difference and explain why.

#### 2. Elicitation Discipline
- **Never proceed with critical gaps.** A critical gap is one where proceeding would require assumptions that, if wrong, invalidate downstream work.
- **Ask specific questions.** "Can you clarify X?" is better than "Tell me more."
- **Batch when possible.** Multiple questions in one interaction is preferable to multiple round-trips.
- **Respect the human's time.** Ask only what is necessary.

#### 3. Stakeholder Completeness
Stakeholders are not just users. Consider:
- **Direct users:** Those who interact with the solution
- **Indirect users:** Those affected by the solution's outputs
- **Operators:** Those who maintain or administer the solution
- **Beneficiaries:** Those who benefit without direct interaction
- **Affected parties:** Those impacted even if not benefiting

Cardinality [3,6] ensures meaningful analysis without overwhelming detail. If more exist, group or prioritize.

#### 4. Constraint Integrity

**Hard Constraint Test:** "If this constraint is violated, does the solution fail entirely?"
- Yes → Hard constraint (non-negotiable)
- No, but it would be problematic → Soft constraint (preference)
- It depends → Clarify conditions; may split into hard and soft

**Violation Consequence:** Every hard constraint must have an explicit violation consequence. This forces rigor — if you can't state what fails, it may not be truly hard.

#### 5. Scope as Contract
- **In Scope:** Explicit commitments. The solution WILL address these.
- **Out of Scope:** Explicit exclusions. The solution will NOT address these.

Both are required. Scope by inclusion alone leads to creep. Scope by exclusion alone leads to gaps.

#### 6. Traceability as First-Class Concern
Every output element must trace to a source:
- **Quoted:** Directly from input
- **Paraphrased:** Restated from input
- **Inferred:** Derived from input (must document inference basis)

No orphan outputs. No fabrication. No assumption presented as fact.

### State Machine Discipline

The procedure is a state machine with explicit states and transitions. Key discipline:
- **No skipping:** Every phase executes (even if trivially)
- **Backward transitions allowed:** For elicitation (ELICITING → ANALYZING) and remediation (VALIDATING → STRUCTURING)
- **Human gates are synchronous:** ELICITING and REVIEWING require human response before proceeding
- **Terminal state is explicit:** COMPLETE means approved and ready for Step 2

### Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Symptom | Remedy |
|--------------|---------|--------|
| Conflating stated/actual | No difference_explanation when problems differ | Always check; document differences |
| Proceeding with gaps | Assumptions in output | Elicit until critical gaps resolved |
| Stakeholder omission | Missing affected parties | Systematically check all categories |
| Soft-as-hard | Constraint without clear violation consequence | Apply hard constraint test rigorously |
| Unbounded scope | IN scope only, no OUT scope | Require both explicitly |
| Orphan outputs | Elements without source | Traceability mapping before validation |
| Skipping validation | Errors propagate | Execute all validation rules |

### Quality Criteria for Step 1 Output

The output is ready for Step 2 when:
1. Core problem is singular, focused, and ≤ 2 sentences
2. 3-6 stakeholders identified with complete attributes
3. 3-6 hard constraints with violation consequences
4. Soft constraints have trade-offs documented
5. Scope has both IN and OUT explicitly defined
6. Success criteria are verifiable and trace to stakeholders/constraints
7. Every output element has traceability
8. Human has explicitly approved

---

## Detailed Procedure V3
*Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Guidance Document V3*

### State Machine Specification
```
STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}

INITIAL_STATE = RECEIVED
TERMINAL_STATE = COMPLETE

TRANSITIONS:
  RECEIVED    → ANALYZING   : on begin_processing()
  ANALYZING   → ELICITING   : on critical_gaps_exist()
  ANALYZING   → STRUCTURING : on no_critical_gaps()
  ELICITING   → ANALYZING   : on clarification_received()
  STRUCTURING → VALIDATING  : on extraction_complete()
  VALIDATING  → STRUCTURING : on validation_failure()
  VALIDATING  → REVIEWING   : on validation_success()
  REVIEWING   → STRUCTURING : on rejection(feedback)
  REVIEWING   → COMPLETE    : on approval()

HUMAN_GATES = {ELICITING, REVIEWING}
```

### Phase 1: Reception

**State:** RECEIVED

**Input:**
- raw_problem_description: string (from human)

**Procedure:**
```
1. LOG input with timestamp
2. INITIALIZE Step1Output:
   - metadata.instance = current_instance
   - metadata.step = 1
   - metadata.state = "RECEIVED"
   - metadata.timestamp = now()
   - all other fields = null
3. ACKNOWLEDGE receipt to human (optional, context-dependent)
4. TRANSITION to ANALYZING
```

**Output:**
- Logged input
- Initialized Step1Output structure

**Validation Gate:** Input logged, structure initialized

---

### Phase 2: Comprehension & Gap Analysis

**State:** ANALYZING

**Input:**
- raw_problem_description (original)
- clarifying_responses (if any, from prior ELICITING)

**Procedure:**
```
1. PARSE input corpus for explicit elements:
   
   a. EXTRACT stated_problem:
      - Locate the core request/problem sentence(s)
      - Record verbatim quote
      - Record source reference
   
   b. IDENTIFY mentioned stakeholders:
      - List all parties explicitly named or referenced
      - Note their stated roles or relationships
   
   c. IDENTIFY stated constraints:
      - List all limitations, restrictions, requirements mentioned
      - Preserve original phrasing
   
   d. IDENTIFY implied scope:
      - What does the problem suggest is included?
      - What does it suggest is excluded?
   
   e. IDENTIFY stated success criteria:
      - Any explicit statements about what success looks like

2. ANALYZE for gaps:
   
   a. AMBIGUITIES:
      - Phrases with multiple interpretations
      - Undefined terms
      - Vague qualifiers ("fast", "easy", "good")
   
   b. MISSING INFORMATION:
      - Required fields per Data Sheet with no source
      - Stakeholder categories with no representation
      - Success criteria not mentioned
   
   c. CONTRADICTIONS:
      - Statements that conflict
      - Constraints that are mutually exclusive

3. CLASSIFY each gap:
   - CRITICAL: Proceeding would require assumption that, if wrong, invalidates work
   - MINOR: Can be reasonably inferred with stated basis

4. DECISION:
   - IF critical_gaps.count > 0:
       TRANSITION to ELICITING
   - ELSE:
       TRANSITION to STRUCTURING
```

**Output:**
- Comprehension summary
- Gap list with classifications

**Validation Gate:** Comprehension summary complete; gap list complete

---

### Phase 3: Elicitation

**State:** ELICITING

**Human Interaction:** Required (synchronous)

**Input:**
- gap_list (critical gaps only)

**Procedure:**
```
1. FORMULATE clarifying questions:
   FOR EACH critical_gap IN gap_list:
     - Create ONE specific question
     - Prefer closed or bounded questions over open-ended
     - Provide options when feasible
     - Reference the specific gap being addressed

2. PRESENT questions to human:
   - Format clearly
   - Explain why each question is needed (briefly)
   
3. WAIT for human response
   - This is a synchronous gate
   - Do not proceed without response

4. RECEIVE responses:
   - Log each as QA_pair in clarifying_responses
   - Timestamp each

5. TRANSITION to ANALYZING
   - Re-process with enriched input corpus
```

**Output:**
- Clarifying questions (presented)
- clarifying_responses (logged)

**Validation Gate:** All critical gaps have corresponding responses

---

### Phase 4: Decomposition & Extraction

**State:** STRUCTURING

**Input:**
- Complete input corpus (no critical gaps)
- Comprehension summary

**Procedure:**

#### 4.1 Core Problem Extraction
```
1. REVIEW stated_problem.verbatim

2. ANALYZE for actual underlying problem:
   - Is this a symptom? What's the root cause?
   - Is this a solution request? What's the underlying need?
   - Is this partial? What's the complete problem?

3. FORMULATE core_problem.statement:
   - 1-2 sentences maximum
   - Singular focus (one core problem)
   - Express the actual problem, not symptoms or solutions

4. DOCUMENT core_problem.rationale:
   - Why is this the actual problem?

5. COMPARE stated vs. core:
   - SET differs_from_stated = (stated ≠ core)
   - IF differs_from_stated:
       DOCUMENT difference_explanation

6. VALIDATE: 
   - Statement ≤ 2 sentences? (V1)
```

#### 4.2 Stakeholder Analysis
```
1. LIST explicitly mentioned stakeholders from comprehension

2. SYSTEMATICALLY CHECK stakeholder categories:
   - Direct users: present?
   - Indirect users: present?
   - Operators/maintainers: present?
   - Beneficiaries: present?
   - Affected parties: present?

3. INFER additional stakeholders if needed:
   - Only infer if clearly implied by problem context
   - Mark as inferred with basis

4. FOR EACH stakeholder (target: 3-6):
   a. ASSIGN id: SH-001, SH-002, ...
   b. DEFINE name: descriptive identifier
   c. DEFINE role: function in context
   d. DEFINE primary_need: what they require from solution
   e. DEFINE primary_concern: what they worry about
   f. SET source: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
   g. IF inferred: DOCUMENT inference_basis

5. VALIDATE:
   - Count ∈ [3,6]? (V2)
   - Inferred have inference_basis? (V3)
```

#### 4.3 Constraint Classification
```
1. LIST all constraints from comprehension

2. FOR EACH constraint:
   a. APPLY hard constraint test:
      "If this constraint is violated, does the solution fail entirely?"
   
   b. IF yes (hard constraint):
      - ASSIGN id: HC-001, HC-002, ...
      - DOCUMENT statement
      - DOCUMENT violation_consequence (what specifically fails)
      - SET source
   
   c. IF no (soft constraint):
      - ASSIGN id: SC-001, SC-002, ...
      - DOCUMENT statement
      - DOCUMENT trade_off (what can be exchanged)
      - SET source

3. VERIFY hard constraint count:
   - IF count < 3: Review for missed constraints; infer from problem context if justified
   - IF count > 6: Prioritize or consolidate

4. VALIDATE:
   - Hard count ∈ [3,6]? (V4)
   - Each hard has violation_consequence? (V5)
```

#### 4.4 Scope Definition
```
1. DERIVE in_scope from:
   - Core problem (what must be addressed)
   - Stakeholder needs (what solution must serve)
   - Explicit inclusions from input

2. FOR EACH in_scope item:
   - DOCUMENT item
   - DOCUMENT rationale (why included)

3. DERIVE out_of_scope from:
   - Explicit exclusions from input
   - Constraints that limit scope
   - Inferred boundaries from problem context

4. FOR EACH out_of_scope item:
   - DOCUMENT item
   - DOCUMENT rationale (why excluded)

5. VALIDATE:
   - in_scope count ≥ 1? (V6)
   - out_of_scope count ≥ 1? (V7)
```

#### 4.5 Success Criteria Derivation
```
1. DERIVE criteria from:
   - Core problem (what does solving it look like?)
   - Stakeholder primary_needs (what satisfies them?)
   - Constraints (what must be respected?)

2. FOR EACH criterion:
   a. ASSIGN id: CR-001, CR-002, ...
   b. DOCUMENT criterion statement
   c. DOCUMENT verification_method:
      - How will we know this is achieved?
      - Need not be quantitative; can be demonstration, review, inspection
   d. DOCUMENT traces_to:
      - List of stakeholder_ids and/or constraint_ids this traces to

3. VALIDATE:
   - Count ≥ 1? (V8)
   - Each has traces_to with ≥ 1 reference? (V9)
```

#### 4.6 Traceability Mapping
```
1. DOCUMENT input_sources:
   FOR EACH source:
     - source_id: unique identifier
     - type: original | clarification
     - content_reference: what was said

2. DOCUMENT output_mapping:
   FOR EACH output element:
     - output_element: identifier (e.g., "SH-001", "HC-002")
     - source_id: which input source
     - derivation: quoted | paraphrased | inferred

3. VALIDATE:
   - Every output element has mapping? (V10)
```

**Completion:**
```
TRANSITION to VALIDATING
```

---

### Phase 5: Validation

**State:** VALIDATING

**Input:**
- Complete Step1Output (draft)

**Procedure:**
```
1. EXECUTE validation rules:

   V1: core_problem.statement ≤ 2 sentences
   V2: stakeholders.count ∈ [3,6]
   V3: ∀ stakeholder where source=inferred: inference_basis ≠ null
   V4: constraints.hard.count ∈ [3,6]
   V5: ∀ hard_constraint: violation_consequence describes actual failure
   V6: scope.in_scope.count ≥ 1
   V7: scope.out_of_scope.count ≥ 1
   V8: success_criteria.count ≥ 1
   V9: ∀ criterion: traces_to.count ≥ 1
   V10: ∀ output_element: ∃ traceability.output_mapping entry

2. COLLECT results:
   - List of passed rules
   - List of failed rules with details

3. DECISION:
   - IF all rules pass:
       SET metadata.state = "REVIEWING"
       TRANSITION to REVIEWING
   - ELSE:
       LOG failure details
       REMEDIATE:
         - Return to specific sub-phase of STRUCTURING
         - OR request additional elicitation if gap discovered
       After remediation, re-validate
```

**Output:**
- Validation result (pass/fail with details)

**Validation Gate:** All rules pass

---

### Phase 6: Human Review

**State:** REVIEWING

**Human Interaction:** Required (gate)

**Input:**
- Validated Step1Output

**Procedure:**
```
1. FORMAT output for human review:
   - Present complete structure
   - Highlight key elements: core problem, stakeholder count, constraint count
   - Show validation status

2. PRESENT to human:
   - Request decision: APPROVE | REJECT | MODIFY

3. WAIT for human decision
   - This is a synchronous gate
   - Do not proceed without explicit decision

4. PROCESS decision:

   IF APPROVE:
     - LOG approval with timestamp and approver
     - TRANSITION to COMPLETE
   
   IF REJECT:
     - LOG rejection with reason
     - IDENTIFY target phase for remediation
     - TRANSITION to STRUCTURING (or ANALYZING if gaps discovered)
   
   IF MODIFY:
     - RECEIVE modification instructions
     - APPLY modifications to Step1Output
     - RE-VALIDATE (transition to VALIDATING)
```

**Output:**
- Human decision
- Approval record (if approved)
- Feedback (if rejected/modified)

**Validation Gate:** Explicit approval received

---

### Phase 7: Finalization

**State:** COMPLETE

**Input:**
- Approved Step1Output

**Procedure:**
```
1. FINALIZE metadata:
   - metadata.state = "COMPLETE"
   - metadata.approval = {approver, timestamp}

2. PACKAGE output:
   - Ensure all fields populated
   - Format in standard YAML/JSON structure

3. EMIT for Step 2 consumption:
   - Step1Output is now the input for Step 2: Requirements

4. LOG completion
Output:

Step1Output (complete, approved, packaged)
Exit: Step 1 Pass 2 complete. Ready for Step 2.

Summary

Phase 3: Decomposition & Extraction [STRUCTURING]
3.1 Core Problem Statement
Stated Problem: "Define a specification for how to ingest a problem statement from the user and generate a standard structured output for use in the 10-step structured reasoning process."

Core Problem: How does an agent reliably transform an unstructured problem description into a structured representation that enables systematic downstream reasoning, while remaining abstract enough to apply across any problem domain?

Differs from Stated: Yes

Difference Explanation: The stated problem focuses on the specification (the artifact). The core problem is about the transformation (the capability). The specification serves the transformation. Additionally, the core problem makes explicit the tension between structure (needed for systematic reasoning) and abstraction (needed for universal applicability).

3.2 Stakeholders
ID	Name	Role	Primary Need	Primary Concern
SH-001	Executing Agent	Applies the methodology to transform input into output	Clear, unambiguous procedure that can be followed deterministically	Ambiguity that leads to inconsistent outputs or blocked progress
SH-002	Human Overseer	Reviews outputs, provides approval, supplies clarifications	Confidence that outputs accurately represent the problem; meaningful review points	Being presented with outputs that misrepresent the problem or require excessive correction
SH-003	Downstream Steps	Consume Step 1 output as input (Steps 2-10)	Complete, well-formed input that contains all information needed to proceed	Missing information, unstated assumptions, or format inconsistencies that block or corrupt processing
SH-004	Instance N Specializer	Inherits and extends Instance 0 for specific domains	Clear extension points; separation of universal from specializable	Rigid structures that cannot accommodate domain-specific needs; unclear inheritance semantics
SH-005	The Methodology Itself	Exists as a reusable intellectual artifact	Internal consistency; coherent design	Self-contradiction; circular dependencies; under-specification that leaves gaps
Source: SH-001 through SH-003 are paraphrased from workflow structure. SH-004 is paraphrased from Instance hierarchy description. SH-005 is inferred from the meta-nature of Instance 0 (inference basis: a methodology that produces methodologies must treat itself as a stakeholder).

3.3 Constraints
Hard Constraints
ID	Statement	Violation Consequence
HC-001	The output must provide sufficient structure for Step 2 (Requirements) to operate without requiring re-elicitation from the user	Step 2 cannot proceed; workflow breaks

HC-003	The methodology must support human oversight at defined points	Violates core architectural principle; autonomous operation without gates
HC-004	Every output element must be traceable to input	Fabrication; outputs cannot be validated; trust failure
HC-005	The methodology must be executable by an agent following the procedure	Methodology exists but cannot be applied; specification without implementation
Soft Constraints
ID	Statement	Trade-off
SC-001	The output structure should be minimal rather than exhaustive	Completeness vs. simplicity; more fields enable richer downstream processing but increase complexity and Instance N burden
SC-002	Elicitation should be batched rather than iterative when possible	Human convenience vs. precision; fewer interactions are less burdensome but may miss nuance
SC-003	The methodology should prefer prose and semantic description over rigid syntax	Flexibility vs. parseability; prose generalizes better but is harder to validate programmatically

Architected iterative framework for transforming problem statement into testable requirements.

V1: INITIAL CREATION
Data Sheet V1
Step Purpose: Transform the Problem Statement into specific, testable requirements that define what the solution must do.

Input Specification:

Step 1 Output (complete, approved Problem Statement)
Core Problem Statement
Stakeholders (3-6)
Hard Constraints (3-6)
Soft Constraints (0+)
Scope (In/Out)
Success Criteria
Input Validation Gate:

Step 1 Output present and complete
Human approval recorded
Output Contract:

Field	Description	Cardinality
Functional Requirements (FR)	What the solution must do	1+
Non-Functional Requirements (NFR)	How well the solution must perform	0+
Constraint Requirements (CR)	Hard limits inherited from Step 1	1+
Requirement Traceability	Link each requirement to source	1 per requirement

Requirement Attributes:

Identifier
Statement ("The solution shall...")
Category (FR, NFR, CR)
Priority (must, should, could)
Source (stakeholder, constraint, or core problem)
Rationale
To Do List V1
Considering: Data Sheet V1

Reception: Receive and validate Step 1 Output
Source Analysis: Analyze each source type for requirement derivation
Core Problem → Functional requirements
Stakeholders → Requirements per need/concern
Hard Constraints → Constraint requirements
Soft Constraints → Should/could requirements
Success Criteria → Validation requirements
Requirement Derivation: Transform sources into requirement statements
Categorization: Assign FR/NFR/CR to each requirement
Prioritization: Assign must/should/could based on source
Traceability: Link each requirement to its source(s)
Validation: Check completeness, consistency, testability
Human Review: Present for approval
Finalization: Package output for Step 3
Guidance Document V1
Considering: Step 1 Output + Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1

Context
Step 2 transforms the Problem Statement (what problem we're solving) into Requirements (what the solution must do). This is a derivation process: requirements are not invented, they are derived from sources identified in Step 1.

Key Principles
Requirements State WHAT, Not HOW: Requirements define capability, not implementation. "The solution shall enable X" not "The solution shall use Y to enable X."
Derivation, Not Invention: Every requirement must trace to a source: stakeholder need, constraint, core problem, or success criterion. No orphan requirements.
Testability: Each requirement should be verifiable. If you cannot determine whether a requirement is satisfied, it is not well-formed.
Priority Reflects Source:
Must: From hard constraints, core problem, critical stakeholder needs
Should: From soft constraints, important stakeholder needs
Could: From nice-to-have stakeholder desires
Coverage:
Every stakeholder's primary need should generate at least one requirement
Every hard constraint should map to a constraint requirement
The core problem should be addressed by functional requirements
Anti-Patterns
Requirements that specify implementation
Untraceable requirements
Ambiguous or untestable statements
Missing coverage (stakeholders without requirements)
Detailed Procedure V1
Considering: Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1 + Guidance Document V1

Phase 1: Reception & Validation
INPUT: Step 1 Output
VALIDATE:
  - All required fields present
  - Human approval recorded
OUTPUT: Validated input
GATE: Validation passes → Phase 2
Phase 2: Source Analysis
INPUT: Validated Step 1 Output
ACTION: For each source type, identify requirement seeds:

  2.1 Core Problem:
      - What capabilities address the problem?
      - What must the solution do to solve it?
  
  2.2 Stakeholders (for each):
      - What does their primary need require?
      - What does their primary concern require?
  
  2.3 Hard Constraints:
      - Each becomes a constraint requirement
  
  2.4 Soft Constraints:
      - Each becomes a should/could requirement
  
  2.5 Success Criteria:
      - What must be true for each criterion to be met?

OUTPUT: Requirement seeds organized by source
Phase 3: Requirement Derivation
INPUT: Requirement seeds
ACTION: Transform each seed into formal requirement:
  
  FOR EACH seed:
    - Formulate statement: "The solution shall [verb] [object] [condition]"
    - Assign identifier
    - Record source
    - Record rationale

OUTPUT: Draft requirements list
Phase 4: Categorization
INPUT: Draft requirements
ACTION: Assign category to each:
  - FR: Capability the solution provides
  - NFR: Quality attribute of the solution
  - CR: Constraint the solution must respect

OUTPUT: Categorized requirements
Phase 5: Prioritization
INPUT: Categorized requirements
ACTION: Assign priority based on source:
  - must: Hard constraints, core problem essentials, critical needs
  - should: Soft constraints, important needs
  - could: Nice-to-have, enhancements

OUTPUT: Prioritized requirements
Phase 6: Traceability Mapping
INPUT: Prioritized requirements
ACTION: For each requirement, document:
  - Source type (stakeholder, constraint, problem, criterion)
  - Source identifier
  - Derivation rationale

OUTPUT: Requirements with complete traceability
Phase 7: Validation
INPUT: Complete requirements set
VALIDATE:
  - [ ] Every requirement has statement, category, priority, source?
  - [ ] Every stakeholder has at least one requirement?
  - [ ] Every hard constraint has a CR?
  - [ ] All requirements testable?
  - [ ] No contradictions between requirements?

OUTPUT: Validation result
GATE: Pass → Phase 8; Fail → remediate
Phase 8: Human Review
INPUT: Validated requirements
ACTION: Present for approval
WAIT: Human decision
OUTPUT: Approved requirements (or feedback for revision)
Phase 9: Finalization
INPUT: Approved requirements
ACTION: Package for Step 3 consumption
OUTPUT: Step 2 Complete Output
V2: FIRST REFINEMENT

Considering: Detailed Procedure V1 + Guidance Document V1 + To Do List V1

Refinements:

Procedure revealed need for intermediate structures (requirement seeds, draft requirements)
Guidance emphasized derivation patterns vary by source type
Need to distinguish between requirements derived from same source
Input Specification (Refined):

Field	Source	Required
Core Problem Statement	Step 1	Yes
Stakeholders	Step 1	Yes (3-6)
Hard Constraints	Step 1	Yes (3-6)
Soft Constraints	Step 1	No (0+)
Scope	Step 1	Yes
Success Criteria	Step 1	Yes
Traceability	Step 1	Yes
Input Validation:

Step 1 state = COMPLETE
Human approval present
Output Specification (Refined):

Step2Output:
  metadata:
    instance: integer
    step: 2
    state: enum
    timestamp: datetime
    input_reference: Step1Output.id
    approval: {approver, timestamp}
  
  requirements:
    - id: string
      statement: string
      category: FR | NFR | CR
      priority: must | should | could
      source:
        type: stakeholder | constraint | problem | criterion
        id: string
        derivation: string
      rationale: string
      testability: string

    stakeholders: mapping of stakeholder_id → [requirement_ids]
    constraints: mapping of constraint_id → [requirement_ids]
    problem: [requirement_ids addressing core problem]
  
  traceability:
    forward: requirement_id → source_ids
    backward: source_id → requirement_ids
Derivation Patterns by Source:

Source Type	Derivation Pattern	Typical Category	Typical Priority
Core Problem	Problem → Capability needed → FR	FR	must
Stakeholder Need	Need → What satisfies it → FR/NFR	FR or NFR	must/should
Stakeholder Concern	Concern → What mitigates it → NFR	NFR	should
Hard Constraint	Constraint → CR directly	CR	must
Soft Constraint	Constraint → FR/NFR	FR or NFR	should/could
Success Criterion	Criterion → What enables verification → FR/NFR	FR or NFR	must/should
State Definitions:

Guidance Document V2
Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Detailed Procedure V1

Context (Refined)
Step 2 is the bridge between problem definition (Step 1) and success definition (Step 3). Requirements operationalize the problem: they state what the solution must do such that the problem is solved. Step 3 will transform requirements into objectives that define success.

Instance 0 Consideration: At the universal level, requirement categories (FR, NFR, CR) are abstract. Instance N may introduce domain-specific categories (e.g., Security Requirements, Agency Requirements). The derivation patterns should accommodate extension.

Derivation Philosophy
Requirements are derived, not invented.

The Step 1 Output contains all the sources from which requirements emerge:

Core Problem → What must the solution do?
Stakeholder Needs → What must the solution provide?
Stakeholder Concerns → What must the solution protect against?
Hard Constraints → What must the solution never violate?
Soft Constraints → What should the solution respect?
Success Criteria → What must be verifiable?
The derivation process is systematic: each source is analyzed, and requirements are extracted. This ensures:

Completeness: Nothing in Step 1 is ignored
Traceability: Every requirement has a source
Coverage: Every stakeholder, constraint, and criterion is addressed
Requirement Quality
The "Shall" Form: Requirements use "shall" to indicate obligation: "The solution shall [verb] [object]."

Testability: A requirement is testable if there exists a method (demonstration, inspection, analysis, test) to determine whether it is satisfied. At Instance 0, testability is semantic (can we tell?), not procedural (how exactly?).

Atomicity: Each requirement should express one obligation. Compound requirements should be split.

Abstraction Level: Requirements state WHAT, not HOW. Implementation details are out of scope.

Priority Semantics
Priority	Meaning	Source Indicators
must	Solution fails without this	Hard constraints, core problem essentials, critical stakeholder needs
should	Solution disappoints without this	Soft constraints, important needs, significant concerns
could	Solution enhanced by this	Nice-to-have, minor concerns, stretch goals
Coverage Requirements
At minimum:

Every stakeholder's primary need → at least one requirement
Every hard constraint → exactly one CR
Core problem → at least one FR
Soft constraints and success criteria should also generate requirements, but coverage is not strictly enforced.

Anti-Patterns (Refined)
Anti-Pattern	Example	Remedy
Implementation in requirement	"The solution shall use a database"	State capability: "The solution shall persist data"
Orphan requirement	Requirement with no source	Trace or remove
Untestable requirement	"The solution shall be good"	Make specific and verifiable
Missing coverage	Stakeholder with no requirements	Derive from their need/concern
Compound requirement	"The solution shall do X and Y and Z"	Split into atomic requirements
Priority inflation	Everything is "must"	Reserve must for true failures
Detailed Procedure V2
Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Guidance Document V2

State Machine
STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, DERIVING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}

TRANSITIONS:
  RECEIVED   → ANALYZING  : on input_validated()
  ANALYZING  → DERIVING   : on sources_analyzed()
  DERIVING   → VALIDATING : on requirements_complete()
  VALIDATING → DERIVING   : on validation_failure()
  VALIDATING → REVIEWING  : on validation_success()
  REVIEWING  → DERIVING   : on rejection(feedback)
  REVIEWING  → COMPLETE   : on approval()
Phase 1: Reception & Validation [RECEIVED]
INPUT: Step 1 Output

VALIDATE:
  1. Step 1 state = COMPLETE
  2. Approval present with timestamp and approver
  3. Required fields present:
     - core_problem (statement)
     - stakeholders (3-6 with id, need, concern)
     - constraints.hard (3-6 with id, statement)
     - scope.in_scope (≥1)
     - success_criteria (≥1)

IF validation fails:
  HALT with error (cannot proceed without valid Step 1)

INITIALIZE Step2Output:
  - metadata.step = 2
  - metadata.state = RECEIVED
  - metadata.input_reference = Step1Output.id

TRANSITION → ANALYZING
Phase 2: Source Analysis [ANALYZING]
INPUT: Validated Step 1 Output

ACTION: Extract requirement seeds from each source

2.1 CORE PROBLEM ANALYSIS:
    READ core_problem.statement
    ASK: What capabilities must exist to solve this problem?
    FOR EACH capability identified:
      CREATE seed:
        source_type: problem
        source_id: core_problem
        capability: [description]
        suggested_category: FR
        suggested_priority: must

2.2 STAKEHOLDER ANALYSIS:
    FOR EACH stakeholder IN stakeholders:
      
      ANALYZE primary_need:
        ASK: What must the solution do/provide to meet this need?
        CREATE seed:
          source_type: stakeholder
          source_id: stakeholder.id
          source_aspect: need
          capability: [description]
          suggested_category: FR or NFR
          suggested_priority: must or should (based on criticality)
      
      ANALYZE primary_concern:
        ASK: What must the solution do/avoid to address this concern?
        CREATE seed:
          source_type: stakeholder
          source_id: stakeholder.id
          source_aspect: concern
          capability: [description]
          suggested_category: NFR (typically)
          suggested_priority: should (typically)

2.3 HARD CONSTRAINT ANALYSIS:
    FOR EACH constraint IN constraints.hard:
      CREATE seed:
        source_type: constraint
        source_id: constraint.id
        constraint_statement: constraint.statement
        suggested_category: CR
        suggested_priority: must

2.4 SOFT CONSTRAINT ANALYSIS:
    FOR EACH constraint IN constraints.soft:
      ASK: What capability or quality respects this preference?
      CREATE seed:
        source_type: constraint
        source_id: constraint.id
        capability: [description]
        suggested_category: FR or NFR
        suggested_priority: should or could

2.5 SUCCESS CRITERIA ANALYSIS:
    FOR EACH criterion IN success_criteria:
      ASK: What must the solution do/be to enable this verification?
      CREATE seed:
        source_type: criterion
        source_id: criterion.id
        capability: [description]
        suggested_category: FR or NFR
        suggested_priority: must or should

OUTPUT: List of requirement seeds

TRANSITION → DERIVING
Phase 3: Requirement Derivation [DERIVING]
INPUT: Requirement seeds

INITIALIZE:
  fr_counter = 0
  nfr_counter = 0
  cr_counter = 0

FOR EACH seed:
  
  3.1 DETERMINE CATEGORY:
      category = seed.suggested_category
      (may override based on analysis)
  
  3.2 ASSIGN IDENTIFIER:
      IF category = FR:  id = "FR-" + (++fr_counter, zero-padded to 3 digits)
      IF category = NFR: id = "NFR-" + (++nfr_counter, zero-padded to 3 digits)
      IF category = CR:  id = "CR-" + (++cr_counter, zero-padded to 3 digits)
  
  3.3 FORMULATE STATEMENT:
      statement = "The solution shall " + [verb phrase from capability]
      ENSURE: 
        - One obligation per statement
        - No implementation details
        - Testable (can determine satisfaction)
  
  3.4 DETERMINE PRIORITY:
      priority = seed.suggested_priority
      VALIDATE against source:
        - hard constraint → must (mandatory)
        - core problem essential → must
        - soft constraint → should or could
        - stakeholder need (critical) → must
        - stakeholder need (important) → should
        - stakeholder concern → should
        - nice-to-have → could
  
  3.5 DOCUMENT SOURCE:
      source.type = seed.source_type
      source.id = seed.source_id
      source.derivation = [how requirement was derived from source]
  
  3.6 DOCUMENT RATIONALE:
      rationale = [why this requirement exists]
  
  3.7 DOCUMENT TESTABILITY:
      testability = [how satisfaction can be verified]
  
  3.8 ADD to requirements list

OUTPUT: Complete requirements list

TRANSITION → VALIDATING (via coverage and consistency checks)
Phase 4: Coverage & Consistency [DERIVING continued]
INPUT: Requirements list

4.1 BUILD COVERAGE MAP:
    FOR EACH stakeholder:
      coverage.stakeholders[stakeholder.id] = 
        [req.id FOR req IN requirements WHERE req.source.id = stakeholder.id]
    
    FOR EACH hard_constraint:
      coverage.constraints[constraint.id] = 
        [req.id FOR req IN requirements WHERE req.source.id = constraint.id]
    
    coverage.problem = 
      [req.id FOR req IN requirements WHERE req.source.type = problem]

4.2 CHECK COVERAGE:
    FOR EACH stakeholder:
      IF coverage.stakeholders[stakeholder.id] is empty:
        FLAG: Missing coverage for stakeholder
        ACTION: Derive requirement from their need
    
    FOR EACH hard_constraint:
      IF coverage.constraints[constraint.id] is empty:
        FLAG: Missing CR for constraint
        ACTION: Create CR directly from constraint
    
    IF coverage.problem is empty:
      FLAG: No requirements address core problem
      ACTION: Derive FR from core problem

4.3 CHECK CONSISTENCY:
    FOR EACH pair (req_a, req_b) IN requirements:
      IF contradicts(req_a, req_b):
        FLAG: Contradiction detected
        ACTION: Resolve (modify, remove, or escalate to human)
    
    CHECK for duplicates:
      IF semantically_equivalent(req_a, req_b):
        ACTION: Merge or remove duplicate

4.4 CHECK PRIORITY DISTRIBUTION:
    IF all requirements are "must":
      WARNING: Priority inflation
      ACTION: Review and demote non-essentials

OUTPUT: Validated requirements with coverage map

TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 5: Validation [VALIDATING]
INPUT: Requirements with coverage

EXECUTE validation rules:

  V1: ∀ requirement: id ≠ null
  V2: ∀ requirement: statement starts with "The solution shall"
  V3: ∀ requirement: category ∈ {FR, NFR, CR}
  V4: ∀ requirement: priority ∈ {must, should, could}
  V5: ∀ requirement: source.type ≠ null AND source.id ≠ null
  V6: ∀ requirement: rationale ≠ null
  V7: ∀ requirement: testability ≠ null
  V8: ∀ stakeholder: coverage.stakeholders[id].length ≥ 1
  V9: ∀ hard_constraint: coverage.constraints[id].length ≥ 1
  V10: coverage.problem.length ≥ 1
  V11: No unresolved contradictions
  V12: No unresolved duplicates

IF all pass:
  TRANSITION → REVIEWING
ELSE:
  LOG failures
  TRANSITION → DERIVING (remediate)
Phase 6: Human Review [REVIEWING]
INPUT: Validated requirements

PRESENT:
  1. Requirements list (grouped by category)
  2. Coverage summary
  3. Priority distribution
  4. Traceability matrix

REQUEST: APPROVE | REJECT | MODIFY

WAIT for human decision

IF APPROVE:
  LOG approval
  TRANSITION → COMPLETE

IF REJECT:
  LOG feedback
  TRANSITION → DERIVING (with feedback)

IF MODIFY:
  APPLY modifications
  TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 7: Finalization [COMPLETE]
INPUT: Approved requirements

FINALIZE:
  metadata.state = COMPLETE
  metadata.approval = {approver, timestamp}

BUILD traceability:
  forward: requirement_id → [source_ids]
  backward: source_id → [requirement_ids]

PACKAGE Step2Output

EMIT for Step 3

EXIT: Step 2 complete
V3: FINAL REFINEMENT
Data Sheet V3
Considering: Detailed Procedure V2 + Guidance Document V2 + To Do List V2

Input Specification
Required Input: Step 1 Output (COMPLETE, approved)

Field	Path	Validation
Core Problem	core_problem.statement	Non-empty string
Stakeholders	stakeholders[]	Count ∈ [3,6]; each has id, primary_need, primary_concern
Hard Constraints	constraints.hard[]	Count ∈ [3,6]; each has id, statement
Soft Constraints	constraints.soft[]	Count ≥ 0; each has id, statement
In Scope	scope.in_scope[]	Count ≥ 1
Out Scope	scope.out_of_scope[]	Count ≥ 1
Success Criteria	success_criteria[]	Count ≥ 1; each has id, criterion
Approval	metadata.approval	Present with approver, timestamp
Output Specification
Step2Output:
  metadata:
    instance: integer
    step: 2
    state: COMPLETE
    timestamp: datetime
    input_reference: string (Step1Output identifier)
    approval:
      approver: string
      timestamp: datetime
  
  requirements:
    - id: string                    # FR-001, NFR-001, CR-001
      statement: string             # "The solution shall..."
      category: FR | NFR | CR
      priority: must | should | could
      source:
        type: problem | stakeholder | constraint | criterion
        id: string                  # Reference to Step 1 element
        aspect: string | null       # For stakeholders: need | concern
        derivation: string          # How requirement was derived
      rationale: string             # Why this requirement exists
      testability: string           # How to verify satisfaction
  
  coverage:
    stakeholders:                   # stakeholder_id → [requirement_ids]
      [stakeholder_id]: [string]
    constraints:                    # constraint_id → [requirement_ids]
      [constraint_id]: [string]
    problem: [string]               # requirement_ids addressing core problem
  
  summary:
    total: integer
    by_category:
      FR: integer
      NFR: integer
      CR: integer
    by_priority:
      must: integer
      should: integer
      could: integer
  
  traceability:
    forward:                        # requirement_id → source_ids
      [requirement_id]: [string]
    backward:                       # source_id → requirement_ids
      [source_id]: [string]
Requirement Quality Criteria
Criterion	Definition	Validation
Atomic	One obligation per requirement	No "and" connecting distinct obligations
Abstract	States what, not how	No implementation details
Testable	Can determine satisfaction	Testability field describes method
Traced	Has identified source	Source fields non-empty
Prioritized	Has assigned priority	Priority ∈ {must, should, could}
Derivation Patterns
Source	Analysis Question	Typical Output
Core Problem	What capabilities solve this?	FR (must)
Stakeholder Need	What satisfies this need?	FR or NFR (must/should)
Stakeholder Concern	What mitigates this concern?	NFR (should)
Hard Constraint	What limit must be respected?	CR (must)
Soft Constraint	What preference should be honored?	FR or NFR (should/could)
Success Criterion	What enables this verification?	FR or NFR (must/should)
State Definitions
State	Entry Condition	Exit Condition	Human Interaction
RECEIVED	Step 1 Output provided	Input validated	None
ANALYZING	Input valid	All sources analyzed	None
DERIVING	Sources analyzed	Requirements complete, coverage verified	None
VALIDATING	Requirements drafted	All validation rules pass	None
REVIEWING	Validation passed	Human decision received	Required (gate)
COMPLETE	Approved	Terminal	None
Validation Rules
ID	Rule	Severity
V1	Every requirement has non-empty id	Error
V2	Every statement begins "The solution shall"	Error
V3	Category ∈ {FR, NFR, CR}	Error
V4	Priority ∈ {must, should, could}	Error
V5	Source.type and source.id non-empty	Error
V6	Rationale non-empty	Error
V7	Testability non-empty	Error
V8	Every stakeholder has ≥1 requirement	Error
V9	Every hard constraint has ≥1 CR	Error
V10	Core problem has ≥1 FR	Error
V11	No contradictory requirements	Error
V12	No duplicate requirements	Warning
V13	Not all requirements are "must"	Warning
