Guidance Document V3
Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Detailed Procedure V2

Purpose
This document orients the agent executing Step 2 to the context, principles, and considerations for deriving well-formed requirements from a Problem Statement.

Position in Workflow
Step 2 bridges problem definition (Step 1) and success definition (Step 3).

Step 1 answers: What problem are we solving?
Step 2 answers: What must the solution do?
Step 3 answers: What does success look like?
Requirements are the specification that the solution must satisfy. They are the contract between problem and solution.

Instance 0 Considerations
At the universal level:

Categories (FR, NFR, CR) are abstract containers
Instance N may add domain-specific categories (e.g., Security Requirements, Agency Requirements)
Derivation patterns should be extensible without modification to Instance 0
Priority semantics (must/should/could) are universal
Avoid over-constraining: Requirements should be expressed semantically, not syntactically. The "shall" form is guidance for clarity, not rigid syntax enforcement.

Core Principles
1. Derivation, Not Invention
Every requirement traces to a source in Step 1:

Core Problem → Functional capabilities
Stakeholder Needs → Features and qualities
Stakeholder Concerns → Safeguards and constraints
Hard Constraints → Non-negotiable limits
Soft Constraints → Preferences
Success Criteria → Verifiable properties
No orphan requirements. If a requirement cannot be traced, it either derives from an unstated assumption (elicit it) or does not belong.

2. Requirements State WHAT, Not HOW
Requirements define obligation, not implementation.

Avoid	Prefer
"The solution shall use a relational database"	"The solution shall persist data durably"
"The solution shall implement OAuth"	"The solution shall authenticate users securely"
At Instance 0, this principle is especially important: implementation choices are domain-dependent.

3. Atomic Requirements
Each requirement expresses one obligation. Compound requirements hide complexity and impede traceability.

Avoid	Prefer
"The solution shall do X and Y and Z"	"FR-001: ...X", "FR-002: ...Y", "FR-003: ...Z"
4. Testability
A requirement is testable if there exists some method to determine whether it is satisfied. Methods include:

Demonstration: Show the capability working
Inspection: Examine the solution's structure
Analysis: Reason about properties
Test: Execute and measure
At Instance 0, testability is semantic: can we tell if it's satisfied? Instance N may add specific verification methods.

5. Priority Discipline
Priority	Meaning	Consequence of Absence
must	Non-negotiable	Solution fails
should	Important	Solution disappoints
could	Desirable	Solution is less than ideal
Avoid priority inflation. If everything is "must," priority conveys no information. Reserve "must" for true failures.

6. Coverage Completeness
Minimum coverage ensures Step 1 elements are not lost:

Every stakeholder → at least one requirement
Every hard constraint → at least one CR
Core problem → at least one FR
This is a floor, not a ceiling. Additional requirements are expected.

Derivation Patterns (Expanded)
From Core Problem
Problem: "How does an agent reliably transform X into Y?"

Analysis: What capabilities enable this transformation?
  - Capability to receive X
  - Capability to analyze X
  - Capability to produce Y
  - Capability to validate Y

Requirements:
  FR-001: The solution shall receive X from the designated source.
  FR-002: The solution shall analyze X to extract relevant information.
  FR-003: The solution shall produce Y in the specified format.
  FR-004: The solution shall validate Y against defined criteria.
From Stakeholder Need
Stakeholder: Executing Agent
Need: Clear, unambiguous procedure

Analysis: What satisfies this need?
  - Procedure with explicit steps
  - Defined states and transitions
  - No gaps or ambiguities

Requirement:
  FR-005: The solution shall provide an explicit procedure with defined states and transitions.
From Stakeholder Concern
Stakeholder: Human Overseer
Concern: Outputs that misrepresent the problem

Analysis: What mitigates this concern?
  - Traceability to verify derivation
  - Review points to catch errors

Requirement:
  NFR-001: The solution shall provide traceability from outputs to inputs.
From Hard Constraint
Constraint: "Every output element must be traceable to input"

Direct mapping:
  CR-001: The solution shall ensure every output element is traceable to input.
From Success Criterion
Criterion: "An agent can follow the methodology to produce output from any problem"

Analysis: What enables this verification?
  - Methodology must be complete
  - Methodology must be unambiguous
  - Methodology must be domain-agnostic

Requirement:
  FR-006: The solution shall be complete, unambiguous, and domain-agnostic.
Anti-Patterns
Anti-Pattern	Symptom	Remedy
Orphan requirement	No source documented	Trace to source or remove
Implementation requirement	Specifies technology or approach	Abstract to capability
Compound requirement	Multiple "and" clauses	Split into atomic requirements
Untestable requirement	No way to verify	Make specific or remove
Missing coverage	Stakeholder/constraint with no requirements	Derive from source
Priority inflation	All requirements are "must"	Demote non-essentials
Duplicate requirements	Same obligation stated differently	Merge
Contradictory requirements	Mutual exclusion	Resolve or escalate
Quality Checklist
Before human review, verify:

Completeness: All sources analyzed, all coverage met
Consistency: No contradictions or duplicates
Clarity: Each statement unambiguous
Abstraction: No implementation details
Atomicity: One obligation per requirement
Testability: Each requirement verifiable
Traceability: Every requirement traced to source
Priority: Distribution reflects actual importance
Detailed Procedure V3
Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Guidance Document V3

State Machine
STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, DERIVING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}
INITIAL = RECEIVED
TERMINAL = COMPLETE

TRANSITIONS:
  RECEIVED   → ANALYZING   : input_valid()
  ANALYZING  → DERIVING    : sources_analyzed()
  DERIVING   → VALIDATING  : derivation_complete()
  VALIDATING → DERIVING    : validation_failed()
  VALIDATING → REVIEWING   : validation_passed()
  REVIEWING  → DERIVING    : rejected(feedback)
  REVIEWING  → VALIDATING  : modify(changes)
  REVIEWING  → COMPLETE    : approved()

HUMAN_GATES = {REVIEWING}
Phase 1: Reception & Validation [RECEIVED]
Input: Step 1 Output

Procedure:

1. RECEIVE Step 1 Output

2. VALIDATE input:
   CHECK metadata.state = COMPLETE
   CHECK metadata.approval present (approver, timestamp)
   CHECK core_problem.statement non-empty
   CHECK stakeholders.count ∈ [3,6]
   CHECK constraints.hard.count ∈ [3,6]
   CHECK scope.in_scope.count ≥ 1
   CHECK success_criteria.count ≥ 1

3. IF any check fails:
   HALT with error: "Invalid Step 1 Output: [details]"
   (Cannot proceed without valid input)

4. INITIALIZE Step2Output:
   metadata.instance = current_instance
   metadata.step = 2
   metadata.state = RECEIVED
   metadata.input_reference = Step1Output.id
   requirements = []
   coverage = {stakeholders: {}, constraints: {}, problem: []}

5. TRANSITION → ANALYZING
Phase 2: Source Analysis [ANALYZING]
Input: Validated Step 1 Output

Procedure:

INITIALIZE seed_list = []

2.1 ANALYZE CORE PROBLEM:
    READ core_problem.statement
    
    IDENTIFY capabilities required:
      ASK: "What must exist for this problem to be solved?"
      FOR EACH capability:
        CREATE seed:
          source_type: problem
          source_id: core_problem
          capability: [capability description]
          category: FR
          priority: must
        APPEND to seed_list

2.2 ANALYZE STAKEHOLDERS:
    FOR EACH stakeholder IN stakeholders:
      
      ANALYZE need:
        READ stakeholder.primary_need
        ASK: "What must the solution do to satisfy this need?"
        CREATE seed:
          source_type: stakeholder
          source_id: stakeholder.id
          source_aspect: need
          capability: [what satisfies need]
          category: FR or NFR (judge by nature)
          priority: must or should (judge by criticality)
        APPEND to seed_list
      
      ANALYZE concern:
        READ stakeholder.primary_concern
        ASK: "What must the solution do to address this concern?"
        CREATE seed:
          source_type: stakeholder
          source_id: stakeholder.id
          source_aspect: concern
          capability: [what mitigates concern]
          category: NFR (typically)
          priority: should (typically)
        APPEND to seed_list

2.3 ANALYZE HARD CONSTRAINTS:
    FOR EACH constraint IN constraints.hard:
      CREATE seed:
        source_type: constraint
        source_id: constraint.id
        constraint_type: hard
        capability: constraint.statement (direct)
        category: CR
        priority: must
      APPEND to seed_list

2.4 ANALYZE SOFT CONSTRAINTS:
    FOR EACH constraint IN constraints.soft:
      ASK: "What capability or quality honors this preference?"
      CREATE seed:
        source_type: constraint
        source_id: constraint.id
        constraint_type: soft
        capability: [what honors preference]
        category: FR or NFR
        priority: should or could
      APPEND to seed_list

2.5 ANALYZE SUCCESS CRITERIA:
    FOR EACH criterion IN success_criteria:
      ASK: "What must be true for this criterion to be verifiable?"
      CREATE seed:
        source_type: criterion
        source_id: criterion.id
        capability: [what enables verification]
        category: FR or NFR
        priority: must or should
      APPEND to seed_list

OUTPUT: seed_list

TRANSITION → DERIVING
Phase 3: Requirement Derivation [DERIVING]
Input: seed_list

Procedure:

INITIALIZE counters: fr=0, nfr=0, cr=0

FOR EACH seed IN seed_list:

  3.1 ASSIGN CATEGORY:
      category = seed.category
      (May adjust based on deeper analysis)

  3.2 ASSIGN IDENTIFIER:
      SWITCH category:
        FR:  id = "FR-" + pad(++fr, 3)
        NFR: id = "NFR-" + pad(++nfr, 3)
        CR:  id = "CR-" + pad(++cr, 3)

  3.3 FORMULATE STATEMENT:
      statement = "The solution shall " + [verb phrase]
      
      VERIFY atomic:
        IF statement contains multiple obligations:
          SPLIT into separate seeds
          PROCESS each separately
      
      VERIFY abstract:
        IF statement contains implementation details:
          REWRITE to capability level

  3.4 ASSIGN PRIORITY:
      priority = seed.priority
      
      VERIFY consistency:
        IF source is hard constraint AND priority ≠ must:
          SET priority = must
        IF all priorities are must (at end):
          FLAG for review

  3.5 DOCUMENT SOURCE:
      source.type = seed.source_type
      source.id = seed.source_id
      source.aspect = seed.source_aspect (if applicable)
      source.derivation = [how requirement follows from source]

  3.6 DOCUMENT RATIONALE:
      rationale = [why this requirement is necessary]

  3.7 DOCUMENT TESTABILITY:
      testability = [method to verify: demonstration | inspection | analysis | test]

  3.8 CREATE REQUIREMENT:
      requirement = {id, statement, category, priority, source, rationale, testability}
      APPEND to requirements

OUTPUT: requirements list
Phase 4: Coverage & Consistency [DERIVING continued]
Input: requirements list

Procedure:

4.1 BUILD COVERAGE MAP:

    FOR EACH stakeholder IN Step1Output.stakeholders:
      coverage.stakeholders[stakeholder.id] = 
        FILTER requirements WHERE source.id = stakeholder.id
        EXTRACT ids
    
    FOR EACH constraint IN Step1Output.constraints.hard:
      coverage.constraints[constraint.id] = 
        FILTER requirements WHERE source.id = constraint.id
        EXTRACT ids
    
    coverage.problem = 
      FILTER requirements WHERE source.type = problem
      EXTRACT ids

4.2 VERIFY COVERAGE:

    FOR EACH stakeholder:
      IF coverage.stakeholders[stakeholder.id] is empty:
        LOG: "Gap: stakeholder {id} has no requirements"
        DERIVE requirement from stakeholder.primary_need
        ADD to requirements
        UPDATE coverage
    
    FOR EACH hard_constraint:
      IF coverage.constraints[constraint.id] is empty:
        LOG: "Gap: constraint {id} has no CR"
        CREATE CR directly from constraint
        ADD to requirements
        UPDATE coverage
    
    IF coverage.problem is empty:
      LOG: "Gap: core problem has no FR"
      DERIVE FR from core_problem.statement
      ADD to requirements
      UPDATE coverage

4.3 CHECK CONSISTENCY:

    FOR i IN range(len(requirements)):
      FOR j IN range(i+1, len(requirements)):
        IF contradicts(requirements[i], requirements[j]):
          LOG: "Contradiction: {id_i} vs {id_j}"
          RESOLVE:
            - Modify one or both
            - Remove one
            - Escalate to human if unresolvable
        
        IF semantically_equivalent(requirements[i], requirements[j]):
          LOG: "Duplicate: {id_i} and {id_j}"
          MERGE or REMOVE duplicate

4.4 CHECK PRIORITY DISTRIBUTION:

    must_count = COUNT WHERE priority = must
    total_count = len(requirements)
    
    IF must_count = total_count:
      LOG: "Warning: all requirements are must priority"
      REVIEW each for potential demotion

OUTPUT: requirements with coverage verified, consistency checked

TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 5: Validation [VALIDATING]
Input: requirements, coverage

Procedure:

EXECUTE validation rules:

V1:  ∀ req: req.id ≠ null AND req.id ≠ ""
V2:  ∀ req: req.statement starts with "The solution shall"
V3:  ∀ req: req.category ∈ {FR, NFR, CR}
V4:  ∀ req: req.priority ∈ {must, should, could}
V5:  ∀ req: req.source.type ≠ null AND req.source.id ≠ null
V6:  ∀ req: req.rationale ≠ null AND req.rationale ≠ ""
V7:  ∀ req: req.testability ≠ null AND req.testability ≠ ""
V8:  ∀ stakeholder_id: coverage.stakeholders[id].length ≥ 1
V9:  ∀ constraint_id: coverage.constraints[id].length ≥ 1
V10: coverage.problem.length ≥ 1
V11: No unresolved contradictions flagged
V12: No unresolved duplicates flagged (warning)
V13: NOT all requirements are must (warning)

COLLECT results:
  errors = [rule for rule in V1-V11 if failed]
  warnings = [rule for rule in V12-V13 if failed]

IF errors is not empty:
  LOG errors
  TRANSITION → DERIVING (remediate errors)
ELSE:
  LOG warnings (if any)
  TRANSITION → REVIEWING
Phase 6: Human Review [REVIEWING]
Human Interaction: Required (gate)

Input: Validated requirements, coverage, warnings

Procedure:

1. SET state: REVIEWING

2. PREPARE presentation:
   
   a. REQUIREMENTS BY CATEGORY:
      Group by FR, NFR, CR
      Show: id, statement, priority, source summary
   
   b. COVERAGE SUMMARY:
      Stakeholders: [id] → [requirement count]
      Constraints: [id] → [requirement count]
      Problem: [requirement count]
   
   c. PRIORITY DISTRIBUTION:
      must: [count]
      should: [count]
      could: [count]
   
   d. WARNINGS (if any):
      List with explanation

3. PRESENT to human

4. REQUEST decision: APPROVE | REJECT | MODIFY

5. WAIT for human response

6. PROCESS decision:

   IF APPROVE:
     LOG approval (approver, timestamp)
     TRANSITION → COMPLETE
   
   IF REJECT:
     RECEIVE feedback (what to fix, which phase to revisit)
     LOG rejection with feedback
     TRANSITION → DERIVING
   
   IF MODIFY:
     RECEIVE specific modifications
     APPLY modifications to requirements
     TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 7: Finalization [COMPLETE]
Input: Approved requirements

Procedure:

1. SET state: COMPLETE

2. RECORD approval:
   metadata.approval.approver = [from human]
   metadata.approval.timestamp = now()

3. COMPUTE summary:
   summary.total = len(requirements)
   summary.by_category.FR = COUNT WHERE category = FR
   summary.by_category.NFR = COUNT WHERE category = NFR
   summary.by_category.CR = COUNT WHERE category = CR
   summary.by_priority.must = COUNT WHERE priority = must
   summary.by_priority.should = COUNT WHERE priority = should
   summary.by_priority.could = COUNT WHERE priority = could

4. BUILD traceability:
   FOR EACH requirement:
     traceability.forward[requirement.id] = [source.id]
   
   FOR EACH source IN all_sources:
     traceability.backward[source.id] = 
       [req.id FOR req IN requirements WHERE req.source.id = source.id]

5. PACKAGE Step2Output:
   {metadata, requirements, coverage, summary, traceability}

6. EMIT for Step 3

7. LOG completion

EXIT: Step 2 Pass 1 complete
Summary

Phase 2: Source Analysis [ANALYZING]
2.1 Core Problem Analysis
Core Problem:

How does an agent reliably transform an unstructured problem description into a structured representation that enables systematic downstream reasoning, while remaining abstract enough to apply across any problem domain?

Capabilities Required:

Capability	Seed ID
Receive unstructured problem descriptions	SEED-P01
Analyze input to identify constituent elements	SEED-P02
Transform unstructured input into structured representation	SEED-P03
Validate output against defined criteria	SEED-P04
Maintain abstraction suitable for any domain	SEED-P05
Enable systematic downstream reasoning	SEED-P06
2.2 Stakeholder Analysis
SH-001: Executing Agent
Need: Clear, unambiguous procedure that can be followed deterministically Concern: Ambiguity that leads to inconsistent outputs or blocked progress

Seed ID	From	Capability
SEED-S01	Need	Provide explicit procedure with defined steps
SEED-S02	Need	Define states and transitions unambiguously
SEED-S03	Concern	Eliminate ambiguity in procedure specification
SEED-S04	Concern	Provide resolution path when progress is blocked
SH-002: Human Overseer
Need: Confidence that outputs accurately represent the problem Concern: Being presented with outputs that misrepresent the problem

Seed ID	From	Capability
SEED-S05	Need	Enable human verification of output accuracy
SEED-S06	Need	Present outputs in reviewable format
SEED-S07	Concern	Provide traceability for verification
SEED-S08	Concern	Support human correction of errors
SH-003: Downstream Steps
Need: Complete, well-formed input containing all information needed to proceed Concern: Missing information, unstated assumptions, or format inconsistencies

Seed ID	From	Capability
SEED-S09	Need	Produce output with all required fields
SEED-S10	Need	Ensure output is well-formed
SEED-S11	Concern	Make all assumptions explicit
SEED-S12	Concern	Maintain consistent output format
SH-004: Instance N Specializer
Need: Clear extension points; separation of universal from specializable Concern: Rigid structures that cannot accommodate domain-specific needs

Seed ID	From	Capability
SEED-S13	Need	Define clear extension points
SEED-S14	Need	Separate universal elements from specializable elements
SEED-S15	Concern	Avoid rigid structures that prevent extension
SEED-S16	Concern	Allow domain-specific additions without modification
SH-005: The Methodology Itself
Need: Internal consistency; coherent design Concern: Self-contradiction; circular dependencies; under-specification

Seed ID	From	Capability
SEED-S17	Need	Maintain internal consistency
SEED-S18	Need	Present coherent, unified design
SEED-S19	Concern	Avoid self-contradiction
SEED-S20	Concern	Avoid circular dependencies
SEED-S21	Concern	Specify sufficiently to avoid gaps

Phase 3: Requirement Derivation [DERIVING]
Functional Requirements (FR)
FR-001: Input Reception
Statement: The solution shall receive unstructured problem descriptions from a human source.

Attribute	Value
Category	FR

Rationale	The methodology cannot operate without receiving the problem to be processed
Testability	Demonstration: provide input and observe reception

Statement: The solution shall analyze input to identify constituent elements including stated problem, stakeholders, constraints, scope indicators, and success markers.

Attribute	Value
Category	FR

Rationale	Transformation from unstructured to structured requires element identification
Testability	Inspection: examine whether elements are identified from input

Statement: The solution shall identify gaps in the input where information is missing, ambiguous, or contradictory.

Attribute	Value

Priority	must
Source	SEED-S03 (SH-001 Concern), SEED-S11 (SH-003 Concern)

Rationale	Proceeding with gaps risks invalid downstream work

Guidance Document V1
Considering: Step 2 Output + Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1

Context
Step 3 transforms Requirements (what the solution must do) into Objectives (what success looks like). This is a reframing: requirements are obligations; objectives are achievements. The solution satisfies requirements; success is measured by achieving objectives.

Key Principles
Requirements Say "Shall"; Objectives Say "Achieved When":
Requirement: "The solution shall X"
Objective: "X is achieved when [success criteria]"
Objectives Are Verifiable: Each objective must have criteria by which achievement can be determined. Verification need not be quantitative; demonstration, inspection, review are valid methods.
Priority Inheritance:
must requirements → primary objectives
should requirements → secondary objectives
could requirements → tertiary objectives
Consolidation Is Permitted: Related requirements may consolidate into a single objective when they collectively define one coherent success condition.
Coverage: Every must-priority requirement must have a corresponding objective. Should and could requirements should be covered but with flexibility.
Anti-Patterns
Objectives that restate requirements without adding success criteria
Unverifiable objectives
Missing coverage for must requirements
Priority inflation (everything primary)
Detailed Procedure V1
Considering: Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1 + Guidance Document V1

Phase 1: Reception & Validation
INPUT: Step 2 Output
VALIDATE:
  - State = COMPLETE
  - Approval present
  - Requirements present with priorities
OUTPUT: Validated input
GATE: Validation passes → Phase 2
Phase 2: Requirement Analysis
INPUT: Requirements list
ACTION: Categorize by objective type:
  - FR → Capability objectives
  - NFR → Quality objectives
  - CR → Compliance objectives
OUTPUT: Categorized requirements
Phase 3: Transformation
INPUT: Categorized requirements
ACTION: For each requirement (or related group):
  - Reframe from obligation to achievement
  - "The solution shall X" → "X is achieved"
  - Identify what success means for this achievement
OUTPUT: Draft objectives
Phase 4: Success Criteria Definition
INPUT: Draft objectives
ACTION: For each objective:
  - Define: What does success look like?
  - Define: How will we know it's achieved?
OUTPUT: Objectives with success criteria
Phase 5: Prioritization
INPUT: Objectives with criteria
ACTION: Assign priority:
  - Primary: From must requirements
  - Secondary: From should requirements
  - Tertiary: From could requirements
OUTPUT: Prioritized objectives
Phase 6: Framework Construction
INPUT: Prioritized objectives
ACTION: Build success framework:
  - Minimum Viable: All primary objectives
  - Target: Primary + secondary objectives
  - Aspirational: All objectives
OUTPUT: Success framework
Phase 7: Traceability
INPUT: Complete objectives
ACTION: Map each objective to source requirement(s)
OUTPUT: Traceability mapping
Phase 8: Validation
INPUT: Complete output
VALIDATE:
  - Every must requirement has objective
  - Every objective has success criteria
  - Every objective has verification method
  - Priorities assigned correctly
OUTPUT: Validation result
GATE: Pass → Phase 9; Fail → remediate
Phase 9: Human Review
INPUT: Validated output
ACTION: Present for approval
WAIT: Human decision
OUTPUT: Approved output (or feedback)
Phase 10: Finalization
INPUT: Approved output
ACTION: Package for downstream use
OUTPUT: Step 3 Complete Output

Transformation Pattern:

Guidance Document V2
Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Detailed Procedure V1

Context (Refined)
Step 3 completes the definition phase of the 10-step workflow:

Step 1: What problem are we solving?
Step 2: What must the solution do?
Step 3: What does success look like?
After Step 3, the problem is fully defined. Steps 4-7 verify and validate this definition. Step 8 implements. The quality of the definition determines the quality of everything downstream.

Instance 0 Consideration: Objective categories (capability, quality, compliance) are abstract. Instance N may add domain-specific categories. Success criteria should be semantic, not syntactic.

Transformation Philosophy
From Obligation to Achievement:

Requirements express obligations: "The solution shall..." — these are constraints on the solution.

Objectives express achievements: "X is achieved when..." — these are goals to accomplish.

The transformation reframes the same content:

Requirement: What must be done
Objective: What success looks like when it's done
Success Criteria
Each objective requires two components:

Definition: What does success look like?
Describes the achieved state
Specific enough to recognize
Not necessarily quantitative
Verification: How will we know it's achieved?
Method to determine achievement
May be: demonstration, inspection, analysis, review, test
Must be feasible to execute
At Instance 0: Verification methods are semantic (what kind of method) not procedural (exact steps). Instance N may add specific procedures.

Priority Semantics
Requirement Priority	Objective Priority	Framework Level
must	primary	Minimum Viable
should	secondary	Target
could	tertiary	Aspirational
Minimum Viable: If all primary objectives are achieved, the solution is viable (it works). Failure to achieve any primary objective means the solution fails.

Target: If primary + secondary objectives are achieved, stakeholders are satisfied. This is the expected success state.

Aspirational: If all objectives are achieved, the solution exceeds expectations. This is full success.

Consolidation
Related requirements may consolidate into a single objective when:

They collectively define one coherent success condition
Separate objectives would be redundant or confusing
The consolidated objective remains specific and verifiable
Consolidation should not:

Obscure distinct success conditions
Create compound objectives that are hard to verify
Lose traceability (all source requirements must be recorded)
Quality Criteria
Every objective must be:

Criterion	Question	Failure Mode
Specific	Is there only one interpretation?	Ambiguous objectives cannot be verified
Achievable	Can this realistically be done?	Unachievable objectives are meaningless
Relevant	Does this trace to a requirement?	Orphan objectives lack justification
Verifiable	Can we determine if achieved?	Unverifiable objectives cannot guide success
Note: These are not SMART criteria. Objectives need not be time-bound or measurable in quantitative terms. Verification may be qualitative.

Anti-Patterns (Refined)
Anti-Pattern	Symptom	Remedy
Restated requirement	Objective is just "shall" → "achieved" with no criteria	Add meaningful success criteria
Unverifiable objective	No feasible verification method	Define how achievement is determined
Missing coverage	must requirement without primary objective	Transform the requirement
Priority inflation	All objectives are primary	Reserve primary for must requirements
Over-consolidation	Single objective covers unrelated requirements	Split into coherent objectives
Under-consolidation	Many tiny objectives for related requirements	Consolidate where coherent
Orphan objective	Objective without source requirement	Trace or remove
Detailed Procedure V2
Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Guidance Document V2

State Machine
STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, TRANSFORMING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}

TRANSITIONS:
  RECEIVED     → ANALYZING     : input_valid()
  ANALYZING    → TRANSFORMING  : analysis_complete()
  TRANSFORMING → VALIDATING    : transformation_complete()
  VALIDATING   → TRANSFORMING  : validation_failed()
  VALIDATING   → REVIEWING     : validation_passed()
  REVIEWING    → TRANSFORMING  : rejected(feedback)
  REVIEWING    → COMPLETE      : approved()
Phase 1: Reception & Validation [RECEIVED]
INPUT: Step 2 Output

VALIDATE:
  1. Step 2 state = COMPLETE
  2. Approval present (approver, timestamp)
  3. Requirements present:
     - Each has: id, statement, category, priority
     - Categories ∈ {FR, NFR, CR}
     - Priorities ∈ {must, should, could}

IF any check fails:
  HALT with error

INITIALIZE Step3Output:
  metadata.step = 3
  metadata.state = RECEIVED
  metadata.input_reference = Step2Output.id
  objectives = []
  success_framework = {}

TRANSITION → ANALYZING
Phase 2: Requirement Analysis [ANALYZING]
INPUT: Validated Step 2 Output

2.1 GROUP BY CATEGORY:
    fr_requirements = FILTER WHERE category = FR
    nfr_requirements = FILTER WHERE category = NFR
    cr_requirements = FILTER WHERE category = CR

2.2 GROUP BY PRIORITY:
    must_requirements = FILTER WHERE priority = must
    should_requirements = FILTER WHERE priority = should
    could_requirements = FILTER WHERE priority = could

2.3 IDENTIFY CONSOLIDATION CANDIDATES:
    FOR EACH pair of requirements:
      IF semantically_related(req_a, req_b):
        ADD to consolidation_candidates

OUTPUT: 
  - Grouped requirements
  - Consolidation candidates

TRANSITION → TRANSFORMING
Phase 3: Objective Transformation [TRANSFORMING]
INPUT: Grouped requirements, consolidation candidates

INITIALIZE counters: cap=0, qual=0, comp=0

3.1 PROCESS CONSOLIDATION CANDIDATES:
    FOR EACH candidate group:
      IF should_consolidate(group):
        CREATE single objective from group
        MARK all requirements in group as processed
      ELSE:
        LEAVE for individual processing

3.2 PROCESS REMAINING REQUIREMENTS:
    FOR EACH unprocessed requirement:
      
      DETERMINE category:
        IF req.category = FR: obj_category = capability
        IF req.category = NFR: obj_category = quality
        IF req.category = CR: obj_category = compliance
      
      ASSIGN identifier:
        SWITCH obj_category:
          capability: id = "CAP-" + pad(++cap, 3)
          quality: id = "QUAL-" + pad(++qual, 3)
          compliance: id = "COMP-" + pad(++comp, 3)
      
      FORMULATE statement:
        TRANSFORM "The solution shall X" → "X is achieved successfully"
        ENSURE statement describes achievement, not obligation
      
      DETERMINE priority:
        IF req.priority = must: obj_priority = primary
        IF req.priority = should: obj_priority = secondary
        IF req.priority = could: obj_priority = tertiary
      
      DEFINE success_criteria.definition:
        ASK: "What does it look like when this is achieved?"
        WRITE description of successful state
      
      DEFINE success_criteria.verification:
        ASK: "How will we know this is achieved?"
        SELECT method: demonstration | inspection | analysis | review | test
        DESCRIBE application of method
      
      RECORD traces_to:
        requirements = [req.id] (or multiple if consolidated)
      
      ADD objective to objectives list

OUTPUT: objectives list

CONTINUE to Phase 4
Phase 4: Success Framework Construction [TRANSFORMING continued]
INPUT: objectives list

4.1 BUILD MINIMUM VIABLE:
    minimum_viable.objectives = 
      FILTER objectives WHERE priority = primary
      EXTRACT ids
    minimum_viable.description = 
      "Solution is viable; core functionality works"

4.2 BUILD TARGET:
    target.objectives = 
      FILTER objectives WHERE priority ∈ {primary, secondary}
      EXTRACT ids
    target.description = 
      "Stakeholders are satisfied; expected success achieved"

4.3 BUILD ASPIRATIONAL:
    aspirational.objectives = 
      ALL objective ids
    aspirational.description = 
      "Full success; all enhancements included"

OUTPUT: success_framework

CONTINUE to Phase 5
Phase 5: Traceability & Coverage [TRANSFORMING continued]
INPUT: objectives, success_framework

5.1 BUILD TRACEABILITY:
    FOR EACH objective:
      traceability.forward[objective.id] = objective.traces_to.requirements
    
    FOR EACH requirement IN Step2Output.requirements:
      traceability.backward[requirement.id] = 
        FIND objective WHERE requirement.id IN traces_to.requirements

5.2 BUILD COVERAGE MAP:
    FOR EACH requirement:
      coverage.requirements[requirement.id] = 
        traceability.backward[requirement.id]
    
    coverage.by_priority.must = 
      [obj.id FOR obj IN objectives 
       WHERE any source requirement has priority = must]
    (similarly for should, could)

5.3 VERIFY COVERAGE:
    FOR EACH must_requirement:
      IF coverage.requirements[id] is null:
        FLAG: "Must requirement {id} has no objective"
        ACTION: Create objective

OUTPUT: traceability, coverage

TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 6: Validation [VALIDATING]
INPUT: objectives, success_framework, traceability, coverage

EXECUTE validation rules:

  V1: ∀ objective: id ≠ null
  V2: ∀ objective: statement describes achievement (not obligation)
  V3: ∀ objective: category ∈ {capability, quality, compliance}
  V4: ∀ objective: priority ∈ {primary, secondary, tertiary}
  V5: ∀ objective: success_criteria.definition ≠ null
  V6: ∀ objective: success_criteria.verification ≠ null
  V7: ∀ objective: traces_to.requirements.length ≥ 1
  V8: ∀ must_requirement: ∃ objective with primary priority
  V9: success_framework.minimum_viable.objectives = all primary objectives
  V10: success_framework.target.objectives = all primary + secondary
  V11: success_framework.aspirational.objectives = all objectives

QUALITY CHECKS:
  Q1: ∀ objective: is_specific(statement)
  Q2: ∀ objective: is_achievable(statement)
  Q3: ∀ objective: is_verifiable(success_criteria)

IF errors:
  LOG failures
  TRANSITION → TRANSFORMING (remediate)
ELSE:
  LOG warnings (if any)
  TRANSITION → REVIEWING
Phase 7: Human Review [REVIEWING]
INPUT: Validated output

PRESENT:
  1. Objectives by category
  2. Objectives by priority
  3. Success framework (3 levels)
  4. Coverage summary
  5. Quality assessment

REQUEST: APPROVE | REJECT | MODIFY

WAIT for decision

IF APPROVE:
  LOG approval
  TRANSITION → COMPLETE

IF REJECT:
  LOG feedback
  TRANSITION → TRANSFORMING

IF MODIFY:
  APPLY modifications
  TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 8: Finalization [COMPLETE]
INPUT: Approved output

FINALIZE:
  metadata.state = COMPLETE
  metadata.approval = {approver, timestamp}

COMPUTE summary:
  summary.total = len(objectives)
  summary.by_category = counts
  summary.by_priority = counts

PACKAGE Step3Output

EMIT for downstream use

EXIT: Step 3 complete
V3: FINAL REFINEMENT
Data Sheet V3
Considering: Detailed Procedure V2 + Guidance Document V2 + To Do List V2

Input Specification
Required Input: Step 2 Output (COMPLETE, approved)

Field	Path	Validation
Requirements	requirements[]	Count ≥ 1; each has id, statement, category, priority
Coverage	coverage	Present
Summary	summary	Present with counts
Traceability	traceability	Present
Approval	metadata.approval	Present with approver, timestamp
Output Specification
Step3Output:
  metadata:
    instance: integer
    step: 3
    state: COMPLETE
    timestamp: datetime
    input_reference: string
    approval:
      approver: string
      timestamp: datetime
  
  objectives:
    - id: string                      # CAP-001, QUAL-001, COMP-001
      statement: string               # Achievement formulation
      category: capability | quality | compliance
      priority: primary | secondary | tertiary
      success_criteria:
        definition: string            # What success looks like
        verification: string          # How achievement is determined
      traces_to:
        requirements: [string]        # Source requirement ids
  
  success_framework:
    minimum_viable:
      description: string
      objectives: [string]            # Primary objective ids
      achievement_meaning: string     # What achieving this level means
    target:
      description: string
      objectives: [string]            # Primary + secondary ids
      achievement_meaning: string
    aspirational:
      description: string
      objectives: [string]            # All objective ids
      achievement_meaning: string
  
  coverage:
    requirements:                     # requirement_id → objective_id
      [requirement_id]: string
    by_source_priority:
      must: [string]                  # Objective ids from must requirements
      should: [string]                # Objective ids from should requirements
      could: [string]                 # Objective ids from could requirements
  
  summary:
    total: integer
    by_category:
      capability: integer
      quality: integer
      compliance: integer
    by_priority:
      primary: integer
      secondary: integer
      tertiary: integer
    framework_sizes:
      minimum_viable: integer
      target: integer
      aspirational: integer
  
  traceability:
    forward:                          # objective_id → [requirement_ids]
      [objective_id]: [string]
    backward:                         # requirement_id → objective_id
      [requirement_id]: string
Transformation Patterns
Requirement to Objective:

REQUIREMENT                          OBJECTIVE
───────────                          ─────────
"The solution shall X"        →      "X is achieved successfully"
category: FR                  →      category: capability
category: NFR                 →      category: quality
category: CR                  →      category: compliance
priority: must                →      priority: primary
priority: should              →      priority: secondary
priority: could               →      priority: tertiary
Consolidation Pattern:

WHEN: Multiple requirements define aspects of one coherent success condition
THEN: Create single objective tracing to all source requirements
PRESERVE: All requirement ids in traces_to
ENSURE: Consolidated objective remains specific and verifiable
Success Framework Definition
Level	Composition	Meaning
Minimum Viable	All primary objectives	Solution works; core requirements met
Target	Primary + secondary	Stakeholders satisfied; expected success
Aspirational	All objectives	Full success; exceeds expectations
Objective Quality Criteria
Criterion	Definition	Validation Test
Specific	Single clear interpretation	Can two people agree on meaning?
Achievable	Realistic given constraints	Is there a path to achievement?
Relevant	Traces to requirement	Does traces_to.requirements have entries?
Verifiable	Achievement can be determined	Is verification method feasible?
State Definitions
State	Entry Condition	Exit Condition	Human Interaction
RECEIVED	Step 2 Output provided	Input validated	None
ANALYZING	Input valid	Requirements analyzed	None
TRANSFORMING	Analysis complete	Objectives complete	None
VALIDATING	Transformation complete	All validation passes	None
REVIEWING	Validation passed	Human decision received	Required (gate)
COMPLETE	Approved	Terminal	None
Validation Rules
ID	Rule	Severity
V1	Every objective has non-empty id	Error
V2	Every objective statement describes achievement	Error
V3	Category ∈ {capability, quality, compliance}	Error
V4	Priority ∈ {primary, secondary, tertiary}	Error
V5	success_criteria.definition non-empty	Error
V6	success_criteria.verification non-empty	Error
V7	traces_to.requirements.length ≥ 1	Error
V8	Every must requirement has corresponding objective	Error
V9	minimum_viable contains exactly primary objectives	Error
V10	target contains exactly primary + secondary	Error
V11	aspirational contains all objectives	Error
V12	Every should requirement has objective	Warning
V13	Every could requirement has objective	Warning
V14	Objective is specific (quality check)	Warning
V15	Objective is achievable (quality check)	Warning
To Do List V3

Complete Checklist
Phase 1: Reception [RECEIVED]
 Receive Step 2 Output
 Validate input:
 metadata.state = COMPLETE
 metadata.approval present
 requirements[].id all non-empty
 requirements[].statement all non-empty
 requirements[].category ∈ {FR, NFR, CR}
 requirements[].priority ∈ {must, should, could}
 Initialize Step3Output structure
 Set state: ANALYZING
Gate: Input valid → proceed; invalid → halt
Phase 2: Requirement Analysis [ANALYZING]
2.1 Categorization
 Group by category:
 FR → will become capability objectives
 NFR → will become quality objectives
 CR → will become compliance objectives
 Group by priority:
 must → will become primary objectives
 should → will become secondary objectives
 could → will become tertiary objectives
2.2 Consolidation Analysis
 Identify semantically related requirements
 For each candidate group:
 Assess: Do they define one coherent success condition?
 Assess: Would consolidation preserve specificity?
 Assess: Would consolidation preserve verifiability?
 Decision: Consolidate or keep separate
 Record consolidation decisions
 Set state: TRANSFORMING
Phase 3: Objective Transformation [TRANSFORMING]
For each requirement (or consolidated group):

3.1 Identifier Assignment
 Determine category (capability/quality/compliance)
 Assign id with category prefix (CAP-NNN, QUAL-NNN, COMP-NNN)
3.2 Statement Formulation
 Transform from obligation to achievement:
"The solution shall X" → "X is achieved successfully"
 Verify statement describes achieved state, not action
 Verify statement is specific (single interpretation)
3.3 Priority Assignment
 Map from source requirement priority:
must → primary
should → secondary
could → tertiary
 For consolidated objectives: use highest priority among sources
3.4 Success Criteria - Definition
 Ask: "What does it look like when this is achieved?"
 Write definition describing successful state
 Verify definition is observable/recognizable
3.5 Success Criteria - Verification
 Ask: "How will we determine this is achieved?"
 Select verification method:
Demonstration: Show it working
Inspection: Examine structure/content
Analysis: Reason about properties
Review: Human judgment
Test: Execute and measure
 Describe how method applies to this objective
 Verify method is feasible
3.6 Traceability Recording
 Record all source requirement ids in traces_to.requirements
 For consolidated: include all contributing requirements
Phase 4: Success Framework Construction [TRANSFORMING]
4.1 Minimum Viable
 Collect all primary objective ids
 Write description: what this level represents
 Write achievement_meaning: what achieving this means
4.2 Target
 Collect all primary + secondary objective ids
 Write description
 Write achievement_meaning
4.3 Aspirational
 Collect all objective ids
 Write description
 Write achievement_meaning
Phase 5: Coverage & Traceability [TRANSFORMING]
5.1 Build Traceability Maps
 Forward: For each objective, list source requirements
 Backward: For each requirement, identify covering objective
5.2 Build Coverage Map
 Map each requirement to its objective
 Group objectives by source priority (must/should/could)
5.3 Verify Coverage
 Every must requirement → objective exists
 Every should requirement → objective exists (flag if missing)
 Every could requirement → objective exists (note if missing)
 No orphan objectives (all trace to requirements)
Phase 6: Validation [VALIDATING]
 Set state: VALIDATING
 Execute validation rules V1-V15
 Collect errors and warnings
 If errors: remediate, return to TRANSFORMING
 If warnings only: note, proceed
 Set state: REVIEWING
Phase 7: Human Review [REVIEWING]
 Present:
 Objectives by category (capability, quality, compliance)
 Objectives by priority (primary, secondary, tertiary)
 Success framework (3 levels with descriptions)
 Coverage summary
 Any warnings from validation
 WAIT for decision
 If REJECT: note feedback, return to TRANSFORMING
 If MODIFY: apply changes, return to VALIDATING
 If APPROVE: proceed to COMPLETE
Phase 8: Finalization [COMPLETE]
 Set state: COMPLETE
 Record approval in metadata
 Compute summary statistics:
 Total objectives
 By category
 By priority
 Framework sizes
 Package Step3Output
 Emit for downstream use
Guidance Document V3
Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Detailed Procedure V2

Purpose
This document orients the agent executing Step 3 to the context, principles, and considerations for transforming requirements into objectives that define success.

Position in Workflow
Step 3 completes the definition phase:

Step	Question Answered	Output
1	What problem are we solving?	Problem Statement
2	What must the solution do?	Requirements
3	What does success look like?	Objectives
After Step 3, the problem is fully defined. The outputs of Steps 1-3 together constitute the Definition Package:

Problem Statement: The problem
Requirements: The obligations
Objectives: The success criteria
Steps 4-7 verify and validate this definition. Step 8 implements.

Instance 0 Considerations
At the universal level:

Objective categories (capability, quality, compliance) are abstract containers
Instance N may add domain-specific categories
Success criteria should be semantic, not syntactic
Verification methods are types (demonstration, inspection, etc.), not procedures
Avoid over-constraining: Objectives should describe achieved states, not prescribe how to achieve them. Success criteria should describe what success looks like, not how to get there.

Core Principles
1. From Obligation to Achievement
Requirements express obligations: "The solution shall..." Objectives express achievements: "X is achieved when..."

This is not just linguistic transformation. It's a shift in perspective:

Requirement: Constraint on the solution (what must be done)
Objective: Goal for the solution (what success looks like)
2. Success Criteria Are Essential
An objective without success criteria is just a restated requirement. The value of Step 3 is defining what success looks like and how we'll know.

Definition: Describes the achieved state. What do we observe when this objective is met?

Verification: Describes how achievement is determined. What method confirms success?

Both are required. An objective with only definition lacks actionability. An objective with only verification lacks clarity.

3. Priority Reflects Criticality
Priority	Source	Framework	Failure Consequence
Primary	must requirements	Minimum Viable	Solution fails
Secondary	should requirements	Target	Solution disappoints
Tertiary	could requirements	Aspirational	Solution is less than ideal
Do not inflate priority. Primary objectives define the floor for viability. If everything is primary, there is no meaningful floor.

4. Consolidation Is a Tool, Not a Goal
Consolidate when:

Multiple requirements define one coherent success condition
Separate objectives would be redundant
The consolidated objective remains specific and verifiable
Do not consolidate when:

Requirements address genuinely distinct success conditions
Consolidation would create compound objectives
Traceability would become obscured
Preserve all source requirement ids in consolidated objectives.

5. Verifiability Is Non-Negotiable
Every objective must be verifiable. Verification methods include:

Method	Description	When to Use
Demonstration	Show the capability working	Functional capabilities
Inspection	Examine structure or content	Compliance, documentation
Analysis	Reason about properties	Non-functional qualities
Review	Human judgment	Subjective qualities
Test	Execute and measure	Measurable properties
At Instance 0, specify the type of verification, not the procedure. Instance N may add specific procedures.

6. The Success Framework Is Meaningful
The three levels are not arbitrary groupings:

Minimum Viable: The floor. If these objectives are not achieved, the solution does not work. This is the go/no-go boundary.

Target: The expectation. If these objectives are achieved, stakeholders are satisfied. This is the success state.

Aspirational: The ceiling. If all objectives are achieved, the solution exceeds expectations. This is full success.

The framework guides prioritization during implementation and evaluation during assessment.

Transformation Patterns
Simple Transformation
Requirement: "The solution shall receive unstructured problem descriptions"
Objective: "Unstructured problem descriptions are received successfully"
Definition: "The system accepts problem descriptions in any format from human input"
Verification: "Demonstration: provide various problem descriptions and observe reception"
Quality Transformation
Requirement: "The solution shall maintain internal consistency"
Objective: "Internal consistency is maintained throughout"
Definition: "No contradictory specifications exist within the methodology"
Verification: "Analysis: examine specifications for contradictions"
Compliance Transformation
Requirement: "The solution shall ensure every output element is traceable"
Objective: "Complete traceability is achieved"
Definition: "Every output element has documented source and derivation"
Verification: "Inspection: examine traceability records for completeness"
Consolidation Example
Requirements:
  FR-009: "The solution shall define an explicit procedure with phases and transitions"
  FR-010: "The solution shall maintain and expose current state throughout execution"

Analysis: Both address procedural explicitness and state management — related but distinct.
Decision: Keep separate (distinct success conditions)

Alternative case:
  NFR-002: "The solution shall maintain internal consistency"
  NFR-003: "The solution shall avoid circular dependencies"

Analysis: Both address structural integrity of the methodology.
Decision: Could consolidate into "Structural integrity is achieved"
  Definition: "The methodology is internally consistent with no circular dependencies"
  Verification: "Analysis: examine for contradictions and dependency cycles"
  Traces to: [NFR-002, NFR-003]
Anti-Patterns
Anti-Pattern	Symptom	Remedy
Restated requirement	"Doing X is achieved" with no criteria	Add definition and verification
Vague definition	"It works well"	Make specific and observable
Missing verification	No method to determine achievement	Add feasible verification method
Orphan objective	No traces_to.requirements	Trace to source or remove
Over-consolidation	Many requirements → one vague objective	Split into coherent objectives
Under-consolidation	Redundant objectives for same success	Merge with combined traceability
Priority inflation	Everything is primary	Reserve primary for must requirements
Compound objective	"X and Y and Z are achieved"	Split unless truly one condition
Quality Checklist
Before human review, verify each objective:

Specific: Single clear interpretation
Achievable: Realistic given constraints
Relevant: Traces to requirement(s)
Verifiable: Has definition and verification method
And verify the framework:

Minimum Viable: Contains exactly primary objectives
Target: Contains exactly primary + secondary
Aspirational: Contains all objectives
Meanings: Each level has clear achievement meaning
Detailed Procedure V3
Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Guidance Document V3

State Machine
STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, TRANSFORMING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}
INITIAL = RECEIVED
TERMINAL = COMPLETE

TRANSITIONS:
  RECEIVED     → ANALYZING     : input_valid()
  ANALYZING    → TRANSFORMING  : analysis_complete()
  TRANSFORMING → VALIDATING    : transformation_complete()
  VALIDATING   → TRANSFORMING  : validation_failed()
  VALIDATING   → REVIEWING     : validation_passed()
  REVIEWING    → TRANSFORMING  : rejected(feedback)
  REVIEWING    → VALIDATING    : modify(changes)
  REVIEWING    → COMPLETE      : approved()

HUMAN_GATES = {REVIEWING}
Phase 1: Reception & Validation [RECEIVED]
Input: Step 2 Output

Procedure:

1. RECEIVE Step 2 Output

2. VALIDATE input:
   CHECK metadata.state = COMPLETE
   CHECK metadata.approval present
   CHECK requirements.length ≥ 1
   FOR EACH requirement:
     CHECK id non-empty
     CHECK statement non-empty
     CHECK category ∈ {FR, NFR, CR}
     CHECK priority ∈ {must, should, could}

3. IF any check fails:
   HALT with error: "Invalid Step 2 Output: [details]"

4. INITIALIZE Step3Output:
   metadata.instance = current_instance
   metadata.step = 3
   metadata.state = RECEIVED
   metadata.input_reference = Step2Output.id
   objectives = []
   success_framework = {}
   coverage = {}
   traceability = {}

5. TRANSITION → ANALYZING
Phase 2: Requirement Analysis [ANALYZING]
Input: Validated Step 2 Output

Procedure:

2.1 CATEGORIZE REQUIREMENTS:

    by_category = {
      FR: FILTER requirements WHERE category = FR,
      NFR: FILTER requirements WHERE category = NFR,
      CR: FILTER requirements WHERE category = CR
    }
    
    by_priority = {
      must: FILTER requirements WHERE priority = must,
      should: FILTER requirements WHERE priority = should,
      could: FILTER requirements WHERE priority = could
    }

2.2 IDENTIFY CONSOLIDATION CANDIDATES:

    consolidation_candidates = []
    processed = set()
    
    FOR i IN range(len(requirements)):
      IF requirements[i].id IN processed:
        CONTINUE
      
      group = [requirements[i]]
      
      FOR j IN range(i+1, len(requirements)):
        IF requirements[j].id IN processed:
          CONTINUE
        
        IF semantically_related(requirements[i], requirements[j]):
          group.append(requirements[j])
      
      IF len(group) > 1:
        consolidation_candidates.append(group)
        FOR req IN group:
          processed.add(req.id)

2.3 EVALUATE CONSOLIDATION:

    to_consolidate = []
    to_process_individually = []
    
    FOR group IN consolidation_candidates:
      IF should_consolidate(group):
        # Criteria: one coherent success condition, preserves specificity
        to_consolidate.append(group)
      ELSE:
        FOR req IN group:
          to_process_individually.append(req)
    
    # Add all non-candidate requirements
    FOR req IN requirements:
      IF req.id NOT IN processed:
        to_process_individually.append(req)

OUTPUT: 
  by_category, by_priority
  to_consolidate (groups)
  to_process_individually (single requirements)

TRANSITION → TRANSFORMING
Phase 3: Objective Transformation [TRANSFORMING]
Input: Categorized requirements, consolidation decisions

Procedure:

INITIALIZE counters: cap=0, qual=0, comp=0

3.1 PROCESS CONSOLIDATED GROUPS:

    FOR EACH group IN to_consolidate:
      
      # Determine category (use most common, or capability if mixed)
      categories = [req.category FOR req IN group]
      IF all same: obj_category = map_category(categories[0])
      ELSE: obj_category = capability  # default for mixed
      
      # Assign identifier
      SWITCH obj_category:
        capability: id = "CAP-" + pad(++cap, 3)
        quality: id = "QUAL-" + pad(++qual, 3)
        compliance: id = "COMP-" + pad(++comp, 3)
      
      # Formulate consolidated statement
      statement = synthesize_achievement([req.statement FOR req IN group])
      
      # Determine priority (highest among group)
      priorities = [req.priority FOR req IN group]
      IF "must" IN priorities: obj_priority = primary
      ELIF "should" IN priorities: obj_priority = secondary
      ELSE: obj_priority = tertiary
      
      # Define success criteria
      definition = synthesize_definition(group)
      verification = determine_verification_method(group)
      
      # Record traceability
      traces_to = [req.id FOR req IN group]
      
      # Create objective
      objective = {
        id, statement, 
        category: obj_category, 
        priority: obj_priority,
        success_criteria: {definition, verification},
        traces_to: {requirements: traces_to}
      }
      
      objectives.append(objective)

3.2 PROCESS INDIVIDUAL REQUIREMENTS:

    FOR EACH req IN to_process_individually:
      
      # Determine category
      obj_category = map_category(req.category)
        # FR → capability
        # NFR → quality
        # CR → compliance
      
      # Assign identifier
      SWITCH obj_category:
        capability: id = "CAP-" + pad(++cap, 3)
        quality: id = "QUAL-" + pad(++qual, 3)
        compliance: id = "COMP-" + pad(++comp, 3)
      
      # Formulate statement
      statement = transform_to_achievement(req.statement)
        # "The solution shall X" → "X is achieved successfully"
      
      # Determine priority
      obj_priority = map_priority(req.priority)
        # must → primary
        # should → secondary
        # could → tertiary
      
      # Define success criteria
      definition = formulate_definition(req)
        # "What does it look like when this is achieved?"
      
      verification = determine_verification(req)
        # Select method and describe application
      
      # Record traceability
      traces_to = [req.id]
      
      # Create objective
      objective = {
        id, statement,
        category: obj_category,
        priority: obj_priority,
        success_criteria: {definition, verification},
        traces_to: {requirements: traces_to}
      }
      
      objectives.append(objective)

OUTPUT: objectives list

CONTINUE to Phase 4
Phase 4: Success Framework Construction [TRANSFORMING continued]
Input: objectives list

Procedure:

4.1 CLASSIFY OBJECTIVES BY PRIORITY:

    primary_objectives = FILTER objectives WHERE priority = primary
    secondary_objectives = FILTER objectives WHERE priority = secondary
    tertiary_objectives = FILTER objectives WHERE priority = tertiary

4.2 BUILD MINIMUM VIABLE:

    success_framework.minimum_viable = {
      description: "Core functionality achieved; solution is viable",
      objectives: [obj.id FOR obj IN primary_objectives],
      achievement_meaning: "All must-priority requirements are satisfied. The solution works at a fundamental level. Failure to achieve any objective at this level means the solution fails."
    }

4.3 BUILD TARGET:

    target_objectives = primary_objectives + secondary_objectives
    
    success_framework.target = {
      description: "Expected success achieved; stakeholders satisfied",
      objectives: [obj.id FOR obj IN target_objectives],
      achievement_meaning: "All must and should requirements are satisfied. Stakeholders' primary needs and concerns are addressed. This is the expected success state."
    }

4.4 BUILD ASPIRATIONAL:

    success_framework.aspirational = {
      description: "Full success achieved; all enhancements included",
      objectives: [obj.id FOR obj IN objectives],
      achievement_meaning: "All requirements are satisfied, including nice-to-have enhancements. The solution exceeds expectations."
    }

OUTPUT: success_framework

CONTINUE to Phase 5
Phase 5: Coverage & Traceability [TRANSFORMING continued]
Input: objectives, success_framework, Step2Output.requirements

Procedure:

5.1 BUILD FORWARD TRACEABILITY:
    # objective → requirements
    
    FOR EACH objective IN objectives:
      traceability.forward[objective.id] = objective.traces_to.requirements

5.2 BUILD BACKWARD TRACEABILITY:
    # requirement → objective
    
    FOR EACH requirement IN Step2Output.requirements:
      FOR EACH objective IN objectives:
        IF requirement.id IN objective.traces_to.requirements:
          traceability.backward[requirement.id] = objective.id
          BREAK

5.3 BUILD COVERAGE MAP:

    FOR EACH requirement IN Step2Output.requirements:
      coverage.requirements[requirement.id] = traceability.backward[requirement.id]
    
    coverage.by_source_priority = {
      must: [],
      should: [],
      could: []
    }
    
    FOR EACH requirement IN Step2Output.requirements:
      obj_id = coverage.requirements[requirement.id]
      IF obj_id:
        coverage.by_source_priority[requirement.priority].append(obj_id)
    
    # Deduplicate
    FOR priority IN [must, should, could]:
      coverage.by_source_priority[priority] = unique(coverage.by_source_priority[priority])

5.4 VERIFY COVERAGE:

    gaps = []
    
    FOR EACH requirement IN Step2Output.requirements:
      IF coverage.requirements[requirement.id] is null:
        gaps.append({
          requirement: requirement.id,
          priority: requirement.priority
        })
    
    IF gaps with priority = must:
      ERROR: "Must requirement(s) without objectives: [ids]"
      ACTION: Create objectives for gaps
    
    IF gaps with priority = should:
      WARNING: "Should requirement(s) without objectives: [ids]"
    
    IF gaps with priority = could:
      NOTE: "Could requirement(s) without objectives: [ids]"

OUTPUT: traceability, coverage, any gaps

TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 6: Validation [VALIDATING]
Input: objectives, success_framework, coverage, traceability

Procedure:

EXECUTE validation rules:

# Structure validation
V1:  ∀ obj: obj.id ≠ null AND obj.id ≠ ""
V2:  ∀ obj: obj.statement describes achievement (not "shall")
V3:  ∀ obj: obj.category ∈ {capability, quality, compliance}
V4:  ∀ obj: obj.priority ∈ {primary, secondary, tertiary}
V5:  ∀ obj: obj.success_criteria.definition ≠ null AND ≠ ""
V6:  ∀ obj: obj.success_criteria.verification ≠ null AND ≠ ""
V7:  ∀ obj: obj.traces_to.requirements.length ≥ 1

# Coverage validation
V8:  ∀ must_req: ∃ obj IN objectives WHERE must_req.id IN obj.traces_to.requirements
V9:  success_framework.minimum_viable.objectives = 
       {obj.id FOR obj IN objectives WHERE obj.priority = primary}
V10: success_framework.target.objectives = 
       {obj.id FOR obj IN objectives WHERE obj.priority ∈ {primary, secondary}}
V11: success_framework.aspirational.objectives = 
       {obj.id FOR obj IN objectives}

# Warnings
V12: ∀ should_req: ∃ covering objective (warning if missing)
V13: ∀ could_req: ∃ covering objective (warning if missing)

# Quality checks
V14: ∀ obj: is_specific(obj.statement) (warning if fails)
V15: ∀ obj: is_achievable(obj.statement) (warning if fails)

COLLECT results:
  errors = [rule FOR rule IN V1-V11 IF failed]
  warnings = [rule FOR rule IN V12-V15 IF failed]

IF errors:
  LOG errors
  TRANSITION → TRANSFORMING (remediate)
ELSE:
  LOG warnings (if any)
  TRANSITION → REVIEWING
Phase 7: Human Review [REVIEWING]
Human Interaction: Required (gate)

Input: Validated objectives, success_framework, coverage

Procedure:

1. SET state: REVIEWING

2. PREPARE presentation:

   a. OBJECTIVES BY CATEGORY:
      Group by capability, quality, compliance
      For each: show id, statement, priority, success criteria summary
   
   b. OBJECTIVES BY PRIORITY:
      Group by primary, secondary, tertiary
      Show counts and list
   
   c. SUCCESS FRAMEWORK:
      For each level (minimum_viable, target, aspirational):
        Show description
        Show objective count
        Show achievement meaning
   
   d. COVERAGE SUMMARY:
      Requirements covered: [count] / [total]
      By source priority: must [x], should [y], could [z]
   
   e. WARNINGS (if any):
      List with explanations

3. PRESENT to human

4. REQUEST decision: APPROVE | REJECT | MODIFY

5. WAIT for human response

6. PROCESS decision:

   IF APPROVE:
     LOG approval (approver, timestamp)
     TRANSITION → COMPLETE
   
   IF REJECT:
     RECEIVE feedback
     LOG rejection with feedback
     TRANSITION → TRANSFORMING
   
   IF MODIFY:
     RECEIVE modifications
     APPLY modifications
     TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 8: Finalization [COMPLETE]
Input: Approved objectives, success_framework, coverage, traceability

Procedure:

1. SET state: COMPLETE

2. RECORD approval:
   metadata.approval.approver = [from human]
   metadata.approval.timestamp = now()

3. COMPUTE summary:
   summary.total = len(objectives)
   
   summary.by_category = {
     capability: COUNT WHERE category = capability,
     quality: COUNT WHERE category = quality,
     compliance: COUNT WHERE category = compliance
   }
   
   summary.by_priority = {
     primary: COUNT WHERE priority = primary,
     secondary: COUNT WHERE priority = secondary,
     tertiary: COUNT WHERE priority = tertiary
   }
   
   summary.framework_sizes = {
     minimum_viable: len(success_framework.minimum_viable.objectives),
     target: len(success_framework.target.objectives),
     aspirational: len(success_framework.aspirational.objectives)
   }

4. PACKAGE Step3Output:
   {
     metadata,
     objectives,
     success_framework,
     coverage,
     summary,
     traceability
   }

5. EMIT for downstream use

6. LOG completion

EXIT: Step 3 complete
     Definition phase complete (Steps 1-3)
     Ready for verification phase (Steps 4-7) or implementation (Step 8)
Summary
Instance 0 | Pass 1 | Step 3: Objectives

I have completed the V1 → V2 → V3 iteration for the four methodology documents:

Key Design Decisions for Instance 0:

Categories (capability, quality, compliance) map from requirement categories
Priority maps directly from requirement priority (must→primary, etc.)
Success criteria have two components: definition (what) and verification (how)
Verification methods are types, not procedures
Success framework has three meaningful levels with clear semantics
Consolidation is permitted but preserves all traceability

Category	capability
Priority	primary
Success Criteria

— Verification	Demonstration: Provide problem descriptions in various formats (prose, bullets, fragments) and observe successful reception
Traces To	FR-001
CAP-002: Input Analysis
Source: FR-002

Statement: Input is analyzed to identify constituent elements including stated problem, stakeholders, constraints, scope indicators, and success markers.

Attribute	Value
Category	capability
Priority	primary
Success Criteria	
— Definition	The methodology extracts and identifies all relevant elements from the input: what the problem is, who is affected, what limits exist, what boundaries apply, and what success looks like
— Verification	Inspection: Examine output to confirm constituent elements are identified and documented
Traces To	FR-002
CAP-003: Gap Identification
Source: FR-003

Statement: Gaps in input are identified where information is missing, ambiguous, or contradictory.

Attribute	Value
Category	capability
Priority	primary
Success Criteria	
— Definition	The methodology detects and flags: missing required information, statements with multiple interpretations, and conflicting statements
— Verification	Demonstration: Provide input with known gaps; observe correct identification
Traces To	FR-003

Statement: Clarification is elicited from the human when critical gaps are identified.

Attribute	Value
Category	capability
Priority	primary
Success Criteria

— Verification	Demonstration: Provide input with critical gaps; observe elicitation behavior and response integration
Traces To	FR-004

Attribute	Value
Category	capability

Success Criteria

— Verification	Inspection: Verify output contains all required elements; Demonstration: Confirm downstream step can consume output

Synthesized universal methodology's architecture and domain-agnostic foundational principles.
16s



Understanding Instance 0
What Instance 0 Is
Instance 0 is the universal, domain-agnostic methodology for structured reasoning. It is a "methodology for methodologies" — it defines HOW to perform structured reasoning without specifying WHAT domain you're reasoning about.

We have just completed the Definition Phase (Steps 1-3) of Instance 0, which now exists as:

Step	Output	Content
Step 1	Problem Statement	The core transformation problem: unstructured → structured
Step 2	Requirements	29 requirements defining what the methodology must do
Step 3	Objectives	28 objectives defining what success looks like
The Abstraction Level
Instance 0 operates in abstract problem-solving space. It contains:

Universal structures: Categories like FR/NFR/CR (requirements) and capability/quality/compliance (objectives) that apply to any domain
Universal processes: State machines, validation rules, and procedures that work regardless of subject matter
Semantic descriptions: Expressed in meaningful terms, not rigid syntax
Extension points: Clearly identified places where Instance N can specialize
Instance 0 explicitly does not contain:

Domain-specific categories (e.g., "Security Requirements," "Agency Requirements")
Domain-specific examples or patterns
Domain-specific validation rules
Rigid syntactic requirements that would limit generalizability
The Inheritance Model
Instance 0 (Universal)
    │
    │ inherits from
    ▼
Instance N (Domain-Specific)
When someone has a specific problem domain (e.g., agentic systems, software architecture, policy design), they create an Instance N that:

Inherits Instance 0's methodology wholesale
Specializes by adding domain-specific elements:
Domain-specific requirement/objective categories
Domain-specific validation criteria
Domain-specific patterns and examples
Domain-specific terminology
Does not modify Instance 0 — extension only, no alteration
This means Instance 0 is created once and serves all domains.

The Two-Pass System
For each step, we executed two passes:

Pass 1 (Autonomous): Created the methodology through V1 → V2 → V3 iteration

Produced four documents: Data Sheet, To Do List, Guidance Document, Detailed Procedure
Each iteration refined based on insights from previous documents
Ran without human gates (though human could interrupt)
Pass 2 (Gated): Executed the V3 methodology to produce actual output

Followed the Detailed Procedure
Produced the actual deliverable (Problem Statement, Requirements, or Objectives)
Required explicit human approval before proceeding
What We Built
The Instance 0 methodology now defines, at the universal level:

How to ingest a problem statement and produce a structured Problem Statement output
How to derive requirements from a Problem Statement
How to transform requirements into objectives that define success
Each of these includes:

Input/output contracts
State machines with explicit phases and transitions
Validation rules
Human oversight points
Traceability requirements
Quality criteria
The Key Insight
Instance 0 is self-describing. We used Instance 0's own methodology to define Instance 0. The problem we solved was:

"How does an agent reliably transform an unstructured problem description into a structured representation that enables systematic downstream reasoning, while remaining abstract enough to apply across any problem domain?"

The answer to that question IS Instance 0 — the methodology we just created.

What Comes Next
With Instance 0's Definition Phase complete:

Steps 4-7 (Verification, Validation, Evaluation, Assessment) could verify this definition
Step 8 (Implementation) would implement the methodology
Instance N could be created for any specific domain by inheriting Instance 0 and specializing
Instance 0 is now a reusable foundation. Anyone approaching a structured reasoning problem can inherit it rather than starting from scratch.

Instance 1 shifts the problem statement from the general and abstract, into the general and specific as it is to be implemented within a software system.  So this instantiates the general and abstract into a specific form and problem: build software that instantiates the abstract structured reasoning method defined by Instance 0, such that Instance 1 can be used to build further instances that are situated in specific domains of knowledge to address specific problems in that domain.

Understanding Instance 1
The Shift from Instance 0 to Instance 1
Aspect	Instance 0	Instance 1
Level	General and Abstract	General and Specific
Form	Methodology (conceptual)	Software (concrete)
Purpose	Defines HOW to reason structurally	Implements the reasoning methodology
Output	Reusable intellectual framework	Executable system
What Instance 1 Is
Instance 1 is the software instantiation of Instance 0's abstract structured reasoning methodology.

It answers the question:

"How do we build software that implements the abstract structured reasoning method defined by Instance 0, such that the software can be used to create further domain-specific instances?"

Instance 1 is not merely "one example of using Instance 0." It is the implementation layer — the engine that makes all subsequent instances possible.

The Instance Hierarchy (Revised Understanding)
Instance 0 (Abstract Methodology)
    │
    │ instantiated by
    ▼
Instance 1 (Software Implementation)
    │
    │ used to build
    ▼
Instance N (Domain-Specific Applications)
    ├── Instance 2: e.g., Agentic Systems
    ├── Instance 3: e.g., Policy Design
    ├── Instance 4: e.g., Software Architecture
    └── ...
Instance 0: The universal "what and how" of structured reasoning — abstract, conceptual, domain-agnostic

Instance 1: The software system that embodies Instance 0 — concrete, executable, still general-purpose

Instance N (N ≥ 2): Domain-situated applications built using Instance 1 — specific problems in specific knowledge domains

What Instance 1 Must Do
Instance 1 inherits Instance 0's methodology and specializes it for the software domain. This means:

Inheriting all 29 requirements and 28 objectives from Instance 0
Specializing them with software-specific considerations:
Data structures for Problem Statements, Requirements, Objectives
State machine implementations
Validation rule engines
Traceability systems
Human interface for oversight gates
Extension mechanisms for Instance N creation
Adding software-specific requirements:
How is state persisted?
How are documents stored and versioned?
How does the human interact with the system?
How does Instance N registration and inheritance work?
What are the APIs or interfaces?
The Generative Purpose
Instance 1 is generative — its purpose is not to solve a single problem but to enable the creation of problem-solving instances:

Instance 1 + Domain Knowledge → Instance N
A user approaches Instance 1 with:

A specific domain (e.g., legal contract analysis)
Domain-specific categories, validations, patterns
Instance 1 provides:

The structured reasoning engine
The 10-step workflow implementation
The two-pass execution model
The four-document methodology generator
The result is Instance N — a domain-situated structured reasoning capability.

My Understanding
Instance 1 is the bridge between abstraction and application. It takes the "methodology for methodologies" (Instance 0) and makes it operational as software, so that the methodology can be applied to real problems in real domains through further instantiation.

Without Instance 1, Instance 0 remains a conceptual framework. With Instance 1, the framework becomes a tool.

Proposed Revisions to Meta-Prompt
Based on implementing Instance 0 through Steps 1-3, I've identified several areas where the meta-prompt could be clarified, corrected, or enhanced.

1. MAJOR: Instance Hierarchy Revision
Current State (lines 13-35): The document describes only two levels: Instance 0 (Universal) and Instance N (Domain-Specific).

Issue Identified: Through our work, we discovered that Instance 1 has a special structural role — it is not merely "the first domain-specific instance" but rather the software implementation layer that makes all subsequent instances possible.

Proposed Revision:

│                         INSTANCE HIERARCHY                                   │

│                                                                              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Rationale: This three-tier model accurately reflects the architecture we discovered: abstraction (Instance 0) → implementation (Instance 1) → application (Instance N).

2. MODERATE: Clarify Four Document Purposes
Current State (lines 70-75): The descriptions are functional but somewhat vague.

Proposed Revision:

Document	Purpose	Contains
Data Sheet	Define the contract for the step	Input specification, output specification, validation rules, state definitions, transformation patterns
To Do List	Operationalize the contract as executable tasks	Phased checklist with validation hooks at each phase
Guidance Document	Orient the agent to context and principles	Position in workflow, core principles, anti-patterns, quality criteria
Detailed Procedure	Provide algorithmic execution instructions	State machine specification, phase-by-phase procedures, decision logic, human interaction protocol
Rationale: During implementation, each document evolved into a specific structure. Making these structures explicit helps future executions.

3. MODERATE: Elevate "Stated vs. Actual Problem" Concept
Current State (line 325): Mentioned only in Step 1 Guidance as: "Ask 'what problem does the human actually need solved?' not 'what did they ask for?'"

Proposed Revision: Add to Step 1 Output Contract explicitly:

- **Output Contract:**
  - Stated problem (verbatim from user — what they literally asked)
  - Core problem statement (1-2 sentences — what actually needs solving)
  - Differs from stated (boolean + explanation if true)
  - Stakeholders...
And add to Principles section:

8. **Stated vs. Actual** - Preserve what was asked; derive what needs solving
Rationale: This distinction proved critical in Step 1 execution and affects downstream traceability. It deserves first-class status.

4. MINOR: Fix Instance 0 Pass 2 Description
Current State (lines 221-226):

INSTANCE 0, PASS 2: Create System Prompt (optional)
  Input: V3 documents
  Process: Compress into executable prompt
  Output: System prompt for step execution
Issue: In our implementation, Instance 0 Pass 2 executed the methodology to produce actual deliverables (Problem Statement, Requirements, Objectives), not a system prompt.

Proposed Revision:

INSTANCE 0, PASS 2: Execute Methodology
  Input: V3 documents + seed problem (for Step 1) or prior step output (for Steps 2+)
  Process: Follow V3 Detailed Procedure
  Output: Step deliverable (Problem Statement, Requirements, Objectives)
  Gate: HUMAN APPROVAL REQUIRED

Note: The V3 documents themselves serve as the reusable methodology. 
A separate "system prompt" compression is optional and distinct from Pass 2.
Rationale: Pass 2 is execution, not compression. The current description conflates two different activities.

5. MINOR: Add Consolidation Guidance to Step 3
Current State (line 493): "Consolidate related requirements into single objectives when appropriate"

Proposed Addition to Step 3 Guidance:

### Consolidation Criteria

Consolidate when:
- Multiple requirements define ONE coherent success condition
- Separate objectives would be redundant
- The consolidated objective remains specific and verifiable

Do NOT consolidate when:
- Requirements address genuinely distinct success conditions
- Consolidation would create compound objectives
- Traceability would become obscured

Always preserve all source requirement IDs in consolidated objectives.
Rationale: Implementation revealed this decision point needed more guidance.

6. MINOR: Adjust Objective Count Guidance
Current State (line 416): "Objective hierarchy (primary 3-5, secondary 5-10, tertiary rest)"

Issue: Instance 0 produced 23 primary objectives and 5 secondary objectives. The guidance seems more applicable to domain-specific instances.

Proposed Revision:

- Objective hierarchy (primary, secondary, tertiary)
  - Counts vary by instance abstraction level
  - Instance 0: May have many primary objectives (defining core methodology)
  - Instance N: Typically 3-5 primary, 5-10 secondary, rest tertiary
Rationale: The original guidance is reasonable for domain instances but misleading for Instance 0.
