Rationale: Reflects the three-tier architecture discovered during implementation.

8. MINOR: Clarify "Considering" in V1→V2→V3
Current State: The iteration pattern says documents are created "considering" other documents but doesn't explain what this means operationally.

Proposed Addition after line 176:

### What "Considering" Means

When a document is created "considering" other documents:
1. READ the referenced documents
2. IDENTIFY insights relevant to the document being created
3. INCORPORATE those insights:
   - Data Sheet: What inputs/outputs did the procedure reveal we need?
   - To Do List: What tasks did the procedure identify?
   - Guidance: What principles emerged from the procedure?
   - Detailed Procedure: How do all documents inform the algorithm?
4. MAINTAIN CONSISTENCY with referenced documents
5. IMPROVE based on gaps or issues revealed
Rationale: "Considering" is a key operation but was underspecified.

9. EDITORIAL: Section Reorganization for Domain Specialization
Current State (lines 508-530): Examples are given for "Agentic Systems" as if it were Instance N directly inheriting from Instance 0.

Proposed Revision: Reorganize to show:

Instance 1 specialization (software concerns: persistence, APIs, state management, user interface)
Instance N specialization (domain concerns: domain categories, domain validation, domain patterns)
Rationale: With the three-tier model, the Domain Specialization section needs to distinguish between Instance 1 (software) and Instance N (domain) specializations.

Analysis of Colleague's Feedback
I'll assess each recommendation against our Instance 0 implementation experience.

Recommendations to IMPLEMENT
#2: Resolve "4 Documents" vs "extra schema documents" inconsistency
Merit: HIGH

This is a genuine inconsistency. The meta-prompt declares exactly four document types, but then Steps 2 and 3 present schemas as if they're additional deliverables. During our execution, we embedded schemas within Data Sheets, but the document structure is confusing.

Recommendation: Implement. Add clarifying statement that schemas, validation checklists, and quality criteria are subsections of the Data Sheet.

#3: Remove "..." placeholders in V3 documents
Merit: MODERATE-HIGH

Valid executability concern. Ellipses allow handwaving and undermine the contract. A V3 document should be complete and executable.

Recommendation: Implement. Add rule prohibiting ellipses in V3; require explicit assumptions or open questions instead.

#4: Tighten and standardize clarification gates
Merit: HIGH

During execution, we applied implicit policies about when to elicit clarification. Making these explicit would improve consistency. Their suggested framework (trigger rules, max question count, fallback policy) is sound.

Recommendation: Implement. Add Clarification Policy guidance, either globally with per-step overrides or within each step's Data Sheet.

#7: Strengthen traceability conventions and ID standards
Merit: HIGH

We used semantic prefixes throughout (FR-, NFR-, CR- for requirements; CAP-, QUAL-, COMP- for objectives) and they significantly aided traceability. The meta-prompt should codify this.

Recommendation: Implement. Add Naming & ID Conventions section.

Note: The colleague suggests OBJ-### for all objectives. In our execution, we used category prefixes (CAP-, QUAL-, COMP-). I'd recommend the category-prefix approach as it adds semantic information, but either is valid as long as it's consistent.

#9: Complete the 10-step workflow section (as stubs)
Merit: MODERATE

The document claims "10-step workflow" but only specifies Steps 1-3. The current document acknowledges this ("This prompt covers Steps 1-3"), but the colleague is right that stub sections would be cleaner.

Recommendation: Partial implementation. Add explicit stub sections for Steps 4-10 with Purpose statements and a note that full specification is pending. This maintains structural integrity without over-promising.

Recommendations to DEFER or PARTIALLY ACCEPT
#5: Replace "WAIT FOR HUMAN APPROVAL" language

Recommendation: Minor refinement at most. Current language is adequate.

#6: Add explicit "two-layer output" (human + machine-readable YAML)
Merit: CONTEXT-DEPENDENT

This is a valuable pattern, but it's a software implementation concern, not an abstract methodology concern. At Instance 0, we produced structured outputs that were readable and traceable without strict YAML schemas. Mandating dual-layer output at Instance 0 would over-constrain and conflate abstraction levels.

Recommendation: Defer to Instance 1. The software implementation should define output formats. The abstract methodology should allow flexibility.

Alternative: Add as a "should" rather than "must" — encourage structured output without mandating specific format.

#8: Align Step 3 with "Must coverage" rule for constraint-only items
Merit: MODERATE but COMPLEX

The colleague suggests that some Must constraints (e.g., "no design leakage") are better enforced as acceptance gates in Step 4 than as standalone objectives. This is a nuanced point.

During our execution, we created compliance objectives (COMP-) for every constraint requirement, and it worked fine. Their suggestion adds flexibility but also:

Adds dependency on Step 4 (not yet specified)
Complicates the simple "every Must → Primary objective" rule
Requires tracking which constraints are deferred to gates
Recommendation: Defer. The current approach (every Must requirement → objective) is simpler, worked in practice, and maintains clean separation between Steps 3 and 4. Revisit when specifying Step 4.

Feedback #2: Formalizing the "Clarification Loop"

Recommendation: Minor refinement. The current structure is functionally correct, but we could add emphasis in the Detailed Procedure description:

Current:

- States: RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE
Refined:

- States: RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE
- ANALYZING includes mandatory Sufficiency Check; if gaps found → ELICITING
- ELICITING returns to ANALYZING for re-check (loop until sufficient)
This is a minor clarification, not a structural change.

Steps 2+ Seed: Inherited V3 docs + This instance's previous step output

### Detailed Procedure
- State machine with phases matching To Do List
- States: RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE
- **Mandatory Sufficiency Check:** ANALYZING includes Sufficiency Check before exit
  - If gaps found → transition to ELICITING
  - ELICITING returns to ANALYZING for re-check (loop until sufficient)
  - Only exit ANALYZING when sufficiency threshold met OR maximum elicitation rounds reached
...

[Sufficiency Check]            │
│                        │
┌────────┴────────┐               │
▼                 ▼               │
(sufficient)    (gaps found)         │
│                 │               │
▼                 ▼               │

V1: INITIAL CREATION
Data Sheet V1
Step Purpose: Transform the user's software architecture specification into a structured Problem Statement that defines what software system must be built to implement the Instance 0 structured reasoning methodology.

traceability	Links to source document	1

Technology stack extraction and validation
Integration point identification
Non-functional requirement seeds (performance, scalability, reliability)
Deployment context

Guidance Document V1
Considering: User Architecture Spec + Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1

Context
Instance 1 transforms Instance 0's abstract methodology into a software system. Step 1 must define:

What software is being built
What technical constraints govern the build
Who will use and operate the software
What success looks like for the software
The user has provided a Technical Architecture Specification that serves as the problem prompt. This document specifies technologies, patterns, and integration points.

Software-Specific Considerations
Technology Stack Is Given: Unlike abstract Instance 0, the user has specified technologies:
Frontend: Next.js 14+ / Vercel AI SDK / assistant-ui
API: FastAPI (Python 3.11+)
Orchestration: LangGraph 1.0+
Persistence: PostgreSQL + pgvector
Observability: LangSmith or Langfuse
LLM: Claude API
Architecture Patterns Are Given: The specification defines:
Instance hierarchy (Instance 0 → Instance N)
Two-pass execution model
Ten-step workflow
Four document types
State model
Stakeholders Are Implicit: Must be derived from the specification:
End users (who use the frontend)
Human reviewers (who approve at gates)
System operators (who deploy and maintain)
Downstream instances (Instance N that inherit from Instance 0)
Constraints vs. Requirements: The specification blends constraints (must use PostgreSQL) with requirements (must support checkpointing). Separate these.
Principles
Treat the architecture spec as authoritative for technology choices
Distinguish between what the spec says (stated) and what must actually be built (core problem)
Identify gaps in the spec that need resolution
Preserve traceability to specific sections of the spec

IF validation fails:
  TRANSITION → STRUCTURING (remediate)
ELSE:
  TRANSITION → REVIEWING

Phase 7: Finalization [COMPLETE]

### Validation Rules

| ID | Rule | Severity |
|----|------|----------|
| V1 | Core problem ≤ 2 sentences | Error |
| V2 | Stakeholders count ∈ [3, 6] | Error |
| V3 | Hard constraints count ∈ [3, 6] | Error |
| V4 | Technology stack complete (all layers) | Error |
| V5 | Scope has ≥1 IN and ≥1 OUT items | Error |
| V6 | Every output element has traceability | Error |
| V7 | All stakeholders have type | Error |
| V8 | All constraints have category | Error |
| V9 | Integration points identified | Warning |
| V10 | Success criteria have verification method | Warning |

### State Definitions

| State | Description | Human Interaction |
|-------|-------------|-------------------|
| RECEIVED | Architecture spec received | None |
| ANALYZING | Parsing and extracting | None |
| ELICITING | Awaiting clarification | Required |
| STRUCTURING | Building problem statement | None |
| VALIDATING | Checking completeness | None |
| REVIEWING | Awaiting approval | Required (gate) |
| COMPLETE | Approved and packaged | None |

## Guidance Document V2
*Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Detailed Procedure V1*

### Position in Workflow

Instance 1 Step 1 is the first step of the software implementation instance:

| Instance | Step | Purpose |
|----------|------|---------|
| Instance 0 | Step 1 | Define abstract methodology for problem definition |
| **Instance 1** | **Step 1** | **Define what software must be built** |
| Instance N | Step 1 | Define specific domain problem |

Instance 1 inherits Instance 0's methodology but applies it to a concrete software implementation problem.

### Inheritance from Instance 0

Instance 0 Step 1 established:
- The structure of a Problem Statement (core problem, stakeholders, constraints, scope, criteria)
- The process for elicitation (sufficiency check, loop behavior)
- The traceability requirements (every output traces to input)
- The validation rules (cardinality, completeness)

Instance 1 **inherits** these and **specializes** them:
- Adds technology_stack as first-class output
- Adds integration_points as first-class output
- Adds stakeholder types (user, operator, system, developer)
- Adds constraint categories (technology, architecture, behavior, integration)

### The Architecture Specification as Problem Prompt

The user has provided `2_structured-reasoning-architecture_V1.md` — a technical architecture specification. This is NOT a vague problem description; it is a detailed technical document that:

1. **Specifies technology choices** — These become hard constraints
2. **Defines architectural patterns** — These become architectural constraints
3. **Describes behaviors** — These become behavioral constraints
4. **Identifies integration points** — These become integration constraints
5. **Implies stakeholders** — These must be derived

### Stated vs. Core Problem

The architecture spec's stated purpose (Section 1) is:
> "This specification defines a structured reasoning process for producing high-quality solutions through systematic methodology development and execution."

The **core problem** for Instance 1 is different:
> "Build a software system that implements the structured reasoning methodology, using the specified technology stack, such that users can execute the 10-step workflow with human oversight at defined gates."

The stated problem describes the methodology; the core problem describes the software implementation.

### Stakeholder Derivation

The spec does not explicitly name stakeholders. Derive them from context:

| Stakeholder | Source | Type |
|-------------|--------|------|
| End User | "Frontend Layer" — someone uses the UI | user |
| Human Reviewer | "Human approval required" — someone approves | user |
| System Operator | "PostgreSQL", "Observability" — someone deploys/monitors | operator |
| Instance N Developer | "Instance N inherits" — someone creates domain instances | developer |
| The Software System | Executes methodology — is itself an agent | system |

### Constraint Extraction

The spec blends requirements with constraints. Separate them:

**Hard Constraints (non-negotiable):**
- Technology stack (explicitly specified)
- Human approval gates (explicitly required)
- LangGraph checkpointing (explicitly required)
- PostgreSQL persistence (explicitly specified)
- Traceability (design principle)

**Soft Constraints (preferences):**
- Observability choice (LangSmith OR Langfuse)
- Frontend framework details (version flexibility)

### Scope Boundaries

**IN Scope (what the software does):**
- Implements 10-step workflow
- Supports two-pass execution
- Provides human oversight gates
- Persists state to PostgreSQL
- Enables LangGraph orchestration
- Exposes FastAPI endpoints
- Renders Next.js frontend

**OUT Scope (what the software does NOT do):**
- Define domain-specific methodologies (that's Instance N)
- Provide the LLM (uses Claude API externally)
- Define the abstract methodology (that's Instance 0)

### Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Symptom | Remedy |
|--------------|---------|--------|
| Spec as requirements | Treating every spec line as a requirement | Spec defines problem; Step 2 derives requirements |
| Missing stakeholders | Only listing obvious users | Derive from all integration points and roles |
| Technology as requirement | "Must use PostgreSQL" as requirement | PostgreSQL is a constraint; requirements say WHAT, not HOW |
| Over-scoping | Including Instance N concerns | Instance 1 builds the engine; Instance N uses it |

---

## Detailed Procedure V2
*Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Guidance Document V2*

### State Machine
```
STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}
INITIAL = RECEIVED
TERMINAL = COMPLETE

TRANSITIONS:
  RECEIVED     → ANALYZING     : spec_received()
  ANALYZING    → ELICITING     : gaps_found()
  ANALYZING    → STRUCTURING   : sufficient()
  ELICITING    → ANALYZING     : responses_received()
  STRUCTURING  → VALIDATING    : structure_complete()
  VALIDATING   → STRUCTURING   : validation_failed()
  VALIDATING   → REVIEWING     : validation_passed()
  REVIEWING    → STRUCTURING   : rejected(feedback)
  REVIEWING    → VALIDATING    : modify(changes)
  REVIEWING    → COMPLETE      : approved()

HUMAN_GATES = {ELICITING, REVIEWING}
```

### Phase 1: Reception [RECEIVED]
```
INPUT: Architecture specification document

1.1 ACKNOWLEDGE receipt
1.2 VERIFY document parseable
1.3 IDENTIFY sections:
    - Overview
    - Core Architecture
    - Document System
    - State Model
    - Human Interaction
    - Traceability
    - Persistence
    - Execution Protocol
    - Integration Points
    - Constraints

1.4 LOAD inherited methodology:
    - Instance 0 Step 1 V3 Data Sheet
    - Instance 0 Step 1 V3 To Do List
    - Instance 0 Step 1 V3 Guidance
    - Instance 0 Step 1 V3 Detailed Procedure
    - Instance 0 Step 1 Output (reference)

1.5 INITIALIZE Instance1Step1Output structure

1.6 SET state: ANALYZING

TRANSITION → ANALYZING
```

### Phase 2: Analysis [ANALYZING]
```
INPUT: Parsed specification, inherited methodology

2.1 EXTRACT TECHNOLOGY STACK:

    technology_stack = {
      frontend: {
        framework: "Next.js",
        version: "14+",
        libraries: ["Vercel AI SDK", "assistant-ui"]
      },
      api: {
        framework: "FastAPI",
        language: "Python",
        version: "3.11+",
        protocols: ["SSE Streaming", "REST"]
      },
      orchestration: {
        framework: "LangGraph",
        version: "1.0+",
        features: ["State Machine", "Interrupt Gates", "Checkpointing"]
      },
      persistence: {
        database: "PostgreSQL",
        extensions: ["pgvector"],
        purposes: ["Checkpoints", "Conversations", "Audit Logs", "Embeddings"]
      },
      observability: {
        options: ["LangSmith", "Langfuse"],
        purpose: "Tracing, Debugging, Cost Tracking"
      },
      llm: {
        provider: "Claude API",
        purpose: "Agent reasoning and generation"
      }
    }

2.2 EXTRACT ARCHITECTURE ELEMENTS:

    architecture = {
      instance_hierarchy: "Instance 0 (Universal) → Instance N (Domain)",
      execution_model: "Two-Pass (Definition, Execution)",
      workflow: "10 Steps",
      documents: "4 Types per Step",
      state_model: ["WorkflowState", "StepState"],
      transitions: [defined in spec],
      human_interaction: ["Pass 1 interrupt", "Pass 2 gate", "Elicitation", "Escalation"]
    }

2.3 GAP ANALYSIS:

    FOR EACH expected element:
      IF missing OR ambiguous OR contradictory:
        ADD to gaps list
        CLASSIFY criticality

    gaps = [
      # Example gaps to check:
      # - Deployment model (cloud? on-prem?)
      # - Authentication/authorization
      # - Multi-tenancy
      # - Performance requirements
      # - Scalability requirements
    ]

2.4 SUFFICIENCY CHECK:

    critical_gaps = FILTER gaps WHERE criticality = "critical"
    
    IF critical_gaps.length > 0:
      TRANSITION → ELICITING
    ELSE:
      TRANSITION → STRUCTURING
```

### Phase 3: Elicitation [ELICITING]
```
INPUT: Critical gaps

3.1 FORMULATE questions (≤5):

    FOR EACH critical_gap:
      question = {
        text: formulate_question(gap),
        context: why_this_matters(gap),
        impact: what_assumption_if_unresolved(gap)
      }

3.2 PRESENT to human:

    """
    CLARIFICATION NEEDED

    To proceed with Instance 1 Problem Definition, I need clarification:

    1. [Question]
       Context: [Why]
       Impact if unresolved: [Assumption]
    
    ...
    """

3.3 WAIT for responses

3.4 INCORPORATE responses:
    
    FOR EACH response:
      UPDATE relevant extracted elements
      MARK gap as resolved

3.5 TRANSITION → ANALYZING (re-check sufficiency)
```

### Phase 4: Structuring [STRUCTURING]
```
INPUT: Extracted elements, resolved gaps

4.1 STATED PROBLEM:

    stated_problem = {
      verbatim: "This specification defines a structured reasoning process 
                 for producing high-quality solutions through systematic 
                 methodology development and execution.",
      source_section: "Section 1: Overview"
    }

4.2 CORE PROBLEM:

    core_problem = {
      statement: "[Formulate 1-2 sentence statement of what software to build]",
      differs_from_stated: true,
      difference_explanation: "Stated describes methodology; core describes 
                               software implementation of that methodology"
    }

4.3 STAKEHOLDERS:

    stakeholders = []
    counter = 0
    
    FOR EACH identified stakeholder:
      counter++
      stakeholder = {
        id: "SH-" + pad(counter, 3),
        name: stakeholder.name,
        type: stakeholder.type,  # user | operator | system | developer
        primary_need: derived_need,
        primary_concern: derived_concern,
        source: derivation_type,
        source_section: section_reference
      }
      stakeholders.append(stakeholder)
    
    VALIDATE: 3 ≤ len(stakeholders) ≤ 6

4.4 HARD CONSTRAINTS:

    hard_constraints = []
    counter = 0
    
    FOR EACH non-negotiable limit:
      counter++
      constraint = {
        id: "HC-" + pad(counter, 3),
        statement: constraint_text,
        category: classify(technology | architecture | behavior | integration),
        violation_consequence: what_fails_if_violated,
        source: derivation_type,
        source_section: section_reference
      }
      hard_constraints.append(constraint)
    
    VALIDATE: 3 ≤ len(hard_constraints) ≤ 6

4.5 SOFT CONSTRAINTS:

    soft_constraints = []
    counter = 0
    
    FOR EACH preference:
      counter++
      constraint = {
        id: "SC-" + pad(counter, 3),
        statement: constraint_text,
        category: classify(technology | performance | preference),
        trade_off: what_trade_off_exists,
        source: derivation_type,
        source_section: section_reference | null
      }
      soft_constraints.append(constraint)

4.6 SCOPE:

    scope = {
      in_scope: [
        {item: "...", rationale: "..."},
        ...
      ],
      out_of_scope: [
        {item: "...", rationale: "..."},
        ...
      ]
    }
    
    VALIDATE: len(in_scope) ≥ 1 AND len(out_of_scope) ≥ 1

4.7 SUCCESS CRITERIA:

    success_criteria = []
    counter = 0
    
    FOR EACH criterion:
      counter++
      criterion = {
        id: "SC-" + pad(counter, 3),
        criterion: criterion_text,
        verification_method: how_to_verify,
        source: section_reference
      }
      success_criteria.append(criterion)

4.8 INTEGRATION POINTS:

    integration_points = []
    counter = 0
    
    FOR EACH external interface:
      counter++
      point = {
        id: "IP-" + pad(counter, 3),
        name: interface_name,
        type: classify(inbound | outbound | bidirectional),
        protocol: protocol_used,
        description: purpose
      }
      integration_points.append(point)

4.9 TRACEABILITY:

    traceability = []
    
    FOR EACH output_element:
      trace = {
        output_element: element_id,
        source_section: section_reference,
        derivation: classify(quoted | paraphrased | inferred)
      }
      traceability.append(trace)
    
    VALIDATE: no orphan outputs

TRANSITION → VALIDATING
```

### Phase 5: Validation [VALIDATING]
```
INPUT: Structured problem statement

EXECUTE validation rules:

  V1: len(core_problem.statement.split('. ')) ≤ 2
  V2: 3 ≤ len(stakeholders) ≤ 6
  V3: 3 ≤ len(hard_constraints) ≤ 6
  V4: technology_stack has all layers (frontend, api, orchestration, persistence, observability, llm)
  V5: len(scope.in_scope) ≥ 1 AND len(scope.out_of_scope) ≥ 1
  V6: ∀ output_element: ∃ traceability entry
  V7: ∀ stakeholder: stakeholder.type ≠ null
  V8: ∀ constraint: constraint.category ≠ null
  V9: len(integration_points) ≥ 1 (warning if fails)
  V10: ∀ criterion: criterion.verification_method ≠ null (warning if fails)

COLLECT results:
  errors = [rule FOR rule IN V1-V8 IF failed]
  warnings = [rule FOR rule IN V9-V10 IF failed]

IF errors:
  LOG errors
  TRANSITION → STRUCTURING (remediate)
ELSE:
  LOG warnings (if any)
  TRANSITION → REVIEWING
```

### Phase 6: Human Review [REVIEWING]
```
INPUT: Validated problem statement

6.1 PREPARE presentation:

    Present:
    - Core problem statement
    - Differs from stated? + explanation
    - Stakeholders (table: id, name, type, need, concern)
    - Hard constraints (table: id, statement, category)
    - Soft constraints (table: id, statement, trade-off)
    - Technology stack (structured summary)
    - Scope (in/out lists)
    - Success criteria (with verification methods)
    - Integration points (table)
    - Traceability summary
    - Warnings (if any)

6.2 REQUEST decision: APPROVE | REJECT | MODIFY

6.3 WAIT for human response

6.4 PROCESS decision:

    IF APPROVE:
      TRANSITION → COMPLETE
    
    IF REJECT:
      RECEIVE feedback
      TRANSITION → STRUCTURING
    
    IF MODIFY:
      RECEIVE modifications
      APPLY modifications
      TRANSITION → VALIDATING
```

### Phase 7: Finalization [COMPLETE]
```
INPUT: Approved problem statement

7.1 SET state: COMPLETE

7.2 RECORD approval:
    metadata.approval.approver = human
    metadata.approval.timestamp = now()

7.3 PACKAGE Instance1Step1Output

7.4 EMIT for Step 2

EXIT: Instance 1 Step 1 complete
V3: FINAL REFINEMENT
Data Sheet V3
Considering: Detailed Procedure V2 + Guidance Document V2 + To Do List V2

Input Specification
Required Inputs:

Input	Source	Description	Validation
Instance 0 Step 1 V3 Methodology	Inherited	Abstract methodology documents	4 documents present
Instance 0 Step 1 Output	Inherited	Reference problem statement	State = COMPLETE, approved
User Architecture Spec	User	Technical architecture document	Document parseable, sections identifiable
Conditional Inputs:

Input	Source	Condition	Description
Clarifying Responses	User	If elicitation occurred	Answers to clarifying questions
Output Specification
yaml
Instance1Step1Output:
  metadata:
    instance: 1
    level: implementation
    step: 1
    step_name: "Problem Definition"
    state: COMPLETE
    timestamp: datetime
    input_references:
      instance_0_methodology: reference
      instance_0_output: reference
      architecture_spec: reference
    approval:
      approver: string
      timestamp: datetime
      decision: APPROVE
  
  stated_problem:
    verbatim: string
    source_section: string
  
  core_problem:
    statement: string                    # ≤2 sentences
    differs_from_stated: boolean
    difference_explanation: string | null
  
  stakeholders:                          # 3-6 entries
    - id: SH-NNN
      name: string
      type: user | operator | system | developer
      description: string
      primary_need: string
      primary_concern: string
      source:
        derivation: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
        section: string | null
        basis: string                    # If inferred, explain basis
  
  hard_constraints:                      # 3-6 entries
    - id: HC-NNN
      statement: string
      category: technology | architecture | behavior | integration
      violation_consequence: string
      source:
        derivation: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
        section: string
  
  soft_constraints:                      # 0+ entries
    - id: SC-NNN
      statement: string
      category: technology | performance | preference
      trade_off: string
      source:
        derivation: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
        section: string | null
  
  technology_stack:
    frontend:
      framework: string
      version_requirement: string
      libraries:
        - name: string
          purpose: string
    api:
      framework: string
      language: string
      version_requirement: string
      protocols:
        - name: string
          purpose: string
    orchestration:
      framework: string
      version_requirement: string
      features:
        - name: string
          purpose: string
    persistence:
      database: string
      extensions:
        - name: string
          purpose: string
      storage_purposes:
        - name: string
          description: string
    observability:
      options:
        - name: string
      purpose: string
      selection_constraint: string       # e.g., "one of" or "all"
    llm:
      provider: string
      purpose: string
  
  scope:
    in_scope:
      - id: IN-NNN
        item: string
        rationale: string
        source_section: string | null
    out_of_scope:
      - id: OUT-NNN
        item: string
        rationale: string
        source_section: string | null
  
  success_criteria:
    - id: CRT-NNN
      criterion: string
      verification_method: demonstration | inspection | analysis | review | test
      verification_description: string
      source_section: string | null
  
  integration_points:
    - id: IP-NNN
      name: string
      from_component: string
      to_component: string
      direction: inbound | outbound | bidirectional
      protocol: string
      data_exchanged: string
      source_section: string
  
  traceability:
    forward:                             # output → source
      stakeholders:
        - id: SH-NNN
          source_section: string
          derivation: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
      hard_constraints:
        - id: HC-NNN
          source_section: string
          derivation: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
      soft_constraints:
        - id: SC-NNN
          source_section: string
          derivation: quoted | paraphrased | inferred
    coverage:
      spec_sections_referenced: [string]
      spec_sections_not_referenced: [string]
  
  assumptions:                           # If any were made
    - id: ASM-NNN
      assumption: string
      rationale: string
      risk_if_wrong: string
  
  open_questions:                        # If any remain
    - id: OQ-NNN
      question: string
      impact: string
      resolution_path: string
```

### Validation Rules

| ID | Rule | Severity | Remediation |
|----|------|----------|-------------|
| V1 | core_problem.statement ≤ 2 sentences | Error | Condense statement |
| V2 | 3 ≤ stakeholders.count ≤ 6 | Error | Add/remove stakeholders |
| V3 | 3 ≤ hard_constraints.count ≤ 6 | Error | Add/remove constraints |
| V4 | technology_stack complete (all 6 layers) | Error | Fill missing layers |
| V5 | scope.in_scope.count ≥ 1 | Error | Add in-scope items |
| V6 | scope.out_of_scope.count ≥ 1 | Error | Add out-of-scope items |
| V7 | ∀ stakeholder: type ∈ {user, operator, system, developer} | Error | Assign valid type |
| V8 | ∀ hard_constraint: category ∈ {technology, architecture, behavior, integration} | Error | Assign valid category |
| V9 | ∀ output element: traceability entry exists | Error | Add traceability |
| V10 | integration_points.count ≥ 1 | Warning | Review for missing interfaces |
| V11 | ∀ success_criterion: verification_method ≠ null | Warning | Add verification method |
| V12 | core_problem.differs_from_stated → difference_explanation ≠ null | Error | Add explanation |
| V13 | ∀ inferred element: source.basis ≠ null | Error | Explain inference basis |

### State Definitions

| State | Entry Condition | Exit Condition | Human Interaction |
|-------|-----------------|----------------|-------------------|
| RECEIVED | Architecture spec provided | Spec parsed successfully | None |
| ANALYZING | Spec parsed | Sufficiency determined | None |
| ELICITING | Critical gaps found | Responses received | Required |
| STRUCTURING | Sufficient OR responses received | Structure complete | None |
| VALIDATING | Structure complete | Validation passed OR failed | None |
| REVIEWING | Validation passed | Human decision received | Required (gate) |
| COMPLETE | Approved | Terminal | None |

### State Transition Diagram
```
RECEIVED → ANALYZING ←──────────────────┐
               │                        │
               ▼                        │
         [Sufficiency Check]            │
               │                        │
      ┌────────┴────────┐               │
      ▼                 ▼               │
  (sufficient)    (gaps found)         │
      │                 │               │
      │                 ▼               │
      │            ELICITING ───────────┘
      │
      ▼
STRUCTURING ←────────────────┐
      │                      │
      ▼                      │
VALIDATING                   │
      │                      │
      ├──── (fail) ──────────┘
      │
      ▼ (pass)
REVIEWING ←──────────────────┐
      │                      │
      ├──── (reject) ────────┤
      │                      │
      ├──── (modify) ► VALIDATING
      │
      ▼ (approve)
COMPLETE
```

## Guidance Document V3
*Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Detailed Procedure V2*

### Purpose

This document orients the agent executing Instance 1 Step 1 to the context, principles, and considerations for defining the software implementation problem.

### Position in Workflow

Instance 1 is the software implementation of Instance 0's abstract methodology:

| Instance | Level | Step 1 Purpose |
|----------|-------|----------------|
| Instance 0 | Abstract | Define methodology for problem definition |
| **Instance 1** | **Implementation** | **Define what software system to build** |
| Instance N (N≥2) | Application | Define domain-specific problem |

Instance 1 Step 1 produces a Problem Statement that describes what software must be built. This feeds into Instance 1 Step 2 (Requirements) which will define what the software must do.

### Inheritance from Instance 0

Instance 1 inherits Instance 0's Step 1 methodology:

| Inherited | How Used |
|-----------|----------|
| Problem Statement structure | Extended with software-specific fields |
| Stakeholder schema | Extended with type classification |
| Constraint schema | Extended with category classification |
| Traceability requirements | Applied to architecture spec |
| Validation rules | Extended with software-specific rules |
| State machine | Applied unchanged |
| Sufficiency check / Elicitation loop | Applied unchanged |

### Specialization for Software Implementation

Instance 1 adds software-specific elements not present in Instance 0:

| Element | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| technology_stack | Captures specified technologies |
| integration_points | Captures system interfaces |
| stakeholder.type | Classifies stakeholder role |
| constraint.category | Classifies constraint type |
| scope with IDs | Enables traceability |
| assumptions | Documents decisions made with incomplete info |
| open_questions | Documents unresolved issues |

### The Architecture Specification

The user's architecture specification (`2_structured-reasoning-architecture_V1.md`) is a detailed technical document, not a vague problem prompt. It contains:

1. **Explicit technology choices** — The spec TELLS us what to use
2. **Architectural patterns** — The spec DEFINES the structure
3. **Behavioral requirements** — The spec DESCRIBES what happens
4. **Integration topology** — The spec SHOWS how components connect
5. **Constraints** — The spec LIMITS what is acceptable

This is different from Instance 0, where we defined the methodology ourselves. Here, the methodology is given; we extract and structure it.

### Stated vs. Core Problem

**Stated Problem** (from spec):
> "This specification defines a structured reasoning process for producing high-quality solutions through systematic methodology development and execution."

This describes the METHODOLOGY, not the SOFTWARE.

**Core Problem** (for Instance 1):
> "Build a software system that implements the structured reasoning methodology using Next.js, FastAPI, LangGraph, and PostgreSQL, enabling users to execute the 10-step workflow with human oversight at defined gates."

This describes the SOFTWARE to be built.

The difference is significant: Instance 1 is not defining a methodology; it is implementing one as software.

### Stakeholder Derivation

The spec implies stakeholders through its descriptions:

| Spec Reference | Implies Stakeholder |
|----------------|---------------------|
| "Frontend Layer" — "User interface" | End User who uses the UI |
| "Human approval required" | Human Reviewer who approves |
| "PostgreSQL", "Observability" | System Operator who deploys/monitors |
| "Instance N inherits" | Developer who creates domain instances |
| "Agent recommends" | The Software System executing methodology |

All stakeholders are INFERRED, not quoted. Each must have documented basis.

### Constraint Classification

**Hard Constraints** (technology, architecture, behavior, integration):

| Category | Example | Source |
|----------|---------|--------|
| Technology | "Must use PostgreSQL + pgvector" | Section 1.2, 7.1 |
| Architecture | "Two-pass execution" | Section 2.2 |
| Behavior | "Pass 2 must not advance without human approval" | Section 10.1 |
| Integration | "Must use Claude API for LLM" | Section 9.1 |

**Soft Constraints** (technology, performance, preference):

| Category | Example | Source |
|----------|---------|--------|
| Technology | "LangSmith or Langfuse" (choice) | Section 1.2 |

### Scope Discipline

**In Scope — What Instance 1 software does:**
- Implements the 10-step workflow engine
- Provides the two-pass execution mechanism
- Enforces human oversight gates
- Persists state to PostgreSQL
- Orchestrates via LangGraph
- Exposes FastAPI API
- Renders Next.js frontend
- Manages documents (4 types, V1→V2→V3)

**Out of Scope — What Instance 1 software does NOT do:**
- Define domain-specific methodologies (Instance N does this)
- Provide the LLM (Claude API is external)
- Define the abstract methodology (Instance 0 did this)
- Provision infrastructure (deployment is separate)

### Success Criteria

Success criteria must be verifiable. For software:

| Criterion | Verification |
|-----------|--------------|
| "All 10 steps executable" | Demonstration: Run all steps |
| "Pass 2 gates block" | Test: Attempt advance without approval |
| "State recoverable" | Test: Crash and restore |
| "Traceability maintained" | Inspection: Query trace chain |

### Integration Points

The spec defines a layered architecture with clear interfaces:
```
Frontend (Next.js)
    ↕ REST/SSE
API (FastAPI)
    ↕ Invoke/Interrupt
Orchestration (LangGraph)
    ↕ Save/Restore         ↕ API Call
Persistence (PostgreSQL)    LLM (Claude)
```

Each arrow is an integration point to be identified and documented.

### Assumptions and Open Questions

Some information is not in the spec. Document these as:

**Assumptions** — Decisions made with incomplete information:
- State rationale
- State risk if wrong
- Example: "ASSUMPTION: Single-tenant deployment. Rationale: Spec does not mention multi-tenancy. Risk: If multi-tenant required, data isolation changes."

**Open Questions** — Unresolved issues:
- State impact
- State resolution path
- Example: "OPEN QUESTION: Which observability tool? Impact: Affects integration code. Resolution: Defer to implementation decision."

### Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Symptom | Remedy |
|--------------|---------|--------|
| Spec as Requirements | Listing every spec sentence as requirement | Spec defines problem; Step 2 derives requirements |
| Inventing Stakeholders | Adding stakeholders not implied by spec | Every stakeholder must trace to spec content |
| Over-constraining | Making preferences into hard constraints | Only non-negotiable items are hard constraints |
| Under-scoping | Omitting OUT items | Explicitly state what is NOT being built |
| Missing Traceability | Outputs without source | Every output traces to spec section |

### Quality Checklist

Before presenting for human review:

1. [ ] Core problem is 1-2 sentences describing SOFTWARE to build
2. [ ] Core problem differs from stated (methodology vs. implementation)
3. [ ] 3-6 stakeholders, each with type and traced source
4. [ ] 3-6 hard constraints, each with category and traced source
5. [ ] Technology stack complete (all 6 layers)
6. [ ] Scope has IN items (what we build) and OUT items (what we don't)
7. [ ] Success criteria have verification methods
8. [ ] Integration points identified from architecture
9. [ ] All outputs traceable to spec sections
10. [ ] Assumptions documented with rationale and risk
11. [ ] Open questions documented with impact and resolution path

---

## Detailed Procedure V3
*Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Guidance Document V3*

### State Machine
```
STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}
INITIAL = RECEIVED
TERMINAL = COMPLETE

TRANSITIONS:
  RECEIVED     → ANALYZING     : inputs_valid()
  ANALYZING    → ELICITING     : critical_gaps_found()
  ANALYZING    → STRUCTURING   : sufficient()
  ELICITING    → ANALYZING     : responses_received()
  STRUCTURING  → VALIDATING    : structure_complete()
  VALIDATING   → STRUCTURING   : validation_failed()
  VALIDATING   → REVIEWING     : validation_passed()
  REVIEWING    → STRUCTURING   : rejected(feedback)
  REVIEWING    → VALIDATING    : modify(changes)
  REVIEWING    → COMPLETE      : approved()

HUMAN_GATES = {ELICITING, REVIEWING}
```

---

### Phase 1: Reception [RECEIVED]

**Entry:** Architecture spec provided

**Procedure:**
```
1.1 RECEIVE architecture specification document
    VERIFY: Document is present and parseable

1.2 IDENTIFY document sections:
    expected_sections = [
      "1. Overview",
      "2. Core Architecture", 
      "3. Document System",
      "4. State Model",
      "5. Human Interaction",
      "6. Traceability",
      "7. Persistence",
      "8. Execution Protocol",
      "9. Integration Points",
      "10. Constraints"
    ]
    
    FOR EACH section IN expected_sections:
      VERIFY section present
      IF missing: LOG warning

1.3 LOAD inherited methodology:
    instance_0_methodology = {
      data_sheet: load("Instance 0 Step 1 V3 Data Sheet"),
      to_do_list: load("Instance 0 Step 1 V3 To Do List"),
      guidance: load("Instance 0 Step 1 V3 Guidance"),
      procedure: load("Instance 0 Step 1 V3 Detailed Procedure")
    }
    instance_0_output = load("Instance 0 Step 1 Output")
    
    VERIFY: All 5 artifacts loaded

1.4 INITIALIZE output structure:
    Instance1Step1Output = {
      metadata: {
        instance: 1,
        level: "implementation",
        step: 1,
        step_name: "Problem Definition",
        state: "RECEIVED",
        timestamp: now(),
        input_references: {
          instance_0_methodology: reference,
          instance_0_output: reference,
          architecture_spec: reference
        }
      },
      # ... rest initialized empty
    }

1.5 SET state: ANALYZING

TRANSITION → ANALYZING
```

---

### Phase 2: Analysis [ANALYZING]

**Entry:** Inputs validated

**Procedure:**
```
2.1 EXTRACT TECHNOLOGY STACK:

    technology_stack = {
      frontend: {
        framework: "Next.js",
        version_requirement: "14+",
        libraries: [
          {name: "Vercel AI SDK", purpose: "AI interaction"},
          {name: "assistant-ui", purpose: "Chat interface"}
        ]
      },
      api: {
        framework: "FastAPI",
        language: "Python",
        version_requirement: "3.11+",
        protocols: [
          {name: "REST", purpose: "Request/response"},
          {name: "SSE Streaming", purpose: "Real-time updates"}
        ]
      },
      orchestration: {
        framework: "LangGraph",
        version_requirement: "1.0+",
        features: [
          {name: "State Machine", purpose: "Step sequencing"},
          {name: "Interrupt Gates", purpose: "Human checkpoints"},
          {name: "Checkpointing", purpose: "State recovery"}
        ]
      },
      persistence: {
        database: "PostgreSQL",
        extensions: [
          {name: "pgvector", purpose: "Embedding storage"}
        ],
        storage_purposes: [
          {name: "Checkpoints", description: "LangGraph state"},
          {name: "Conversations", description: "Message history"},
          {name: "Audit Logs", description: "State transitions"},
          {name: "Embeddings", description: "Semantic search"}
        ]
      },
      observability: {
        options: [
          {name: "LangSmith"},
          {name: "Langfuse"}
        ],
        purpose: "Tracing, Debugging, Cost Tracking",
        selection_constraint: "one of"
      },
      llm: {
        provider: "Claude API",
        purpose: "Agent reasoning and generation"
      }
    }

2.2 EXTRACT ARCHITECTURE ELEMENTS:

    architecture = {
      instance_hierarchy: EXTRACT from Section 2.1,
      execution_model: EXTRACT from Section 2.2,
      workflow_steps: EXTRACT from Section 2.3,
      document_types: EXTRACT from Section 3.1,
      document_iteration: EXTRACT from Section 3.2,
      workflow_state: EXTRACT from Section 4.1,
      step_state: EXTRACT from Section 4.2,
      state_transitions: EXTRACT from Section 4.3,
      interaction_points: EXTRACT from Section 5.1,
      human_actions: EXTRACT from Section 5.2,
      traceability_chain: EXTRACT from Section 6.1,
      storage_requirements: EXTRACT from Section 7.1,
      recovery_guarantees: EXTRACT from Section 7.2,
      execution_protocol: EXTRACT from Section 8,
      integration_layers: EXTRACT from Section 9.1,
      event_flow: EXTRACT from Section 9.2,
      constraints: EXTRACT from Section 10
    }

2.3 GAP ANALYSIS:

    gaps = []
    
    # Check for common missing elements
    check_items = [
      ("Deployment model", "How is the system deployed?"),
      ("Authentication", "How are users authenticated?"),
      ("Authorization", "How are permissions managed?"),
      ("Multi-tenancy", "Is data isolated per tenant?"),
      ("Performance requirements", "What are latency/throughput targets?"),
      ("Scalability requirements", "How does system scale?"),
      ("Error handling", "How are errors surfaced to users?"),
      ("Backup/recovery", "Beyond checkpoints, how is data backed up?")
    ]
    
    FOR EACH (item, question) IN check_items:
      IF NOT found_in_spec(item):
        gap = {
          item: item,
          question: question,
          criticality: assess_criticality(item)
        }
        gaps.append(gap)

2.4 SUFFICIENCY CHECK:

    critical_gaps = [g FOR g IN gaps IF g.criticality == "critical"]
    
    # For Instance 1, we need to define the SOFTWARE problem.
    # Critical = cannot define core problem or key constraints
    
    can_define_core_problem = TRUE  # Spec clearly describes what to build
    can_identify_stakeholders = TRUE  # Can derive from spec
    can_extract_constraints = TRUE  # Explicitly stated
    
    IF critical_gaps.length > 0 AND NOT (can_define_core_problem AND can_identify_stakeholders AND can_extract_constraints):
      TRANSITION → ELICITING
    ELSE:
      # Note non-critical gaps as assumptions
      FOR EACH gap IN gaps:
        IF gap.criticality != "critical":
          ADD to assumptions
      TRANSITION → STRUCTURING
```

---

### Phase 3: Elicitation [ELICITING]

**Entry:** Critical gaps found

**Human Interaction:** Required

**Procedure:**
```
3.1 FORMULATE questions:

    questions = []
    FOR EACH gap IN critical_gaps:
      question = {
        number: len(questions) + 1,
        text: formulate_question(gap),
        context: gap.question,
        impact_if_unresolved: "Will assume: " + default_assumption(gap)
      }
      questions.append(question)
      
      IF len(questions) >= 5:
        BREAK  # Max 5 questions

3.2 PRESENT to human:

    """
    CLARIFICATION NEEDED
    
    To proceed with Instance 1 Problem Definition, I need clarification:
    
    1. [Question text]
       Context: [Why this matters]
       Impact if unresolved: [What I will assume]
    
    2. ...
    
    Please provide answers, or indicate if I should proceed with stated assumptions.
    """

3.3 WAIT for human response

3.4 PROCESS responses:

    FOR EACH response:
      IF response provides answer:
        UPDATE relevant element
        MARK gap as resolved
      ELSE IF response says "proceed with assumption":
        ADD to assumptions with human acknowledgment

3.5 TRANSITION → ANALYZING (re-run sufficiency check)
```

---

### Phase 4: Structuring [STRUCTURING]

**Entry:** Sufficient information available

**Procedure:**
```
4.1 STATED PROBLEM:

    stated_problem = {

                 for producing high-quality solutions through systematic

4.2 CORE PROBLEM:

    core_problem = {
      statement: "Build a software system that implements the structured 
                  reasoning methodology using Next.js, FastAPI, LangGraph,

                  workflow with human oversight at defined gates.",
      differs_from_stated: TRUE,
      difference_explanation: "The stated problem describes the methodology 
                               being implemented. The core problem describes 
                               the software system that implements it."
    }

4.3 STAKEHOLDERS:

    stakeholders = [
      {
        id: "SH-001",

Step Purpose: Transform the Instance 1 Problem Statement into specific, testable software requirements that define what the structured reasoning system must do.

Input Specification:

Input	Source	Description
Instance 0 Step 2 V3 Docs	Inherited	Abstract methodology for requirements derivation
Instance 1 Step 1 Output	Prior Step	Software problem statement with stakeholders, constraints, scope
Architecture Specification	User	Technical reference for validation
Output Contract:

Field	Description	Cardinality
Functional Requirements (FR)	What the software must do	10-30
Non-Functional Requirements (NFR)	How well the software must perform	5-15
Constraint Requirements (CR)	Limits the software must respect	3-10
Interface Requirements (IR)	How components must interact	5-15
Traceability	Links to stakeholders, constraints, scope	All
Priority	must/should/could for each requirement	All
Coverage Map	Every stakeholder need → ≥1 requirement	1
Software-Specific Additions:

Interface Requirements (IR) as distinct category
Component allocation for each requirement
Testability criteria specific to software
Technology constraint validation
To Do List V1
Considering: Data Sheet V1

Reception: Verify Instance 1 Step 1 Output complete and approved
Input Validation: Confirm all required fields present
Source Analysis: Analyze each source type for requirement seeds:
Core problem → functional requirements
Stakeholder needs → functional/non-functional requirements
Stakeholder concerns → non-functional requirements
Hard constraints → constraint requirements
Scope IN items → functional requirements
Integration points → interface requirements
Success criteria → acceptance requirements
Requirement Derivation: Transform seeds into formal requirements
Categorization: Assign to FR/NFR/CR/IR categories
Component Allocation: Map requirements to technology components
Prioritization: Assign must/should/could based on source
Coverage Verification: Every stakeholder → ≥1 requirement
Consistency Check: No contradictions, no duplicates
Testability Check: Each requirement has verification approach
Validation: Check completeness, consistency, testability
Human Review: Present for approval
Finalization: Package output for Step 3
Guidance Document V1
Considering: Instance 1 Step 1 Output + Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1

Context
Instance 1 Step 2 derives software requirements from the Problem Statement. The Problem Statement defined:

Core problem: Build a software system implementing structured reasoning
5 stakeholders with needs and concerns
6 hard constraints (technology, architecture, behavior, integration)
1 soft constraint (observability choice)
10 in-scope items
7 success criteria
6 integration points
Software-Specific Considerations
Requirements Categories for Software:
FR (Functional): What the system does (features, capabilities)
NFR (Non-Functional): How well it does it (performance, reliability, usability)
CR (Constraint): Limits it must respect (technology, architecture)
IR (Interface): How components interact (APIs, protocols, data formats)
Component Allocation: Each requirement should map to one or more technology components:
Frontend (Next.js)
API (FastAPI)
Orchestration (LangGraph)
Persistence (PostgreSQL)
LLM Integration (Claude API)
Observability (LangSmith/Langfuse)
Derivation Sources:
Stakeholder needs → primarily FR
Stakeholder concerns → primarily NFR
Hard constraints → CR
Integration points → IR
Success criteria → acceptance criteria for requirements
Principles
Requirements state WHAT, not HOW (implementation is Step 8)
Every requirement must be testable
Every requirement must trace to a source
No orphan requirements (all derive from Problem Statement)
No orphan sources (all stakeholders, constraints addressed)
Detailed Procedure V1
Considering: Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1 + Guidance Document V1

Phase 1: Reception [RECEIVED]
INPUT: Instance 1 Step 1 Output

ACTION:
  - Verify state = COMPLETE
  - Verify approval present
  - Load all fields

OUTPUT: Validated input
TRANSITION → ANALYZING
Phase 2: Source Analysis [ANALYZING]
INPUT: Problem Statement

2.1 EXTRACT requirement seeds from each source:

    FROM core_problem:
      - "implement structured reasoning methodology" → FR
      - "execute 10-step workflow" → FR
      - "human oversight at gates" → FR
    
    FROM stakeholders:
      FOR EACH stakeholder:
        need → FR seed
        concern → NFR seed
    
    FROM hard_constraints:
      FOR EACH constraint:
        → CR seed
    
    FROM scope.in_scope:
      FOR EACH item:
        → FR seed
    
    FROM integration_points:
      FOR EACH point:
        → IR seed
    
    FROM success_criteria:
      FOR EACH criterion:
        → Acceptance criteria (attach to relevant requirement)

OUTPUT: Requirement seeds with sources
TRANSITION → DERIVING
Phase 3: Requirement Derivation [DERIVING]
INPUT: Requirement seeds

FOR EACH seed:
  requirement = {
    id: assign_id(category),
    statement: "The system shall...",
    category: FR | NFR | CR | IR,
    priority: derive_priority(source),
    source: {
      type: stakeholder | constraint | scope | integration | criterion,
      id: source_id,
      aspect: need | concern | item | point,
      derivation: how_derived
    },
    component: [affected_components],
    rationale: why_needed,
    testability: how_to_verify
  }

OUTPUT: Draft requirements
TRANSITION → VALIDATING
Phase 4: Validation [VALIDATING]
INPUT: Draft requirements

VALIDATE:
  - Every stakeholder → ≥1 requirement
  - Every hard constraint → ≥1 CR
  - Every in-scope item → ≥1 FR
  - Every integration point → ≥1 IR
  - No contradictions
  - No duplicates
  - All testable

IF validation fails:
  TRANSITION → DERIVING (remediate)
ELSE:
  TRANSITION → REVIEWING
Phase 5: Human Review [REVIEWING]
INPUT: Validated requirements

ACTION:
  - Present requirements by category
  - Present traceability
  - Present coverage map
  - Request approval

WAIT for decision

IF APPROVE: TRANSITION → COMPLETE
IF REJECT: TRANSITION → DERIVING with feedback
Phase 6: Finalization [COMPLETE]
INPUT: Approved requirements

ACTION:
  - Package output
  - Record approval
  - Emit for Step 3
V2: FIRST REFINEMENT
Data Sheet V2
Considering: Detailed Procedure V1 + Guidance Document V1 + To Do List V1

Refinements:

Procedure revealed need for explicit derivation patterns per source type
Guidance emphasized component allocation
Need clearer priority mapping from source types

Input	Source	Required	Validation

Instance 1 Step 1 Output	Prior Step	Yes	State = COMPLETE, approved
Architecture Specification	User	Reference	For validation
Input Validation Gate:

Instance 1 Step 1 state = COMPLETE
Instance 1 Step 1 approval present
Required fields: core_problem, stakeholders, hard_constraints, scope, success_criteria, integration_points
Output Specification
yaml
Instance1Step2Output:
  metadata:
    instance: 1
    level: implementation
    step: 2
    step_name: "Requirements"
    state: COMPLETE
    timestamp: datetime
    input_references:
      instance_0_methodology: reference
      instance_1_step_1: reference
    approval:
      approver: string
      timestamp: datetime
      decision: APPROVE
  
  requirements:
    functional:  # FR-NNN
      - id: FR-NNN
        statement: string        # "The system shall..."
        category: functional
        priority: must | should | could
        source:
          type: problem | stakeholder | scope | criterion
          id: string
          aspect: string
          derivation: string
        component: [string]      # Affected technology components
        rationale: string
        testability:
          method: demonstration | test | inspection | analysis
          description: string
        acceptance_criteria: [string]  # From success criteria if applicable
    
    non_functional:  # NFR-NNN
      - id: NFR-NNN
        statement: string
        category: non_functional
        subcategory: performance | reliability | usability | maintainability | security | observability
        priority: must | should | could
        source:
          type: stakeholder | constraint
          id: string
          aspect: concern | constraint
          derivation: string
        component: [string]
        rationale: string
        testability:
          method: test | analysis | inspection
          description: string
        metric: string | null    # Quantitative if applicable
    
    constraint:  # CR-NNN
      - id: CR-NNN
        statement: string
        category: constraint
        subcategory: technology | architecture | behavior | integration
        priority: must           # Constraints are always must
        source:
          type: constraint
          id: string             # HC-NNN reference
          derivation: string
        component: [string]
        rationale: string
        testability:
          method: inspection | analysis
          description: string
        violation_consequence: string
    
    interface:  # IR-NNN
      - id: IR-NNN
        statement: string
        category: interface
        priority: must | should
        source:
          type: integration
          id: string             # IP-NNN reference
          derivation: string
        from_component: string
        to_component: string
        protocol: string
        data_format: string | null
        rationale: string
        testability:
          method: test | demonstration
          description: string
  
  traceability:
    forward:  # requirement → source
      - requirement_id: string
        source_type: string
        source_id: string
        derivation: string
    
    backward:  # source → requirements
      stakeholder_coverage:
        - stakeholder_id: string
          requirements: [string]
          need_addressed: boolean
          concern_addressed: boolean
      constraint_coverage:
        - constraint_id: string
          requirements: [string]
      scope_coverage:
        - scope_id: string
          requirements: [string]
      integration_coverage:
        - integration_id: string
          requirements: [string]
  
  coverage_summary:
    stakeholders:
      total: number
      with_requirements: number
      coverage_percentage: number
    hard_constraints:
      total: number
      with_requirements: number
      coverage_percentage: number
    scope_items:
      total: number
      with_requirements: number
      coverage_percentage: number
    integration_points:
      total: number
      with_requirements: number
      coverage_percentage: number
  
  summary:
    total_requirements: number
    by_category:
      functional: number
      non_functional: number
      constraint: number
      interface: number
    by_priority:
      must: number
      should: number
      could: number
```

### Validation Rules

| ID | Rule | Severity |
|----|------|----------|
| V1 | Every stakeholder → ≥1 requirement addressing need | Error |
| V2 | Every stakeholder → ≥1 requirement addressing concern | Warning |
| V3 | Every hard constraint (HC-NNN) → ≥1 CR | Error |
| V4 | Every in-scope item (IN-NNN) → ≥1 FR | Error |
| V5 | Every integration point (IP-NNN) → ≥1 IR | Error |
| V6 | All requirements have testability defined | Error |
| V7 | All requirements have component allocation | Error |
| V8 | No duplicate requirement statements | Error |
| V9 | No contradictory requirements | Error |
| V10 | All constraint requirements have priority = must | Error |
| V11 | All requirements have rationale | Warning |
| V12 | Coverage percentage ≥ 100% for all sources | Error |

### Priority Derivation Rules

| Source Type | Source Aspect | Default Priority |
|-------------|---------------|------------------|
| Stakeholder | need | should |
| Stakeholder | concern | should |
| Hard Constraint | — | must |
| Soft Constraint | — | could |
| Scope (in_scope) | — | should |
| Integration Point | — | must |
| Success Criterion | — | must |

### State Definitions

| State | Description | Human Interaction |
|-------|-------------|-------------------|
| RECEIVED | Step 1 output received | None |
| ANALYZING | Extracting requirement seeds | None |
| DERIVING | Transforming seeds to requirements | None |
| VALIDATING | Checking coverage and consistency | None |
| REVIEWING | Awaiting approval | Required (gate) |
| COMPLETE | Approved and packaged | None |

## Guidance Document V2
*Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Detailed Procedure V1*

### Position in Workflow

Instance 1 Step 2 transforms the Problem Statement into Requirements:

| Step | Input | Output | Purpose |
|------|-------|--------|---------|
| Step 1 | Architecture Spec | Problem Statement | Define what to build |
| **Step 2** | **Problem Statement** | **Requirements** | **Define what it must do** |
| Step 3 | Requirements | Objectives | Define what success looks like |

### Inheritance from Instance 0

Instance 0 Step 2 established:
- Requirement schema (id, statement, category, priority, source, rationale, testability)
- Derivation process (source analysis → seed extraction → requirement formulation)
- Coverage requirements (every stakeholder, every constraint)
- Validation rules

Instance 1 **specializes** these:
- Adds Interface Requirements (IR) category
- Adds component allocation
- Adds software-specific subcategories
- Adds acceptance criteria linkage

### Requirement Categories for Software

| Category | Prefix | Description | Derived From |
|----------|--------|-------------|--------------|
| Functional | FR | What the system does | Core problem, stakeholder needs, scope items |
| Non-Functional | NFR | Quality attributes | Stakeholder concerns, soft constraints |
| Constraint | CR | Limits that must be respected | Hard constraints |
| Interface | IR | Component interactions | Integration points |

### Derivation Patterns

**Stakeholder Need → Functional Requirement:**
```
SH-001 Need: "Execute workflows through intuitive interface"
↓
FR-001: "The system shall provide a web-based interface for executing structured reasoning workflows"
```

**Stakeholder Concern → Non-Functional Requirement:**
```
SH-001 Concern: "System responsiveness"
↓
NFR-001: "The system shall display agent responses within 500ms of generation"
```

**Hard Constraint → Constraint Requirement:**
```
HC-001: "Must use specified technology stack"
↓
CR-001: "The system shall be implemented using Next.js 14+ for the frontend layer"
```

**Integration Point → Interface Requirement:**
```
IP-001: "Frontend → API (REST)"
↓
IR-001: "The frontend shall communicate with the API via REST endpoints for workflow commands"
```

### Component Allocation

Each requirement should identify affected components:

| Component | Technology | Typical Requirements |
|-----------|------------|---------------------|
| Frontend | Next.js + assistant-ui | UI, streaming display, user input |
| API | FastAPI | Endpoints, request handling, SSE |
| Orchestration | LangGraph | State machine, steps, interrupts |
| Persistence | PostgreSQL | Storage, checkpoints, queries |
| LLM | Claude API | Agent reasoning, generation |
| Observability | LangSmith/Langfuse | Tracing, debugging |

### Priority Guidelines

| Priority | Meaning | When to Assign |
|----------|---------|----------------|
| must | System fails without it | Hard constraints, critical functionality, gates |
| should | System disappoints without it | Core features, stakeholder needs |
| could | Nice to have | Enhancements, soft constraints |

### Testability Requirements

Every requirement must specify:
- **Method:** demonstration, test, inspection, or analysis
- **Description:** How to verify the requirement is satisfied

Software-specific verification methods:
- **Unit test:** Individual component verification
- **Integration test:** Component interaction verification
- **End-to-end test:** Full workflow verification
- **Performance test:** NFR metric verification
- **Manual inspection:** Code or configuration review

### Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Symptom | Remedy |
|--------------|---------|--------|
| Implementation in requirements | "Use React hooks for state" | Requirements say WHAT, not HOW |
| Untestable requirements | "System shall be user-friendly" | Add measurable criteria |
| Missing traceability | Requirement without source | Every requirement traces to Step 1 |
| Over-specification | 100+ requirements for simple system | Consolidate related requirements |
| Under-specification | 5 requirements for complex system | Ensure coverage of all sources |

---

## Detailed Procedure V2
*Considering: Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Guidance Document V2*

### State Machine
```
STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, DERIVING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING, COMPLETE}
INITIAL = RECEIVED
TERMINAL = COMPLETE

TRANSITIONS:
  RECEIVED    → ANALYZING   : input_valid()
  ANALYZING   → DERIVING    : seeds_extracted()
  DERIVING    → VALIDATING  : requirements_complete()
  VALIDATING  → DERIVING    : validation_failed()
  VALIDATING  → REVIEWING   : validation_passed()
  REVIEWING   → DERIVING    : rejected(feedback)
  REVIEWING   → VALIDATING  : modify(changes)
  REVIEWING   → COMPLETE    : approved()

HUMAN_GATES = {REVIEWING}
```

### Phase 1: Reception [RECEIVED]
```
INPUT: Instance 1 Step 1 Output

1.1 VERIFY input:
    ASSERT step_1_output.state == COMPLETE
    ASSERT step_1_output.approval.decision == APPROVE

1.2 LOAD required fields:
    core_problem = step_1_output.core_problem
    stakeholders = step_1_output.stakeholders  # 5 items
    hard_constraints = step_1_output.hard_constraints  # 6 items
    soft_constraints = step_1_output.soft_constraints  # 1 item
    scope_in = step_1_output.scope.in_scope  # 10 items
    scope_out = step_1_output.scope.out_of_scope  # 5 items
    success_criteria = step_1_output.success_criteria  # 7 items
    integration_points = step_1_output.integration_points  # 6 items
    technology_stack = step_1_output.technology_stack

1.3 INITIALIZE output structure

1.4 SET state: ANALYZING

TRANSITION → ANALYZING
```

### Phase 2: Seed Extraction [ANALYZING]
```
INPUT: Loaded Step 1 fields

2.1 EXTRACT from core_problem:
    seeds.append({
      source: "core_problem",
      text: core_problem.statement,
      category: "FR",
      seeds: [
        "implement structured reasoning methodology",
        "10-step workflow execution",
        "human oversight at gates"
      ]
    })

2.2 EXTRACT from stakeholders:
    FOR EACH stakeholder IN stakeholders:
      seeds.append({
        source: "stakeholder",
        source_id: stakeholder.id,
        need: stakeholder.primary_need,
        concern: stakeholder.primary_concern,
        need_category: "FR",
        concern_category: "NFR"
      })

2.3 EXTRACT from hard_constraints:
    FOR EACH constraint IN hard_constraints:
      seeds.append({
        source: "constraint",
        source_id: constraint.id,
        statement: constraint.statement,
        category: constraint.category,
        requirement_category: "CR",
        priority: "must",
        violation_consequence: constraint.violation_consequence
      })

2.4 EXTRACT from soft_constraints:
    FOR EACH constraint IN soft_constraints:
      seeds.append({
        source: "soft_constraint",
        source_id: constraint.id,
        statement: constraint.statement,
        requirement_category: "NFR",
        priority: "could"
      })

2.5 EXTRACT from scope_in:
    FOR EACH item IN scope_in:
      seeds.append({
        source: "scope",
        source_id: item.id,
        item: item.item,
        requirement_category: "FR",
        priority: "should"
      })

2.6 EXTRACT from integration_points:
    FOR EACH point IN integration_points:
      seeds.append({
        source: "integration",
        source_id: point.id,
        name: point.name,
        from: point.from_component,
        to: point.to_component,
        protocol: point.protocol,
        requirement_category: "IR",
        priority: "must"
      })

2.7 EXTRACT from success_criteria:
    FOR EACH criterion IN success_criteria:
      seeds.append({
        source: "criterion",
        source_id: criterion.id,
        criterion: criterion.criterion,
        verification: criterion.verification_method,
        use_as: "acceptance_criteria"
      })

TRANSITION → DERIVING
```

### Phase 3: Requirement Derivation [DERIVING]
```
INPUT: Extracted seeds

3.1 DERIVE Functional Requirements:
    
    fr_counter = 0
    
    # From core problem
    FOR EACH core_seed IN seeds WHERE source == "core_problem":
      FOR EACH capability IN core_seed.seeds:
        fr_counter++
        FR.append(derive_fr(capability, core_seed, fr_counter))
    
    # From stakeholder needs
    FOR EACH stake_seed IN seeds WHERE source == "stakeholder":
      fr_counter++
      FR.append(derive_fr_from_need(stake_seed, fr_counter))
    
    # From scope items
    FOR EACH scope_seed IN seeds WHERE source == "scope":
      fr_counter++
      FR.append(derive_fr_from_scope(scope_seed, fr_counter))

3.2 DERIVE Non-Functional Requirements:
    
    nfr_counter = 0
    
    # From stakeholder concerns
    FOR EACH stake_seed IN seeds WHERE source == "stakeholder":
      nfr_counter++
      NFR.append(derive_nfr_from_concern(stake_seed, nfr_counter))
    
    # From soft constraints
    FOR EACH soft_seed IN seeds WHERE source == "soft_constraint":
      nfr_counter++
      NFR.append(derive_nfr_from_soft(soft_seed, nfr_counter))

3.3 DERIVE Constraint Requirements:
    
    cr_counter = 0
    
    FOR EACH constraint_seed IN seeds WHERE source == "constraint":
      cr_counter++
      CR.append(derive_cr(constraint_seed, cr_counter))

3.4 DERIVE Interface Requirements:
    
    ir_counter = 0
    
    FOR EACH int_seed IN seeds WHERE source == "integration":
      ir_counter++
      IR.append(derive_ir(int_seed, ir_counter))

3.5 ATTACH acceptance criteria:
    
    FOR EACH criterion_seed IN seeds WHERE use_as == "acceptance_criteria":
      matching_fr = FIND FR WHERE relates_to(criterion_seed)
      IF matching_fr:
        matching_fr.acceptance_criteria.append(criterion_seed.criterion)

3.6 ASSIGN components:
    
    FOR EACH requirement IN (FR + NFR + CR + IR):
      requirement.component = determine_components(requirement, technology_stack)

TRANSITION → VALIDATING
```

### Phase 4: Validation [VALIDATING]
```
INPUT: Derived requirements (FR, NFR, CR, IR)

4.1 BUILD traceability:
    
    FOR EACH requirement:
      forward_trace.append({
        requirement_id: requirement.id,
        source_type: requirement.source.type,
        source_id: requirement.source.id
      })

4.2 BUILD coverage map:
    
    FOR EACH stakeholder:
      coverage = {
        stakeholder_id: stakeholder.id,
        requirements: FIND requirements WHERE source.id == stakeholder.id,
        need_addressed: ANY requirement addresses need,
        concern_addressed: ANY requirement addresses concern
      }
      stakeholder_coverage.append(coverage)
    
    # Similar for constraints, scope, integration

4.3 EXECUTE validation rules:
    
    V1: ∀ stakeholder: ∃ requirement addressing need
    V2: ∀ stakeholder: ∃ requirement addressing concern (warning)
    V3: ∀ hard_constraint: ∃ CR
    V4: ∀ scope_in_item: ∃ FR
    V5: ∀ integration_point: ∃ IR
    V6: ∀ requirement: testability ≠ null
    V7: ∀ requirement: component ≠ empty
    V8: No duplicate statements
    V9: No contradictions
    V10: ∀ CR: priority == must
    V11: ∀ requirement: rationale ≠ null (warning)
    V12: All coverage percentages == 100%

4.4 CALCULATE summary:
    
    summary = {
      total: len(FR) + len(NFR) + len(CR) + len(IR),
      by_category: {FR: len(FR), NFR: len(NFR), CR: len(CR), IR: len(IR)},
      by_priority: {must: count, should: count, could: count}
    }

4.5 COLLECT results:
    
    IF errors:
      LOG errors
      TRANSITION → DERIVING
    ELSE:
      LOG warnings
      TRANSITION → REVIEWING
```

### Phase 5: Human Review [REVIEWING]
```
INPUT: Validated requirements

5.1 PRESENT:
    - Requirements by category (FR, NFR, CR, IR)
    - Traceability matrix
    - Coverage summary
    - Validation results
    - Warnings (if any)

5.2 REQUEST decision: APPROVE | REJECT | MODIFY

5.3 WAIT for human response

5.4 PROCESS decision:
    
    IF APPROVE:
      TRANSITION → COMPLETE
    IF REJECT:
      RECEIVE feedback
      TRANSITION → DERIVING
    IF MODIFY:
      APPLY modifications
      TRANSITION → VALIDATING
```

### Phase 6: Finalization [COMPLETE]
```
INPUT: Approved requirements

6.1 SET state: COMPLETE
6.2 RECORD approval
6.3 PACKAGE Instance1Step2Output
6.4 EMIT for Step 3
V3: FINAL REFINEMENT
Data Sheet V3
Considering: Detailed Procedure V2 + Guidance Document V2 + To Do List V2

Input Specification
Required Inputs:

Input	Source	Description	Validation
Instance 0 Step 2 V3 Methodology	Inherited	Abstract methodology	4 documents present
Instance 1 Step 1 Output	Prior Step	Problem Statement	State=COMPLETE, approved
Input Field Requirements:

Field	Type	Count	Required For
core_problem	object	1	FR derivation
stakeholders	array	5	FR/NFR derivation
hard_constraints	array	6	CR derivation
soft_constraints	array	1	NFR derivation
scope.in_scope	array	10	FR derivation
success_criteria	array	7	Acceptance criteria
integration_points	array	6	IR derivation
technology_stack	object	1	Component allocation
Output Specification
yaml
Instance1Step2Output:
  metadata:

        statement: string

        priority: must | should | could
        source:
          type: stakeholder | constraint
          id: string
          aspect: concern | constraint
          derivation: string
        component: [string]
        rationale: string
        testability:

    constraint:
      - id: CR-NNN

        source:

```

### Validation Rules

| ID | Rule | Severity | Remediation |
|----|------|----------|-------------|
| V1 | ∀ stakeholder: ≥1 FR addressing need | Error | Add FR for unaddressed need |
| V2 | ∀ stakeholder: ≥1 NFR addressing concern | Warning | Consider adding NFR |
| V3 | ∀ HC: ≥1 CR | Error | Add CR for unaddressed constraint |
| V4 | ∀ IN-scope: ≥1 FR | Error | Add FR for unaddressed scope item |
| V5 | ∀ IP: ≥1 IR | Error | Add IR for unaddressed integration |
| V6 | ∀ requirement: testability.method ≠ null | Error | Define test method |
| V7 | ∀ requirement: component ≠ [] | Error | Assign component |
| V8 | No duplicate requirement statements | Error | Merge or differentiate |
| V9 | No contradictory requirements | Error | Resolve contradiction |
| V10 | ∀ CR: priority = must | Error | Set CR priority to must |
| V11 | ∀ requirement: rationale ≠ null | Warning | Add rationale |
| V12 | ∀ CRT: attached to ≥1 FR | Warning | Attach criteria |
| V13 | Total requirements ≥ 20 | Warning | May be under-specified |
| V14 | Total requirements ≤ 60 | Warning | May be over-specified |

### State Definitions

| State | Entry | Exit | Human |
|-------|-------|------|-------|
| RECEIVED | Step 1 output provided | Input validated | None |
| ANALYZING | Input validated | Seeds extracted | None |
| DERIVING | Seeds extracted | Requirements complete | None |
| VALIDATING | Requirements complete | Validation passed/failed | None |
| REVIEWING | Validation passed | Decision received | Required |
| COMPLETE | Approved | Terminal | None |
