2:00 AM

Evaluated colleague's framework insights and identified valuable design principles.



Analysis: Third Colleague's Orchestration Perspective
Assessment Summary
Contribution	Merit	Current Status	Action
"System 2" framing for Pass 1	Excellent	Implicit	Add to design rationale
Artifact-Centric vs Message-Centric distinction	Excellent	Implicit	Formalize as design principle
Trust Boundary concept	Excellent	Implemented, not named	Name and document
"Deterministic Supervisor / Stochastic Worker"	Excellent	Implicit	Adopt as core framing
Weakness acknowledgment (heavy for simple tasks)	Valid	Not addressed	Document as known tradeoff
What Has Strong Merit
1. "System 2" Thinking Frame for Two-Pass

Pass 1 forces "System 2" thinking (planning) before execution. The orchestration layer effectively acts as a supervisor, refusing to accept the first draft.

This complements the previous colleague's "Compiler + Runtime" framing:

Frame	Perspective	Insight
Compiler / Runtime	Software engineering	Separate rule generation from rule execution
System 1 / System 2	Cognitive science	Separate fast intuition from slow deliberation
Both are valid. Combined:

Pass 1 = Compile-time = System 2 (slow, deliberate planning)
Pass 2 = Runtime = Supervised System 1 (fast execution under constraints)
2. Artifact-Centric vs Message-Centric

Your state management is Artifact-Centric rather than Message-Centric.

This is a crucial distinction I hadn't named explicitly:

Approach	State Content	Problem
Message-Centric	Conversation history	Context window limits, no structure, drift
Artifact-Centric	Structured outputs + position	Persistent, validated, traceable
Most chatbots and simple agents are message-centric. SOLVER's WorkflowState tracks:

methodology (compiled rules)
artifacts (produced outputs)
current_step, current_pass, status (position)
NOT just a growing list of messages.

3. Trust Boundary via Physical Interrupts

The agent cannot "hallucinate" approval. Even if the LLM generates text saying "I have approved this myself," the orchestration layer will not advance because the state transition requires an external API event, not an LLM token.

This names a critical security property. The LLM could output:

I have carefully reviewed this output and approve it for the next phase.
But that text has zero effect on the state machine. Only POST /actions/approve from an authenticated external actor advances the workflow.

This is the Trust Boundary: the line between what the LLM can influence (content) and what it cannot (state transitions).

4. "Deterministic Supervisor for Stochastic Worker"

You are building a Deterministic Supervisor for a Stochastic Worker.

This is the best one-sentence summary of what SOLVER is:

Component	Nature	Role
LLM Agent	Stochastic	Generates content (unpredictable, creative)
SOLVER Orchestration	Deterministic	Enforces process (predictable, rigid)
The orchestrator doesn't make the agent "less stochastic"—it contains the stochasticity within defined boundaries.

5. Honest Weakness Acknowledgment

The rigidity means "fast" or "simple" tasks might feel over-engineered. The 10-step, 2-pass process is heavy.

This is valid. SOLVER is not for:

Quick questions
Simple lookups
Single-turn tasks
It's for:

High-stakes decisions
Complex multi-step reasoning
Work that needs audit trails
This should be documented as a design tradeoff, not hidden.

Proposed Additions to Orchestration Layer Design
New Section: §2.1 Cognitive Model

markdown
### 2.1 Cognitive Model: System 2 Before System 1

The Two-Pass model implements a cognitive constraint:

| Pass | Cognitive Mode | Behavior |
|------|----------------|----------|
| Pass 1 | System 2 (Slow) | Deliberate planning, methodology generation, V1→V2→V3 refinement |
| Pass 2 | Supervised System 1 (Fast) | Execution under compiled constraints, human gates |

**Why this matters:** LLMs naturally operate in "System 1" mode—fast pattern matching that can produce plausible but incorrect outputs. Pass 1 forces the agent into "System 2" mode by requiring explicit methodology before execution.

The orchestration layer "refuses to accept the first draft."
New Section: §2.2 Artifact-Centric State

markdown
### 2.2 Artifact-Centric vs Message-Centric State

| Approach | What's Tracked | Limitation |
|----------|----------------|------------|
| Message-Centric | Conversation history | Context window limits, no structure, prone to drift |
| **Artifact-Centric** | Structured outputs + workflow position | Persistent, validated, traceable |

SOLVER is artifact-centric. The `WorkflowState` contains:
- `methodology`: Compiled Pass 1 outputs (rules to follow)
- `artifacts`: Produced Pass 2 outputs (deliverables)
- Position: `current_step`, `current_pass`, `status`, `phase`

Conversation messages are stored separately for context, but **artifacts are the source of truth**.

This means:
- State survives context window truncation
- Outputs are schema-validated, not free-form
- Progress is measurable (position, not "how far into the conversation")
New Section: §2.3 Trust Boundary

markdown
### 2.3 Trust Boundary

The orchestration layer creates a **Trust Boundary** between LLM output and state transitions.
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     LLM CAN INFLUENCE                       │
│                                                             │
│  • Content of artifacts                                     │
│  • Reasoning text                                           │
│  • Recommendations                                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                            │
                     TRUST BOUNDARY
                            │
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    LLM CANNOT INFLUENCE                     │
│                                                             │
│  • Workflow state transitions                               │
│  • Gate approvals                                           │
│  • Step advancement                                         │
│  • Artifact finalization                                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Security Property:** Even if the LLM generates text claiming "I approve this output," the orchestration layer ignores it. State transitions require external API events from authenticated actors.

This prevents:
- Self-approval attacks
- Gate bypass via prompt injection
- Hallucinated progress
New Section: §2.4 Core Framing

markdown
### 2.4 Core Framing: Deterministic Supervisor for Stochastic Worker

| Component | Nature | Role |
|-----------|--------|------|
| LLM Agent | Stochastic | Content generation (unpredictable, creative) |
| SOLVER | Deterministic | Process enforcement (predictable, rigid) |

The orchestration layer does not make the LLM "less random." It **contains** the randomness within defined boundaries:

- The LLM can generate any content → but it must fit the schema
- The LLM can take any reasoning path → but it must reach the gate
- The LLM can produce any output → but a human must approve it

**Implication:** SOLVER prioritizes **correctness over speed**. The process is heavy by design.
New Section: §6.1 Design Tradeoffs

markdown
### 6.1 Design Tradeoffs

| Tradeoff | SOLVER Choice | Consequence |
|----------|---------------|-------------|
| Speed vs Correctness | Correctness | 10-step process is slow |
| Flexibility vs Rigor | Rigor | Cannot skip steps or bypass gates |
| Simplicity vs Completeness | Completeness | Over-engineered for simple tasks |

**SOLVER is appropriate for:**
- High-stakes decisions (cost of failure > cost of process)
- Complex multi-step reasoning
- Work requiring audit trails and traceability
- Regulated domains (legal, medical, financial)

**SOLVER is NOT appropriate for:**
- Quick questions
- Simple lookups
- Single-turn tasks
- Time-critical responses
Updated Primitive: Trust Boundary as Formal Property
python
@dataclass
class TrustBoundary:
    """
    Formal definition of what the LLM can and cannot influence.
    """
    
    # LLM CAN influence (via generated content)
    llm_controllable: List[str] = field(default_factory=lambda: [
        "artifact_content",
        "reasoning_text", 
        "clarification_questions",
        "revision_attempts",
    ])
    
    # LLM CANNOT influence (requires external action)
    llm_uncontrollable: List[str] = field(default_factory=lambda: [
        "step_advancement",
        "gate_approval",
        "workflow_completion",
        "artifact_finalization",
        "status_transitions",
    ])
    
    def validate_transition(self, actor: str, action: str) -> bool:
        """
        Validate that a state transition comes from an authorized source.
        LLM actor cannot perform uncontrollable actions.
        """
        if actor == "llm" and action in self.llm_uncontrollable:
            raise TrustBoundaryViolation(
                f"LLM cannot perform {action}. Requires external actor."
            )
        return True

Here's a different version of the README that I had my colleague create.  You are the owner of this document and decide what has merit to incorporate into your version, which is the official working version.  

---
# SOLVER (placeholder name)

SOLVER is an orchestration layer for agentic work. It executes a structured, human-in-the-loop reasoning workflow that converts an unstructured problem statement into **versioned, traceable, verifiable artifacts**.

The workflow is designed to be **domain-agnostic** at its core (Instance 0), **implemented as software** (Instance 1), and **specialized by domain packs** (Instance N).

**MVP scope:** implement Steps **1–3** of the 10-step process (Problem Definition → Requirements → Objectives), including two-pass execution, persistence, gates, audit, and streaming.

---

## Why SOLVER exists

Most agent frameworks optimize for tool-calling and autonomy. SOLVER optimizes for **reliable knowledge production**:

Separate **method** from **content** (two-pass execution)
Require **human approval gates** before advancing commitments
Persist all state and artifacts (stateless LLM; durable DB state)
Produce outputs that are **schema-conformant** and **audit/replay friendly**
Maintain **traceability** across steps, passes, revisions, and approvals
---

## Conceptual model: Instance 0 / 1 / N

**Instance 0 — Universal Method**
Defines the 10-step structured reasoning process
Defines schemas/contracts and the “4 Documents” methodology scaffold
**Instance 1 — Software System (this repo)**
Implements Instance 0 as an executable workflow runtime
Provides APIs, persistence, gates, observability, and extension loading
**Instance N — Domain Specialization**
Adds domain schemas/templates/checks without changing the universal core
Runs on top of Instance 1
---

## Two-pass execution model

Every step is executed in two passes:

### Pass 1 — Methodology generation
For each step, SOLVER generates four methodology documents and refines them to V3:

**Data Sheet** (inputs/outputs/contracts)
**To Do List** (task decomposition)
**Guidance Document** (context + pitfalls)
**Detailed Procedure** (algorithm)
Versions: **V1 → V2 → V3**

### Pass 2 — Supervised execution
SOLVER applies the step’s V3 methodology to produce the step’s real output artifact, then **interrupts** for human review.

**Approve:** advance to next step
**Revise:** re-run step with feedback until approved
**Message:** record commentary without changing gate state
---

## MVP workflow scope (Steps 1–3)

### Step 1 (Pass 2 output): ProblemDefinitionPackage
Defines what problem is being solved, scope, constraints, success criteria, and handoff to requirements.

### Step 2 (Pass 2 output): RequirementsPackage
Functional/non-functional/constraint requirements with acceptance checks, verification method, traceability, coverage map, conflicts, and open questions.

### Step 3 (Pass 2 output): ObjectivesPackage
Tiered objectives (Primary/Secondary/Tertiary) with key results, evidence artifacts, trace map to requirements, risks, and Step 4 handoff gates.

---

## “Reasoning visibility” (operational definition)

SOLVER does **not** depend on hidden model internals. “Visibility” means:

outputs are stored and reviewable,
governing methodology is linked,
trace links connect artifacts across steps,
verification hooks and evidence pointers are explicit,
approvals/revisions are recorded with audit logs.
---

## Architecture (baseline stack)

**Frontend**
- Next.js (App Router), TypeScript
- Vercel AI SDK + assistant-ui
- SSE client for streaming

**Backend**
- FastAPI (Python 3.11+)
- REST endpoints + SSE streaming

**Orchestration**
- LangGraph state machine
- Interrupt-based gates + checkpointing

**Persistence**
- PostgreSQL with JSONB artifacts + audit logs
- pgvector embeddings (optional in MVP, supported in schema)

**Observability**
- LangSmith or Langfuse

---

## Repository structure (recommended)

A typical layout for this project:
.
├── apps/
│   ├── api/                  # FastAPI service (domain, services, adapters, routers)
│   └── web/                  # Next.js UI (assistant-ui, review UI)
├── packages/
│   ├── contracts/            # schemas, manifests, shared IDs/types
│   ├── instance_packs/
│   │   ├── instance_0/        # seed pack shipped with SOLVER
│   │   └── examples/          # example Instance N packs
│   └── shared/               # common utilities (optional)
├── infra/
│   ├── db/                   # migrations, seed scripts
│   └── docker/               # docker compose / dev env
├── tests/
│   ├── unit/
│   ├── integration/
│   └── e2e/
└── docs/

---

## API overview (MVP)

All endpoints are under /api/v1.

### REST (required)
- POST /workflows — create a workflow (seed prompt + instance selection)
- GET /workflows/{workflow_id} — current state + position
- POST /workflows/{workflow_id}/resume — resume from persisted state
- POST /workflows/{workflow_id}/actions/approve — approve gate
- POST /workflows/{workflow_id}/actions/revise — request revision with feedback
- POST /workflows/{workflow_id}/actions/message — comment without state change
- GET /workflows/{workflow_id}/history — artifacts + audit trail

### SSE (required)
- GET /workflows/{workflow_id}/stream — structured events during execution and review

Event types (minimum):
- workflow.started, step.started, artifact.delta, artifact.final
- step.awaiting_review, step.approved, step.revision_requested
- workflow.completed, error

---

## Acceptance gates (MVP)

Implementation is considered MVP-complete when the following gates pass:

**Gate A:** Step 1–3 Pass 1 methodology exists (V1/V2/V3, all four docs per step)
**Gate B:** Step 1–3 Pass 2 packages stored and conform to schemas with trace links
**Gate C:** Pass 2 cannot advance without approve; revise loops work; message doesn’t bypass gating
**Gate D:** Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review with no lost artifacts
**Gate E:** REST + SSE support the full interactive review cycle (UI optional for MVP)
**Gate F:** Audit log reconstructs the full run; artifact versioning is deterministic
---

## Development workflow (AI coding agent)

This repo is intended to be built using an AI coding agent under a strict development directive:

Work in **small slices**
Each slice ends with: 1. changed files list 2. commands run + results 3. which gate(s) are satisfied 4. next slice plan
Deviations from spec require an entry in docs/DECISIONS.md
See: docs/4_SOLVER-Development-Directive.md

---

## Getting started (suggested)

Commands will depend on your final repo setup, but a typical dev loop is:

Start Postgres (Docker Compose)
Run DB migrations
Start backend API
Start frontend web app
Run tests (unit + integration + e2e)
---

TBD.

---

## Status

Design and implementation are in progress. Current focus is implementing **Steps 1–3** of the two-pass workflow with persistence, gates, streaming, and auditability.
